  - **The notices are checked on every build.** `scripts/ffmpeg/notices.py` lists every file the build compiled from the build's own dependency records (FFmpeg's, x264's and libvpx's `.d` files, the autotools libraries' `.deps`, dav1d's ninja log; NASM includes followed by hand, zlib's objects by name), with the headers they include, reads every comment in them, and `build.sh` stops when a notice, or another statement about a license, isn't in `ffmpeg-notices.txt` with that file listed, or when a statement has no decision in `notices.py`: the upstream license text it needs, which must be in `licenses/ffmpeg/`, or why it needs nothing more (public domain, GPL or LGPL code that the programs' license covers, code FFmpeg says it wrote itself, a note about a listed notice). `ffmpeg-notices.txt` carries that decision under each statement. So a new version or component can't bring one in unnoticed, or undecided. The macOS arm64 build's AArch64 sources were read the same way without building them, until it runs. `SOURCE.md` has the configure line and the pins; the FFmpeg section of `about.hbs` the notices entry. Aaron D. Gifford's own release of his SHA-2 code (`sha2-1.0.1.tgz` on aarongifford.com) couldn't be reached from the machine that did this, nor its Wayback Machine copy, so `sha2-gifford.txt` is the notice from Ruby's copy of his `sha2.c` (ldns's copy has it word for word too): to confirm against the original when it can be reached.
- **Targets:**
  - `windows-x86_64`: cross-compiled on Linux with mingw-w64's GCC in the win32 thread model, fully static. The exe imports only Windows' own DLLs. libvpx is kept on Win32 threads too (a `pthread.h` that doesn't compile makes its configure take its MSVC path), so no winpthreads.
  - `macos-arm64` and `macos-x86_64`: native builds with Xcode's clang on an Apple silicon and an Intel runner, for macOS 11.0 or later, joined with `lipo` and signed as today (D25). libvorbis's `configure` loses `-force_cpusubtype_ALL`, an option of Apple's old linker: the one change the script makes to a source.
  - `linux-x86_64`: only for the `engines-it` tests.
- **Where it runs:** CI is lane A's: a job per target that runs `scripts/ffmpeg/build.sh <target>` (`windows-x86_64` on ubuntu-24.04, the two macOS targets on their runners), cached on the hash of `scripts/ffmpeg/**` and `licenses/ffmpeg/**` (the source bundle carries both). Every draft release carries `grape-ffmpeg-sources.tar` (`release.yml`, #27), and every CI run that builds the programs has it in its `ffmpeg-source` artifact. At M7 the binaries go on Grape's GitHub Release too, and `scripts/sidecars.lock` points at them. Until then BtbN and Riedl stay in the lock for local testing only, and D25's gate keeps them out of every bundle.
- **The source bundle:** `dist/grape-ffmpeg-sources.tar` holds every tarball exactly as downloaded (with the mingw-w64 runtime's source, below), `scripts/ffmpeg/` and `licenses/ffmpeg/`. It's byte for byte the same from every target's run of one commit, and building from it downloads nothing (`GRAPE_FFMPEG_TARBALLS`).
- **Downloads:** each tarball goes into a new, empty folder over HTTPS only; its size and SHA-256 must match `sources.json`, and its file list must pass (one top folder, no absolute path, no `..`, no link that leaves it) before `tar` sees it. Two official URLs couldn't be fetched from the machine that pinned them (downloads.xiph.org for Opus, codeload.github.com for libvpx), so their hashes come from copies that other packagers' records of the official files confirm; `sources.json` says which. A review the same day confirmed both byte for byte from upstream: Opus from a mirror of downloads.xiph.org whose `SHA256SUMS.txt` lists the same hash, and libvpx by recreating GitHub's archive from the `v1.17.0` tag (`git archive` through `gzip -cn`).
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26 (a shared 4-core container):
  - `linux-x86_64` built in 524 s (FFmpeg itself 305 s): `ffmpeg` 27.98 MB, `ffprobe` 27.76 MB, linked only to glibc. All nine `engines-it` media tests pass against it: the 104 pairs, the hostile playlists, cancel, the names and `the_sidecar_has_everything_grape_uses_and_is_redistributable`. After the media engine's three review rounds were merged (2026-10-26), all 25 `engines-it` tests pass against it and against FFmpeg 6.1.1. `-protocols` lists `file` and `pipe` only, and `-buildconf` has `--disable-network`.
  - `windows-x86_64` built in 615 s (FFmpeg itself 386 s): `ffmpeg.exe` 28.01 MB, `ffprobe.exe` 27.81 MB, PE32+ x86-64. They import only `KERNEL32.dll`, `SHELL32.dll`, `bcrypt.dll` and `msvcrt.dll`. The link map shows no winpthreads, libssp or libatomic; from GCC only seven small `libgcc` helpers, and from mingw-w64 its startup code and parts of `libmingwex`. Under wine 9.0 both run: `-version` prints the configure line from the build, `-protocols` lists `file` and `pipe`, Grape's own argv turns an MP4 into WebM (VP9 and Opus), MP3 and a two-pass GIF, `ffprobe` reads the WebM, and an HLS playlist renamed `.mp4` is refused.
  - Reproducible: two `windows-x86_64` builds of one commit in different folders came out byte for byte the same (after keeping the folder out of libvpx's build string and the dates out of the PE header). A `linux-x86_64` build from the unpacked source bundle, with nothing downloaded, matched except its GNU build ID. The source bundle (34.9 MB) was the same from the Linux and the Windows run.
  - The notices check and the runtime sources, rebuilt the same day with the final script, offline from the tarball cache: the `linux-x86_64` and `windows-x86_64` builds read 3,220 and 3,227 files and found each of their 111 notices and 18 statements in `ffmpeg-notices.txt`, under every file. (Run again on the same trees after a review found libvorbis's `lpc.c` and `zlib.h` cut short: a notice stopped at the last paragraph with a license word in it and lost the names and date signed under it. It now runs to the end of its comment, or to where the library's own license starts; that added those lines, the authors under four MIPS files' notices and the three IJG files' descriptions, and nothing else. It was 109 notices before: three of those MIPS files had the same notice, which now differs by its author.) A missing entry, an entry missing its last line, an unlisted file, a new file with an ISC notice, an object with no source, a statement with no decision, a missing upstream text and an entry whose decision line differs each stop it. The Windows exes are byte for byte the ones built before the change, and the source bundle, now 45.3 MB with `mingw-w64-v11.0.1.tar.bz2` in it, is the same from both runs.
  - The script's failures: a wrong size or SHA-256 (the file is deleted, nothing unpacked), a tarball with `..`, a link out or a member outside its folder, bad JSON or an `http://` URL, a work folder it didn't make, a target for another OS.
  - **Flaky under load, not this build:** `every_audio_and_video_pair_converts` failed in 2 of about 30 runs while the machine was loaded (load 11 on 4 cores): WMV to GIF lost its last two frames (0.83 s against 1.09 s). Run alone, the GIF pass loses them about 1 time in 13 with this build and with BtbN's 9.0.2 alike (3 of 40 each, under wine), and never with FFmpeg 6.1. It's FFmpeg 9.0 with D22's two-pass GIF arguments, as lane B found later: the ASF demuxer gives the packets FFmpeg reads while probing no duration unless a parser times them (none does for WMV2), so in a short WMV every frame has none, and when the source ends before the palette, the GIF pass's second input, has reached the filter graph, 9.0 ends the stream at the last frame's start instead of at the end the decoder worked out. It doesn't need the video to start after the audio: a WMV without audio loses them too. `lane-b/media-fixes` has the GIF pass read a WMV without B-frames from its start again (`-ss 0`), so every packet has its duration: none of 400 conversions under load lost a frame. A WMV with B-frames is read once, since that seek lands past its first frames; H.264 and MPEG-4 ones are timed by their parser anyway, and a short WMV3 or VC-1 one with B-frames can still now and then lose its last GIF frames (a known limit in D22).
  - Not tested: the macOS targets (no Mac here; their first run is CI's), and the Windows exe on Windows itself.
- **Toolchain runtimes in the Windows exe** (settled 2026-10-26): the programs are GPL-2.0-or-later, and the exe links two runtimes of the toolchain statically.
  - mingw-w64's C runtime, v11.0.1 (startup code, parts of `libmingwex`): its exact source, the upstream tarball, is pinned in `sources.json` (`runtime_sources`, with URL, size and SHA-256) and goes into the source bundle; `build.sh` checks it like the others for every target but doesn't build it, since the toolchain brings the runtime. Ubuntu 24.04's `mingw-w64-x86-64-dev` 11.0.1-3build1 is built from that same tarball (its `.dsc` lists the same SHA-256), and its patches touch only build files and tools. Its notices are `mingw-w64-runtime.txt`.
  - GCC 13.2.0's `libgcc` helpers (stack probing, 64-bit division, popcount): GPL-3.0-or-later with the GCC Runtime Library Exception 3.1, which lets what GCC compiles be distributed under any terms, so the programs' license doesn't change. `gcc-runtime-exception.txt` is GCC's `COPYING.RUNTIME`, and `LICENSE-GPLv3.txt` (FFmpeg's `COPYING.GPLv3`) the GPLv3 it refers to, next to `LICENSE.txt` (GPLv2).
- **Amended 2026-10-26 (D26):** the tools add the filters `ebur128`, `volume` and `alimiter` (Normalize Volume's measurement, gain and limiter), the `null` muxer (its measuring passes, which open no file) and the encoders `alac`, `pcm_s32le` and `pcm_f32le` (Normalize and Audio Channels keep ALAC, 32-bit and float WAV): nineteen encoders and H.263, sixteen muxers. `alac` uses the `lpc` code FLAC already built. `loudnorm` stays out: it would bring libebur128's MIT notice (`ebur128.c`). Both targets were rebuilt the same day from `scripts/ffmpeg` and `licenses/ffmpeg` as committed here, offline from the tarball cache; their `BUILDINFO.txt` names commit `b57b903`, this change before this record was added to it.
  - `linux-x86_64` in 316 s (FFmpeg itself 162 s): `ffmpeg` 28,028,432 bytes, SHA-256 `16481303571e92614a94ade214e99f68f007f2ebce160052898ce7a0f576baae`; `ffprobe` 27,815,376 bytes, `fd431854f03111de6413c42c201d996e8fa522852ac6386247582cb21d9f31d3`. All 25 `engines-it` media tests pass against it and against FFmpeg 6.1.1. `the_sidecar_has_everything_grape_uses_and_is_redistributable` now names the seven, and fails against the earlier build ("encoder alac missing").
  - `windows-x86_64` in 505 s (FFmpeg itself 337 s, with cargo building on the same four cores): `ffmpeg.exe` 28,058,624 bytes, `040a681d5dfbeebc678a9bb5c67954c7d6faca25346eaf47796d75e35ce1a41e`; `ffprobe.exe` 27,861,504 bytes, `7555aea2550e58c8503fd5989c24b7f88f202c3154356825157ddab02e51694f`. They import only `KERNEL32.dll`, `SHELL32.dll`, `bcrypt.dll` and `msvcrt.dll`. Run under wine 9.0 (`/usr/lib/wine/wine64`, which isn't on `PATH`; this record first said the machine had none): both report `9.0.2-grape` with the configure line in their `BUILDINFO.txt`; their `-encoders`, `-filters`, `-muxers`, `-demuxers`, `-decoders` and `-protocols` lists are line for line the Linux build's; `ebur128=peak=true` measures a 997 Hz sine at −12 dB as −33.1 LUFS with a −30.1 dBTP true peak, and Normalize's limited chain (+20 dB, `alimiter` oversampled 4×) gives −4.5 LUFS and −1.5 dBTP, both exactly as on Linux; ALAC round-trips 16-bit (`s16p`) and 24-bit (`s32p`) PCM sample for sample, in files byte for byte the Linux build's. 32-bit samples come back as 24 bits from both (the `alac` encoder writes at most 24, as `flac` does without `-strict experimental`), so D26's Normalize Volume and Audio Channels can't keep a 32-bit ALAC or FLAC source as it is.
  - The notices check read 3,231 and 3,238 files, eleven more each (`alacenc.c`, `af_alimiter.c`, `af_volume.c`/`.h`, `f_ebur128.c`/`.h`, their x86 assembly and init files, `nullenc.c`), and found the same 111 notices and 18 statements: `ffmpeg-notices.txt` doesn't change.
  - The source bundle came out byte for byte the same from both runs: 45,352,960 bytes, SHA-256 `1497f9a548699a0ab8b0fe60ee2d45610da3b3c552a350ee004c48d85978a95f`.
  - The macOS targets are CI's (lane A), as before.
- **Revisit if:** a pinned library gets a security fix; Grape needs another component (add it to the lists in `build.sh`, which checks them exactly); hardware encoders come (Media Foundation and VideoToolbox are OS frameworks, so no new library; NVENC, AMF and oneVPL each add MIT headers or a dispatcher); the CI image's mingw-w64 or GCC changes (check `mingw-w64-runtime.txt` and `gcc-runtime-exception.txt` against it, and pin its mingw-w64 source in `runtime_sources`); or GitHub retires a macOS runner type.

## D25: Fetching and bundling sidecars (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Superseded in part (2026-10-26):** the prebuilt BtbN and martin-riedl FFmpeg builds pinned below are for dev testing only and are never bundled: both link GPL-2.0-only code (libzvbi) into a GPL-3.0 build, and about 100 libraries Grape doesn't use. Grape builds its own minimal FFmpeg instead (D24, lane B); CI builds it per target, and the lock will point at its assets on Grape's own GitHub Release. The fetch scripts, the lock format, the checks and the bundling gate stay as decided here.
- **Decision:** the sidecar builds are pinned in one file, `scripts/sidecars.lock`, and two scripts fetch them from it: `scripts/fetch-sidecars.ps1` on Windows and `scripts/fetch-sidecars.sh` on macOS. Only the lock is committed. The binaries land in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/binaries/`, which git ignores. The pin itself (FFmpeg 9.0.2: BtbN's build for Windows, martin-riedl.de's for macOS) is D22, lane B.
- **The lock** is JSON (`format` 1, with `_comment` keys for notes):
  - `archives`: each download by id, with its https URL, its SHA-256 and its size in bytes.
  - `sidecars`: each program's version and, per target triple, the archive it comes from and its path inside it, plus a `license` path where the archive has one (BtbN's `LICENSE.txt`). One archive can supply several files: BtbN's zip has both programs.
  - heif-enc goes in the same way once lane B pins it, with a Windows target only. The scripts' version check is FFmpeg's (`-hide_banner -version`, first line `<name> version …`), which heif-enc doesn't answer, so `fetch-sidecars.ps1` needs a check for it first. (Superseded: D33 builds `grape-heif` in CI; it isn't in `sidecars.lock`.)
- **The checks**, in this order:
  - Downloads use HTTPS only (curl `--proto =https --proto-redir =https`; Invoke-WebRequest with TLS 1.2 or later), into a new, empty temp folder that's always deleted at the end.
  - The archive's size and SHA-256 must match the lock before anything is extracted. A mismatch deletes it and changes nothing.
  - Only the paths the lock names are extracted. Both scripts check every field of the lock by the same rules first: a path may not have wildcards, backslashes, `.` or `..` parts, or start with `/`. The macOS script also rejects a key that appears twice.
  - macOS: the two per-arch files must be arm64 and x86_64. `lipo -create` merges them into `<name>-universal-apple-darwin`, which is signed ad hoc (lipo breaks the build's own signature) and must pass `codesign --verify --strict`. The per-arch files stay too, because tauri-build looks for them while a universal build compiles each arch (tauri-cli 2.12.1 `desktop.rs`, tauri-build 2.7.1).
  - Each program runs with `-hide_banner -version`, on its own OS only. The first line must start with `<name> version` and name the lock's version as a whole token (BtbN prints `n9.0.2-17-g2a571b6068-20260930`, Riedl `9.0.2-https://www.martin-riedl.de`). The configuration line must not have `--enable-nonfree`.
  - Only then do the files replace the old ones in `binaries/`, and the stamp, `binaries/sidecars.stamp`, is written last: the lock's SHA-256, the target, and each file's SHA-256. A run that finds all of them matching says the sidecars are already current and changes nothing. `-Force` / `--force` fetches again.
- The scripts never change `PATH` and install nothing. This is the only download, at build time. Grape itself never downloads anything (golden rule 3).
- `binaries/ffmpeg-LICENSE.txt` is the BtbN archive's GPLv3 text: a dev reference only, never bundled (what ships is Grape's own build, with `licenses/ffmpeg/`, D24). The scripts never write into `licenses/`, which is committed and written by hand (`licenses/README.md`). The Riedl zips carry no license file.
- **Bundling** uses a config overlay, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.sidecars.conf.json`, which sets `bundle.externalBin` to `["binaries/ffmpeg", "binaries/ffprobe"]` and adds `licenses/ffmpeg/` to `bundle.resources`. Its `build.beforeBuildCommand` runs the gate below before the UI build, so a `tauri build` that passes it fails unless every check passes: the GPL gate is a check, not just a rule. `tauri dev` doesn't run it, so the lock's builds still work for dev testing. *Replaced 2026-10-26 (#27) by one overlay per OS, `tauri.sidecars.windows.conf.json` and `tauri.sidecars.macos.conf.json`: see the update under the gate below. The rest of this bullet holds for them.*
  - A build passes it as `--config src-tauri/tauri.sidecars.conf.json` (from `apps/desktop`). The CLI merges it into `tauri.conf.json` as a JSON merge patch (RFC 7396: objects merge, arrays and values replace) and hands it to tauri-build as `TAURI_CONFIG` (tauri-cli 2.12.1 `helpers/config.rs`).
  - Tauri never picks it up by itself (it reads only `tauri.<platform>.conf.json` on its own), so `cargo` builds and today's CI builds don't need the binaries.
  - Installed, the files lose their suffix: `ffmpeg.exe` and `ffprobe.exe` next to `Grape.exe` on Windows, `ffmpeg` and `ffprobe` in `Grape.app/Contents/MacOS` on macOS.
- **The gate** is `scripts/check-sidecars.mjs` (2026-10-26, agreed with lane B; tests in `scripts/check-sidecars.test.mjs`). The overlay's `beforeBuildCommand` runs it as `node ../../scripts/check-sidecars.mjs && pnpm -C ui build` for the build's `TAURI_ENV_TARGET_TRIPLE`, so it gates a local build as well as CI's. It refuses the build, naming each failure, unless every `externalBin` in the overlay:
  - has a rule in the script (a new sidecar needs one);
  - is in `binaries/` for that target (`<name>-<triple>`, `.exe` on Windows, `universal-apple-darwin` for the DMG) and runs. The first line of `-hide_banner -version` must be `<name> version <v>` with `<v>` ending in `-grape` (or `-grape.<n>`), the marker of Grape's own build (`--extra-version=grape`, D24). So the lock's BtbN and Riedl builds, for dev testing only, can never ship;
  - has its texts in `licenses/<folder>/`, with `SOURCE.md` for GPL code (ffmpeg and ffprobe share `licenses/ffmpeg/`), and a `## FFmpeg` section in `about.hbs` and in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (golden rule 6, ENGINES rule 3).
  - It has no condition on the lock's URLs: CI's build jobs take FFmpeg from the D24 build jobs in the same run, never from the lock, and Grape's own release of the build waits for M7 (lane B writes the lock's values then).
  - No build passes the overlay yet, so no installer or DMG carries FFmpeg. CI's build jobs and `release.yml` get it with lane B's media engine and the D24 build jobs. (Superseded by the update below.)
  - **Update 2026-10-26 (#27):** CI builds Grape's own FFmpeg (D24) and every installer and DMG now carries `ffmpeg` and `ffprobe` with `licenses/ffmpeg/`, and Grape's bsdtar with `licenses/libarchive/` (D27). One overlay per OS replaced `tauri.sidecars.conf.json` and the HEIC slice's `tauri.heif.windows.conf.json`, because a second overlay that sets `externalBin` would replace the first's list: `tauri.sidecars.windows.conf.json` (ffmpeg, ffprobe, bsdtar, grape-heif) and `tauri.sidecars.macos.conf.json` (ffmpeg, ffprobe, bsdtar). CI's installer and DMG jobs and `release.yml` pass them with the binaries from the same run's build jobs (the DMG job with the per-arch files as well as the universal one, as above), and the gate checks the merged list in `TAURI_CONFIG`. The draft release carries `grape-ffmpeg-sources.tar` and grape-heif's source bundle, both in `SHA256SUMS.txt`. Without `TAURI_CONFIG` (the Sidecars job, a run by hand) the gate reads the target OS's sidecars overlay (`defaultOverlay(triple)`; `--overlay` still picks another), so on Windows the Sidecars job's run also reports bsdtar and grape-heif missing. CI's `engines-it` jobs run the media tests against the same run's builds. So a missing `ffmpeg` or `ffprobe` is now a broken install, with its own toast (D35's amendment).
  - CI: the UI job runs the script's tests, and the Sidecars job runs it against the lock's builds, which it must refuse for the missing marker.
  - *2026-10-28 (D41):* for grape-heif the gate also reads the banner's configuration line, whose `protocol=N` must be `PROTOCOL` in `crates/grape-heif-proto/src/lib.rs`: a helper built for another protocol (D33's `1.23.7-grape.1` speaks 1) would refuse every HEIC and AVIF job, so it's refused like a foreign build, with how to get the right one.
- **CI:** a `Sidecars` job on `windows-latest` and `macos-latest`.
  - It restores `binaries/` from a cache keyed on the OS and the hashes of the lock and both fetch scripts (a restored folder skips the download and the checks, so a changed script must miss the cache), `actions/cache@v6` (v6.1.0 is from 2026-06-23, D9). Only a run that fetched and checked everything saves one, and the script checks a restored folder against its stamp.
  - It runs the fetch script, then runs it again, which must find the sidecars current: Windows PowerShell 5.1 and then PowerShell 7 on Windows, macOS's `/bin/bash` 3.2 on the Mac.
  - It lists the files with their sizes (and `lipo -info` on the Mac), builds nothing, keeps no git credentials, and has the workflow's read-only token.
- **Testing:** `GRAPE_SIDECARS_NO_RUN=1` skips the `-version` runs (and lipo and codesign on the Mac) and writes no stamp, so the download can be tested on another OS. The next normal run checks everything again. It's for testing only.
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26:
  - The `.sh`: the lock checks, and the download, hash check and extraction of the four Riedl zips (Mach-O arm64 and x86_64). With stand-ins for `uname`, `lipo`, `codesign` and `arch`, its whole real path: the stamp, a second run, `--force`, a changed or deleted file, Rosetta or not.
  - Its failures, each leaving `binaries/` unchanged and no temp folder: version 9.0.21 or 19.0.2, `--enable-nonfree`, no configuration line, another program's banner, a universal file missing an arch, a wrong SHA-256, a wrong size, a flipped byte in the download, and Ctrl+C.
  - The `.ps1` in PowerShell 7.6.6: the BtbN download, hash check and extraction (PE32+ x86-64, and the GPLv3 text), the stamp, `-Force`, a changed file, a wrong SHA-256, and the `-version` checks against stand-in programs.
  - Neither script has run on a Mac or the PC yet. Windows PowerShell 5.1 and macOS's bash 3.2 first run in CI.
- **Revisit if:** heif-enc is pinned (a Windows-only sidecar needs its own overlay or a `tauri.windows.conf.json`, since tauri-build wants every `externalBin` for the target it builds; done for HEIC: D33), Grape ships for Windows ARM64 (`aarch64-pc-windows-msvc`), the Windows sidecars get signed in M7 (the bundler signs them in place in `binaries/`, which changes their hashes, so the next run fetches again), or Riedl's Intel builds end (January 2027).

## D26: Lane B's v1 tools: Compress, Remove Metadata, Normalize Volume, Audio Channels, Remove Audio (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `run_job` runs five v1 tools on 31 more tool/format pairs (38 with every sidecar):
  - Compress on JPG and PNG (image engine, no sidecar) and on MP3, M4A, WAV (→ FLAC), FLAC, MP4, MOV and MKV (FFmpeg);
  - Remove Metadata on JPG, PNG, WebP and GIF (Grape's own container strip, no sidecar) and on MP3, M4A, WAV, FLAC, MP4, MOV and MKV (FFmpeg stream copy);
  - Normalize Volume and Audio Channels on MP3, M4A, WAV and FLAC;
  - Remove Audio on MP4, MOV and MKV.
  WebP, TIFF and HEIC Compress, and TIFF and HEIC Remove Metadata, stay "coming". *Amended by D28 (2026-10-28): those five pairs are D28's.* The numbers are in ENGINES.md → Tools. Lane B builds the slice in small commits; until a pair's commit lands, `run_job` refuses it and the app says "… is coming" (D35).
- **Rules:**
  - Only Remove Metadata removes metadata. The other tools keep tags, `creation_time` (FFmpeg drops it on every remux, `ffmpeg_mux_init.c:3034`), iPhone QuickTime keys, cover art, chapters, ICC and EXIF/XMP/IPTC.
  - Tools keep every track except what they remove. They map streams by index, unlike conversions ("only what the user sees", D22).
  - Compress output must be ≤90 % of the source (lossy) or ≤95 % (exact).
  - A job that would change nothing (already compact, mono or stereo, at the target loudness, no sound to remove, too quiet to measure) writes nothing and fails with the new `ErrorKind::NoChange`.
  - Remove Metadata and Remove Audio never re-encode. Normalize and Channels keep codec, depth and rate; a 32-bit ALAC or FLAC source, which FFmpeg's encoders would write as 24 bits, is refused.
- **Remove Metadata:**
  - **Images:** a container-level strip (JPEG segments, PNG chunks, WebP chunks, GIF blocks). Image data stays byte for byte. ICC is kept. An EXIF holding only Orientation (and the DCF Adobe RGB tags when that's how the source signaled its color space) replaces the dropped EXIF when needed, so nothing rotates or shifts color. Data after a JPEG's end (gain maps, motion-photo video, MPF images) is dropped and reported.
  - **Media:** `-c copy -map_metadata -1 -map_chapters -1 -fflags +bitexact`, with track languages and MKV fonts restored. Cover art, data tracks, image attachments, chapters and MP4/MOV timecode are dropped and reported. MP3 gets no ID3 at all. A second ffprobe of the output must find only structural keys, or nothing is published.
  - The report (`Note::MetadataRemoved`) gains Location, Camera, Comment, Copyright, Software, Iptc, Thumbnail, CoverArt, Chapters and Embedded.
  - This replaces ENGINES.md's "Images: decode and re-encode" (amends D16).
- **Normalize Volume:**
  - Measure with FFmpeg's `ebur128` (true peak; mono measured as dual mono).
  - When the peaks allow it, a linear `volume` gain.
  - Otherwise the gain plus a 4× (≤48 kHz) or 2× (≤96 kHz) oversampled `alimiter` at the ceiling −0.5 dB, re-measured and corrected at most twice.
  - Ceiling −1 dBTP for WAV, FLAC and ALAC; −2 dBTP for MP3 and AAC.
  - Target −24…−10 LUFS (default −16). Within 0.5 LU with the peaks under the ceiling is `NoChange`.
  - ReplayGain, R128 and iTunNORM tags are removed.
  - Not `loudnorm`: its dynamic mode rides the gain toward the running loudness (`af_loudnorm.c:477-488, 561-565`), resamples to 192 kHz, and would add libebur128's MIT notice. TOOLS_AND_ACTIONS.md's engine column changes with this.
- **Audio Channels:**
  - stereo → mono is (L+R)/2;
  - mono → stereo duplicates at full level;
  - 3 or more labeled channels use swresample's matrix normalized with `rematrix_maxval=1` (5.1 → stereo: FL 0.414, FC 0.293, SL 0.293, no LFE), the same for every codec;
  - 3 or more unlabeled channels average to mono and are refused for stereo.
- **Compress:** Small, Balanced and High per ENGINES.md → Tools.
  - x264 CRF 28/23/20 at `medium`, short side ≤720/1080/2160, Small ≤30 fps; AAC 48/64/96 kb/s per channel; LAME V7/V5/V2.
  - FLAC: 16-bit ≤48 kHz / ≤24-bit ≤48 kHz / lossless.
  - AAC at 48 kHz at most (FFmpeg's encoder takes at most 96 kHz); FLAC at most 8 channels, its limit.
  - JPEG: q70 ≤2048 px / q80 ≤4096 px / q85 with image's encoder, EXIF kept with the stored orientation.
  - PNG: lossless at every preset.
  - HLG video becomes 8-bit H.264 with its HLG tags. PQ video, CMYK JPEG, APNG, WAV/FLAC deeper than 24-bit or float, and WAV of more than 8 channels are refused.
- **API:**
  - grape-core: `ToolKey::has_v1_panel()` (D31's v1 rule) and `ToolParams::LUFS_MIN/MAX/STEP` with `lufs_ok` (D12 extension).
  - grape-engines: `ErrorKind::NoChange` (`ErrorKind` becomes `#[non_exhaustive]`), `Note::Compressed { before, after }`, and the ten `MetaField`s.
  - `png` 0.18.1 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, already in the lock through image) becomes a direct dependency, for PNG Compress.
- **FFmpeg (amends D24):** + filters `ebur128`, `volume`, `alimiter`; + muxer `null`; + encoders `alac`, `pcm_s32le`, `pcm_f32le`. No new license notice (`notices.py scan`: 0). Linux and Windows were rebuilt with them the same day (D24's amendment has the record); the macOS universal build is CI's (lane A).
- **Not chosen:**
  - jpeg-encoder: IJG license, and its optimized tables are misdecoded by zune-jpeg 0.5.15.
  - Palette PNG (`color_quant`), libwebp, and the TIFF IFD rewriter: later slices.
  - The ITU-level downmix with a measured gain, and early stop for video Compress.
- **Tested:** 2026-10-28, on Linux: `cargo test` for grape-core and grape-engines (exact argv, plans, strips and reductions as unit tests; every tool through `run_job`, the FFmpeg ones with a scripted FFmpeg, the image ones end to end); `engines-it` against Grape's 9.0.2 build and FFmpeg 6.1.1 (43 media tests, the five FFmpeg tools on sources made with lavfi, outputs read back with `ffprobe`); clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux, Windows and Apple silicon. CI's `engines-it` jobs run the same tests against Grape's own Windows and macOS builds. After the image tools' review, Compress was measured in a release build: a 12000×12000 JPEG at Balanced peaks about 820 MiB over the process's own, an 8000×8000 PNG at Small about 370 MiB, and a cancel returns within 0.44 s and 0.14 s. Not on the PC or a Mac yet: `TESTING.md` → "Media tools, M4" and "Image tools, M4". The design was checked on Linux, 2026-10-26, against the 9.0.2 source and Grape's 9.0.2 build, and FFmpeg 6.1.1 for the filters 9.0.2 lacked: the option names, `creation_time` dropped on every remux, the Dolby Vision boxes needing `-strict unofficial`, the downmix coefficients, cover art kept by a re-encode in MP3, M4A and FLAC, `-id3v2_version 0` writing no ID3, HLG through x264, Apple keys and `creation_time` kept by Remove Audio's argv, the Normalize plan on four sources (each within 0.1 LU of −16 LUFS, true peak at most −1.5 dBTP, with 15.6 dB of limiting on the hardest), and `notices.py scan` over the new components' files.
- **Needs the PC** (`TESTING.md` → "Media tools, M4" and "Image tools, M4"):
  - iPhone SDR and HDR (HLG/Dolby Vision) MOVs through Mute and Compress, imported into Photos (date, location, playback);
  - a GoPro MP4;
  - Android and Pixel Ultra HDR and motion-photo JPEGs through Remove Metadata (Explorer's Properties empty, still upright);
  - a podcast MP3 normalized and checked in a meter;
  - a 24/96 FLAC;
  - a 32-bit float WAV;
  - a 5.1 FLAC to stereo;
  - a macOS screenshot PNG.
- **Revisit if:**
  - HLG H.264 looks wrong on the PC or in Photos (then refuse it or add tone mapping);
  - limiting depth on real speech sounds bad (cap it);
  - zune-jpeg 0.5.16 is stable and 14 days old (jpeg-encoder with optimized tables);
  - users ask for album gain, palette PNG or WebP Compress.

## D27: Archives (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `grape-engines` converts among ZIP, TAR and GZIP (the six v1 pairs: zip → tar or gz, tar → zip or gz, gz → zip or tar) and runs Extract Archive on all three, in pure Rust, in `src/archive/`. Lane A builds that module by agreement with lane B, who owns the crate. It is wired into `run_job` (`Engine::Archive` and its arms, D35). RAR is read through Grape's own bsdtar sidecar (below).
- **Crates** (grape-engines only):

| Crate | Version | License | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| zip | =9.0.2, no default features, `deflate-flate2`, `deflate64` | MIT | Reads and writes ZIP. Deflate64 because Explorer reportedly uses it for big zips. The defaults would add bzip2 and PPMd (their licenses fail `cargo deny`), zstd-sys (C, and too new for D9) and a crypto stack |
| tar | 0.4.46, no default features (no xattr) | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | Reads and writes tar, GNU long names included |
| flate2 | 1.1.10, no default features, `rust_backend` | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | gzip, and ZIP's Deflate, on miniz_oxide |
| crc32fast | 1.5.2 (already locked through flate2) | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | RAR header CRCs in the preflight, and each RAR entry's CRC32 checked against bsdtar's output |
| unicode-normalization | 0.1.25 (already locked through lopdf → stringprep) | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | RAR entry names normalized to NFC (bsdtar on macOS returns NFD) |

  - **New `Cargo.lock` entries:** zip 9.0.2 (published 2026-10-11, MIT), tar 0.4.46 (2026-05-18), filetime 0.2.29 (2026-05-12, MIT/Apache-2.0), typed-path 0.12.3 (2026-02-11, MIT OR Apache-2.0) and deflate64 0.1.12 (2026-03-23, MIT). Everything else they need was already locked: flate2 1.1.10, miniz_oxide 0.9.1, crc32fast 1.5.2, indexmap, memchr, and nix 0.31.3 on macOS.
  - **The lock is pinned** with `cargo update -p zip --precise 9.0.2` and `cargo update -p typed-path --precise 0.12.3`. Plain resolution picks zip 9.1.x and typed-path 0.12.4 or 0.13.0, which fail D9's 14 days. zip 9.0.2 passes by about 5 hours. `zip = "=9.0.2"` keeps it there.
  - **miniz_oxide, not zlib-rs:** miniz_oxide is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` and is already compiled in through `png`. Turning on zlib-rs anywhere would switch the whole graph to it, png and tiff included. `rust_backend` names it explicitly, so archives don't depend on png's features. zlib-rs would be faster (not measured) but has a lot of `unsafe`.
  - `cargo deny` passes with no new license, and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` is regenerated (267 crates).
- **One name validator** (`archive/names.rs`) checks every entry, for extracting and for converting. Windows' rules apply on every OS, so an archive is judged the same everywhere:
  - Names are split on both `/` and `\`. `.` components are dropped.
  - Any of these refuses the whole job, naming the entry in the toast (logs never carry names): an absolute path, a drive letter, UNC or `\\?\`, a `..` component, an empty component in the middle, a control character, any of `< > : " | ? *` (`:` covers ADS), a trailing dot or space, and the device names CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, CONIN$, CONOUT$, COM0–9, LPT0–9 and COM/LPT¹²³, with any extension. The spec listed `:` and the device names; `< > " | ? *` are added because Windows can't create them either.
  - So does a name over 4,096 bytes (Linux's `PATH_MAX`; it also bounds what one name costs to check) or a component over 255 UTF-16 units (what NTFS holds; counted in UTF-16 so long CJK names Windows takes aren't refused). If a volume still refuses a name while extracting (ext4 and APFS hold 255 bytes), the toast names the entry the same way instead of blaming the destination.
  - Two paths that are equal ignoring case (Grape's case fold, as in planning), or a file where a folder must be, refuse the job: "{name} has two items named …". A folder named twice is fine, and is written once.
  - The zip crate keeps one entry per exact name: a later record replaces the earlier one's data in its place. So Grape reads the central directory a second time and refuses a ZIP that names a file twice byte for byte.
  - **Not the crates' helpers:** zip 9.0.2's `enclosed_name` returns `../b` for `\\?\C:\../b` on every OS (fixed in 9.0.4, too new for D9). It also strips absolute prefixes instead of refusing them, and accepts device names and ADS. tar's `unpack` creates links with unchecked targets and writes devices as plain files. Grape never calls `enclosed_name`, `mangled_name`, `extract`, `unpack` or `unpack_in`.
- **Bounds** (`Limits`, injected smaller in tests):
  - At most 100,000 entries, at most 64 GiB written, and past 1 GiB written, at most 1000× the archive's size.
  - They're held on the bytes actually decompressed, as they stream. A ZIP is also refused up front by its declared sizes: zip fails an entry that inflates past its declared size, and Grape fails one that comes up short.
  - The tar crate reads GNU long names and PAX records whole, so those are held to 16 MiB per entry.
  - The name check keeps every path it has seen, keyed by its parent and its own component, so a deep name costs memory in step with its length. Those paths, the folders that entries only imply included, are held to twice the entry bound ("more than 100,000 items").
  - The zip crate reserves memory for, and parses, as many central directory records as a ZIP's end record claims; a ZIP64 one needs only 46 bytes of file per claimed record. So before it runs, Grape reads every end record in the last 64 KiB (where the format puts them) and refuses a claim over the bound. A ZIP with no end record there is refused as damaged. The zip crate tries end records further back only when every one near the end has failed; that path isn't pre-checked.
  - Over a bound, nothing is kept: "{name} is larger unpacked than Grape will write (more than 64 GiB)." The bounds cover conversions too, because zip → tar writes uncompressed.
- **Links and special files:**
  - Extract never creates or follows a link. Symlinks and hard links are left out and counted.
  - A conversion carries a symlink as a link entry when its target, resolved from the link's folder, stays inside the archive, with `..` only at its start. After a folder name, a `..` could climb out of a folder that is itself a link (`up` → `../..`, then `up/../../x`): the OS follows `up` first. An escaping one is left out and counted.
  - Hard links, devices, FIFOs, sockets and tar types Grape doesn't know are left out and counted. So are `__MACOSX/` entries and `._` AppleDouble files.
  - The engine returns the counts (`LeftOut`, as `JobOutcome::notes`), and the toast says "2 links left out", "1 special file left out", "1 repeated file left out" or "3 items left out"; macOS metadata only in the log (D35).
- **Refused archives:** an encrypted ZIP entry gives "{name} is password-protected." (no password prompt). bzip2, LZMA, Zstandard, XZ, PPMd or AES gives "{name} uses bzip2 compression, which Grape can't read yet." (`NotYetSupported`). Damaged data or a bad checksum gives "{name} isn't a readable ZIP file."
- **Sniffing** (FORMAT_MATRIX rule 3): the container comes from the first bytes, not the extension: `PK\3\4` (or `PK\5\6` for an empty ZIP), `1F 8B`, or a tar header with a valid checksum (POSIX, GNU or v7). No match gives "{name} doesn't look like a ZIP file." A file of only zero blocks, 1,024 to 10,240 bytes (the tar crate's empty tar and GNU tar's), is an empty tar, and so is a gzip whose whole data is one: converting it gives an empty archive, extracting it an empty folder. More zeros than that, gzipped, are one plain file (a disk image).
- **Plain gz:** a gzip whose data doesn't start with a tar header is one file.
  - It's named by the header's FNAME if that is one safe name, else by the source's stem (`notes.txt.gz` → `notes.txt`). It's dated by MTIME, else by the source's own mtime. The `._` rule for macOS metadata doesn't apply to it: `._notes.txt.gz` still gives its file.
  - Members are read one by one, and zero padding after the last one is allowed (flate2's `MultiGzDecoder` refuses it).
  - Converting one to ZIP or TAR reads it twice, to measure it and then to write it, because a tar entry needs its size first.
- **What carries over:**
  - Relative paths, folders (empty ones too), mtimes, and Unix modes masked to 0o755 (no setuid, setgid or sticky bit, no group or other write).
  - tar → tar.gz and tar.gz → tar keep the tar's bytes exactly. Their entries are still checked, and nothing is left out. The other pairs re-pack the entries.
  - Because other tools read those kept bytes, an entry's PAX records may hold at most one `path`, equal to the name Grape read, and a global header none; a PAX record that doesn't parse refuses the tar as damaged. The tar crate takes a GNU long name over a PAX `path`, and the first of two `path` records, where GNU tar and Python's `tarfile` take the PAX one or the last (`../../evil.txt` instead of `ok.txt`).
  - Entry data isn't read for those two pairs (the tar reader skips it), so a sparse entry counts against the bounds by the bytes it stores, not by its holes. Re-packed or extracted, it counts at its full size.
  - ZIP output: Deflate level 6, UTF-8 names, Unix modes, ZIP64 for entries from 3.75 GiB, and an extended-timestamp field next to the DOS time. tar output: GNU format (long names as `L`/`K` records), owner 0.
  - ZIP's DOS times have no time zone and std has no time zone API, so they're read and written as UTC. Explorer shows them as local time, so a time can look shifted by the UTC offset; the extended timestamp, which 7-Zip and Info-ZIP read, is exact.
  - A ZIP name that has no UTF-8 flag and isn't valid UTF-8 is read as CP437, so names from other OEM code pages (Japanese, for example) come out garbled.
  - A tar name or gzip FNAME that isn't valid UTF-8 is read as Windows-1252, its five unassigned bytes as U+FFFD. Read as Latin-1 (RFC 1952 says so for FNAME), the bytes 0x80 to 0x9F would be control characters and refuse the whole job; Shift-JIS names, whose lead bytes fall there, come out garbled instead.
- **Extract:**
  - It writes into a `.grape-tmp-…` folder in the destination folder: regular files (`create_new`) and folders only, with their mtimes, and on Unix their modes with owner read and write (rwx for folders) always kept. The temp folder is hidden on macOS but shows in Explorer while it runs.
  - **Folder publish:** `rename` would replace an existing empty folder (Unix, Windows 1607 and later), so it isn't used for the folder. The final name is reserved with `create_dir` and re-planned (" 2", " 3") like a file name, up to 3 times, then "A folder named … keeps appearing; try again." Each top-level item is then renamed into it, and the temp folder is removed. That is no-clobber but not atomic. A rename refused with "access denied" is retried for up to 3.15 s: Windows won't rename a folder while a file inside it is open, and a virus scanner opens each new file right after Grape closes it.
  - On an error or a cancel, the temp folder and any reserved folder are removed. The archive is opened read-only and never touched.
- **Mark of the Web (Windows):** if the source has a `Zone.Identifier` stream, Grape writes the same bytes onto a converted archive and onto every extracted file (not folders), as Explorer does. A converted archive gets it on the temp file and again once published, because the copy fallback (a volume without hard links) copies only the data. An extracted file gets it before its mtime is set, since writing a stream may change the file's last-write time. On a volume without streams (FAT32, exFAT) it's skipped. macOS's `com.apple.quarantine` needs xattr calls, so it belongs in `grape-platform` later. Maxwell's call: on by default (proposed), or a setting.
- **RAR** (2026-10-26, decided by lane A under Maxwell's "just go"; proposed, confirm):
  - **What:** RAR → ZIP, TAR or GZIP, and Extract Archive on RAR 4 and RAR 5, in `src/archive/rar/`. `bsdtar`, a separate program Grape builds from libarchive (BSD-2-Clause) and bundles, turns the RAR into a tar stream. Grape's own preflight, name check, bounds and writers do the rest. Not the OS's bsdtar: Windows 10 22H2 can have libarchive 3.5.x, macOS 13–15.3 has 3.5.3, and neither decodes filtered RAR4. Not linked into Grape either, so a crash in the C decoder ends only bsdtar (decision 9: the sidecar is kept rather than a `--grape-rar-worker`).
  - **libarchive 3.8.10** (2026-10-14; `libarchive-3.8.10.tar.gz` SHA-256 `a1bffbe5…cc57c`, 9,301,407 bytes), pinned in `scripts/bsdtar/sources.json`. *D9 security override:* it fixes the silent truncation of a split RAR5 file whose next volume is missing (#3414, `dec66c38` on `patch/3.8`) and the seekable-ZIP reader outbidding RAR5 (#3412, `ac1aad27`), and carries about 25 RAR/RAR5 hardening merges. It passes D9 by itself on 2026-10-28. 3.8.9, this entry's earlier pin, has both bugs and prints "(null)" for read errors.
  - **Patches** in `scripts/bsdtar/patches/`, applied in order. Each SHA-256 is in `sources.json`:
    - `0001` upstream b59b9e16 (PR #3696), with its NULL-name guard moved outside `#if HAVE_REGEX_H`, which compiled it out of MSVC builds. *D9 security override* (merged 2026-10-18, passes D9 on 2026-11-01): a NULL-pointer crash on names that can't be converted. Stock 3.8.10 crashes on Linux too.
    - `0002` upstream 777dc1a (PR #3696), the library hunk only. *D9 security override* (2026-10-18 → 11-01): an access violation on Windows for names outside the code page (#3681, #3695).
    - `0003` upstream ef743732 (PR #3709). *D9 security override* (2026-10-18 → 11-01): an unbounded RAR4 symlink target, a memory DoS.
    - `0005` Grape's: `activeCodePage` UTF-8 in bsdtar's Windows manifest, so every RAR name converts.
    - `0006` Grape's: `@archive` reads rar and rar5 only, with no filters and no `mtree:checkfs`. **Required:** stock 3.8.10 writes an empty tar with exit 0 for every piped RAR4, reads a RAR4 whose last member is a ZIP as that ZIP when given the file, copies local files named by an mtree spec, and runs an external `gzip` from `PATH`.
    - `0007` Grape's: the version line becomes `bsdtar 3.8.10-grape.1 - …`, the marker the bundling gate looks for. `.1` goes up with every change to the series.
    - D9 is about third-party releases, so it doesn't apply to 0005–0007.
    - **e1d79210** (PR #3707, report `@archive` data warnings) is **not** carried: it's a correctness fix that no RAR path reaches once 0006 is in, so the security override doesn't cover it. It passes D9 on 2026-11-04 if it's ever wanted.
  - **The command** is `bsdtar -c -P -f - --format pax @-`.
    - Grape writes the RAR into bsdtar's stdin through a pipe, from a thread, and never hands it a file handle. Stderr is read on another thread and only its first 8 KiB is kept.
    - It runs in a new, empty, private folder, removed afterwards, with only these variables: `PATH` (that folder), `TZ=UTC0` (RAR4 DOS times then read as UTC, like D27's ZIP times), `TAR_READER_OPTIONS=rar:hdrcharset=UTF-8`, `SystemRoot` on Windows, and `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8` on macOS. Without a UTF-8 locale, `hdrcharset` makes bsdtar fail on every archive. On Windows it also runs with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`.
    - `-P` keeps names as stored, so Grape's validator refuses `/abs` and `..\x`.
  - **The output counts only when** bsdtar exits 0 with nothing on stderr; its stdout ends with a tar end marker followed only by zero bytes (it pads to 10,240 bytes); and its entries match the RAR's own headers one to one, by kind, size and CRC32. bsdtar doesn't check the CRC32 of stored entries. A warning (a name it can't translate, an unreadable name it skips) still exits 0, and so does a junction or file-copy entry, which it writes as an empty folder or file.
    - Its stderr is mapped to a reason by libarchive's own message text, never shown and never logged. The log gets the matched pattern and a byte count.
  - **Names** are normalized to NFC, for conversions and extraction alike, before the name check: bsdtar on macOS returns NFD.
  - **No time cap; a stall watchdog.** If Grape has waited 60 s on bsdtar while bsdtar took no input and wrote no output, Grape kills it ("x.rar took too long, so Grape stopped."). Time Grape spends writing its own output doesn't count. Cancel kills bsdtar, joins the threads and reaps the process, all within 50 ms of work. Progress is the share of the RAR written into bsdtar.
  - **RAR5 junctions and file copies** ("store identical files as references") are left out and counted: junctions as links, file copies as `LeftOut::copies`.
  - **Refused before bsdtar starts**, by a Rust walk of the RAR's headers (`rar/header.rs`). It reads every header and skips the data, then reports the first of these in order:
    - encrypted headers or entries, zero-length ones included: "x.rar is password-protected.";
    - multi-volume (the main header's flag, a split entry, or an end block that says more follows): "x.rar is one part of a multi-part RAR archive, which Grape can't read yet.";
    - solid RAR4 (libarchive can't read past the first file, so no partial output): "x.rar uses RAR 4's solid compression, which Grape can't read yet.";
    - a RAR5 dictionary over 64 MiB, read from the full 5-bit field plus RAR 7's fraction bits (libarchive's own check reads only 4 bits): "x.rar needs a RAR dictionary over 64 MiB, which Grape can't read yet.";
    - an unknown unpacked size, a compression version or redirection type libarchive doesn't know, or RAR 7 compression: "x.rar uses a RAR feature Grape can't read yet.";
    - damage (a bad header CRC, a cut file, a stored RAR5 file whose data area isn't its size, which bsdtar pads with zeros and exit 0 when there's no CRC32 to check, or **no end-of-archive block**, which libarchive accepts, so a file cut between entries would otherwise convert): "x.rar isn't a readable RAR file.";
    - two RAR4 files in a row with one name (`\` read as `/`), which libarchive takes for the parts of a split file and bsdtar skips with a warning: "x.rar has two items named a.txt.";
    - more than 100,000 entries, or declared sizes over the bounds.
    - The walk checks the cancel at every read from the file, at most 64 KiB apart (a RAR5 header can be 2 MiB).
    - A self-extracting archive (an `MZ` or ELF stub before the signature) gets "x.rar is a self-extracting archive, which Grape can't read yet.": the RAR signature must be at offset 0.
  - **After bsdtar:** names it can't encode give "x.rar has item names in a text encoding Grape can't read yet." A RAR file (not a folder) named `notes\`, `a/` or `./`, which bsdtar writes as a file with its data, gives "x.rar has an item with a name Grape won't write: …" rather than becoming a folder or vanishing. A crash or an abnormal exit gives "Grape's RAR helper stopped unexpectedly." If it can't start: "Grape's RAR helper couldn't start."
  - **Bundling:** bsdtar joins FFmpeg in `tauri.sidecars.conf.json`'s `externalBin`, with `licenses/libarchive/`. It's one overlay because a second one would replace the array. When CI builds FFmpeg, per-OS overlays (`tauri.sidecars.windows.conf.json` with grape-heif too, and `tauri.sidecars.macos.conf.json`) replace it and the HEIC slice's `tauri.heif.windows.conf.json`. No build ever passes two overlays that both set `externalBin`. The gate has a `bsdtar` rule (the `-grape` marker, `licenses/libarchive/`, a `## libarchive` notices section). So no installer or DMG carries bsdtar until CI builds FFmpeg too (lane B, D24). Until then RAR runs in `tauri dev` and in CI's tests. `about.hbs` words bsdtar in the future tense until then, since every bundle ships `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`; the change that bundles it puts that back in the present tense. The cost: tauri-build fails on any listed file that's missing, so every `tauri dev` with this overlay, FFmpeg testing included, needs a bsdtar in `binaries/` too (`build.ps1 -Install` / `build.sh --install`, or CI's artifact). Installed, it's `bsdtar.exe` next to `Grape.exe` and `Grape.app/Contents/MacOS/bsdtar`. The app finds it there at startup, never on `PATH` (with `SidecarPaths::bsdtar`, lane B).
    - **Update 2026-10-26 (#27):** CI builds FFmpeg now (D24), so the per-OS overlays above replaced `tauri.sidecars.conf.json` and `tauri.heif.windows.conf.json`, and every installer and DMG carries bsdtar, from the same run's `bsdtar` workflow, with `licenses/libarchive/`. `about.hbs` words it in the present tense again. A RAR drop without it is now a broken install on Windows and macOS: "Grape's RAR helper is missing" / "Reinstalling Grape brings it back." (D35's amendment).
  - **Build:** `scripts/bsdtar/build.ps1` (Windows x64, MSVC, static CRT /MT) and `build.sh` (macOS arm64 and x86_64, deployment target 13.0, merged with `lipo` and signed ad hoc; and Linux, for local tests only). Every optional library is off. The patches are applied with `git apply` in a throwaway repository inside the extract: inside the Grape checkout, which holds `target/`, `git apply` would skip every file and still exit 0. Each file a patch names must have changed. CI's `bsdtar` workflow builds both in every run that needs them and checks:
    - the imports (KERNEL32, ADVAPI32 and bcrypt only on Windows; libSystem only on macOS);
    - the exact version line;
    - the manifest, `minos 13.0` and both architectures;
    - the copyright lines of the compiled sources against `licenses/libarchive/copyright-lines.txt`;
    - a smoke test in Grape's exact environment on libarchive's own test files: filtered RAR4, solid and Unicode RAR5 pass; encrypted and multi-volume fail; ZIP, mtree and gzip input are refused.
    - It's cached by the hash of `scripts/bsdtar/` and `licenses/libarchive/copyright-lines.txt`: the copyright lines are checked only in a full build, so a change to them must miss the cache. No GitHub Release until M7. `scripts/sidecars.lock` has no bsdtar entry.
  - **Licenses:** `licenses/libarchive/`: COPYING; the copyright lines of every compiled source file (COPYING's notice is a template with `<author(s)>`); the UC Regents, NetBSD Foundation, PostgreSQL (Windows code), BLAKE2 (CC0) and Unicode notices; OpenBSD's ISC notices for arc4random and readpassphrase (compiled only where the system lacks them, so normally in neither shipping build); and `SOURCE.md`. Plus a hand-written `## libarchive` section in `about.hbs`. No `deny.toml` or `about.toml` change: they see only crates. (The earlier note here, "It needs BSD-2-Clause in `deny.toml`", is withdrawn: adding it would pass, through zerocopy, but would silently admit future BSD-2-only crates.)
  - **Tests:**
    - a Rust writer of stored RAR4 and RAR5 archives for every preflight case;
    - process tests against a fake bsdtar (the test binary): exact pumping, the environment and folder, a 1 MiB stderr, crashes, a stall, slow-but-steady progress, cancel;
    - with the real bsdtar (`--features rar-it`, `GRAPE_TEST_BSDTAR`), eight of libarchive's test files embedded as uuencoded text with their BSD-2-Clause notice, plus the writer's cases.
    - **Tested** (2026-10-26, Linux only; Windows and macOS run first in CI):
      - the build: `build.sh` on Ubuntu 24.04 (gcc 13.3, CMake 3.28) built, checked and installed bsdtar in about 65 s: 912,584 bytes, libc only, the exact version line, the copyright lines matching, byte-identical to the prototype's build and the same from a root inside the checkout. Negative runs: a tampered patch, a skipped `git apply`, bad pins in `sources.json`, a stock binary, an extra library token and a changed binary each stop it with a clear message. `build.ps1`'s parts that don't need MSVC (reading `sources.json`, the download and its checks, extracting, patching inside the checkout, the version line, the copyright lines, byte for byte the same as `build.sh`'s) ran in PowerShell 7.6 on Linux; its MSVC build, `dumpbin`, `mt.exe` and `vswhere` steps have not run anywhere yet;
      - `smoke.py`: 13 of 13 cases pass against that build; stock 3.8.10 fails 9 of them (empty output for every piped RAR4, encrypted headers and a lone volume "converted", ZIP, mtree, gzip and empty input accepted or misreported);
      - the gate: 17 tests (`node --test scripts/check-sidecars.test.mjs`; the 17th checks that the bsdtar workflow's cache keys cover `copyright-lines.txt`), and the real gate passes the Linux build;
      - the engine: 53 new unit tests without bsdtar (the preflight on 39 writer cases and every refusal, a stored RAR5 file shorter than its size, two RAR4 files in a row with one name, and a cancel seen within 64 KiB of reading, through a FIFO on Unix; the tap, a file named like a folder among its refusals, and `drain_zeros`; 13 runner tests, the stderr classifier among them, and 11 engine tests against a fake bsdtar, which is the test binary: pumping, the environment and folder, a 1 MiB stderr, a crash, exit 3, a stall, slow-but-steady progress, cancel at a progress callback and cancel while the job waits on a quiet bsdtar, a missing program); 12 `rar-it` tests with the real Linux build (the 8 libarchive files converted to ZIP, TAR and GZIP and extracted, with CRC32s matched to the headers; the 23 writer cases the preflight accepts; DOS times as UTC; NFC; 4 MiB of trailing junk; the version line; files named `notes\`, `./`, `a/` and `a\`; two RAR4 files in a row with one name; a short stored RAR5 file without a CRC32). All pass on Linux: `rar-it` 63 passed, 1 ignored (the fake's entry point). Against stock 3.8.10, 6 of the first 9 `rar-it` tests fail, on the empty RAR4 output (the tap's count check catches it) and on the version line;
      - the workflows pass actionlint.
  - **Revisit if:**
    - a libarchive release contains b59b9e16 (with the guard outside the `#if`), 777dc1a and ef743732: drop those patches;
    - users want multi-part RARs: piping every volume in order into stdin works (tested on libarchive's sets);
    - Windows ARM64 ships (`-A ARM64`);
    - unknown RAR5 redirection types or BLAKE2-only entries turn up in real archives;
    - D24's FFmpeg build jobs slip: bsdtar could ship on its own in a temporary overlay.
  - *2026-10-28 (lane A): bsdtar is contained.* It goes through the app's containment hook (D33), as the image helper and the SVG and PDF workers do, and fails closed like them.
    - On Windows it starts with `DETACHED_PROCESS`, not `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` ("The command" above): its three stdio are pipes, so it needs no console, and the hidden one `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` makes would have a host that is a second process in a one-process job.
    - Right after the spawn, before the pump starts, so before bsdtar has read a byte of the RAR, the hook puts it in a Job Object of its own: **1 GiB** of committed memory, ended when its job ends or Grape exits or crashes, one active process, no error dialogs, and the UI limits unless Grape itself runs in a job.
    - A bsdtar that can't be contained is killed and reaped, its folder removed, and the job says "Grape's RAR helper couldn't start.", the words for one that can't start; the hook's reason is only in the detail (`RarFailure::Contain`).
    - One that fails by itself (an odd exit, exit 1 or anything on stderr) after committing 90% of the cap says "x.rar needs too much memory, so Grape stopped." (`RarFailure::OverMemory`): libarchive reports an allocation that failed as an error of its own. A clean run, and one Grape killed (a cancel, a stall, its own refusal), keep their verdicts.
    - **Why 1 GiB**, read in libarchive 3.8.10's readers (`archive_read_support_format_rar.c`, `archive_read_support_format_rar5.c`, `archive_read.c`): RAR 4 holds a PPMd model of at most 256 MiB (`(n + 1) << 20`, as the archive asks) beside an LZSS window of at most 4 MiB (`DICTIONARY_MAX_SIZE`), a 256 KiB filter VM and a 128 KiB unpack buffer. RAR 5 holds a window of at most 64 MiB and, from the start of each file, a filter buffer of the same size (later at most 4 MiB a filter), with 8,192 filter slots. The read-ahead buffer doubles from 10 KiB to fit what a reader asks for, about 20 MiB for RAR 5's largest block (16 MiB). So about 300 MiB at most for one archive; 1 GiB leaves more than three times that. RAR 4's list of pending filters has no bound of its own, so a crafted file can make it grow with its size: the cap stops that.
    - **Tests:** unit tests with a fake hook: it's called once and before a byte is pumped (the source's shared read position is still 0 after a pause in the hook); a refusing hook kills the fake at once (it would write a file 1.5 s later), leaves no folder and is worded as above; the 90% rule after a crash and after exit 1 with and without stderr, and not after a clean run or a cancel. On Windows a `rar-it` test converts and extracts a RAR 4 and a RAR 5 with each real bsdtar in a Job Object as the app makes it (under cargo nested, so without the UI limits), and CI's `rar-it` job also runs the test binary outside cargo with `GRAPE_TEST_JOB_UI=1`: a child that breaks away from the runner's job does the same, and each bsdtar's job must have the UI limits. GitHub's runner allows no breakaway, so there that step only warns, and the PC checks the UI-limited job (`TESTING.md` → "RAR, M3").
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26, by the unit tests in `archive/`:
  - Every pair, and extraction of zip, tar, tar.gz, tgz and plain gz. Outputs are read back and compared entry by entry: names, bytes, links, mtimes and modes, through round trips too.
  - Refusals: zip-slip names, device names, trailing dots, ADS, case duplicates, escaping links and the two-step link attack, a 1 GiB gzip bomb and others under small limits, too many entries, encrypted and bzip2 ZIPs, lying sizes, bad checksums and truncation.
  - After the review (same day): names too long for any volume, implied folders against the bound, a deep name checked in linear time, chained links, a ZIP naming a file twice, a folder named twice, PAX records that name an entry two ways, a sparse tar kept byte for byte under a small bound, ZIP end records claiming too many entries (ZIP64 too) or missing near the end, ZIP progress following the entries written, the mark on the published file, a plain gz of a `._` file, Windows-1252 tar names, empty tars and tarballs, and (Linux) a name ext4 refuses. Each of those tests was checked to fail with its fix reverted.
  - Cancel half-way. Every case leaves the source unchanged and no temp file or folder behind.
  - The Mark-of-the-Web test runs only on Windows (CI); it also checks that extracted files keep the archive's mtimes. Nothing has run on the PC or a Mac yet.
  - **Manual QA (Windows, not yet run):** extract a one-folder archive with Defender's real-time protection on, and check that it publishes (the folder move retries) and that its files carry the mark.
- **Revisit if:** zip 9.0.4 or later passes D9 (take it: it fixes `enclosed_name`, which Grape doesn't use, and adds duplicate detection), users hit OEM-code-page names or shifted DOS times, LZMA or Zstandard ZIPs are wanted (lzma-rust2 is Apache-2.0), or names with `:` and the other refused characters turn out to be common in real archives (they refuse the whole job today).

## D29: The PDF engine (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `grape-engines` converts PDF → JPG, PNG, TXT and MD, and runs Split PDF, Merge PDFs and Remove Metadata on PDFs, in `src/pdf/`. Lane A builds it by agreement with lane B, who owns the crate (as for D27). PDFium renders pages and reads text; lopdf, with Grape's own page copier, splits, merges and removes metadata. **Every PDF job runs in a separate worker process.** It's wired into `run_job` and the app (D35).
- **Why a worker process:**
  - PDFium is a large C++ parser, and Grape feeds it untrusted files. pdfium-render's bindings are process-global (bound once, never unloaded), its `thread_safe` mutex panics on every later call once it's poisoned, PDFium's partition_alloc is off in this build, and a memory fault or a failed allocation kills whatever process it happens in. In Grape's own process that would take the tray, the drag hooks and every other job down with it (golden rule 4).
  - lopdf has had crashes on crafted files that `catch_unwind` can't catch: RUSTSEC-2026-0187 (deep nesting → stack overflow, fixed in 0.42). A stack overflow or a failed allocation aborts.
  - Killing the process is the only hard stop for a slow page. PDFium's pausable renderer exists only as raw `unsafe` bindings, and parsing and text extraction have no pause hook at all.
  - Worker processes also run side by side, where in-process jobs would queue on pdfium-render's one mutex.
  - It's not a sandbox: the worker has the user's rights. A low-integrity or AppContainer token (Windows) or a sandboxed helper (macOS) is v2.
- **How it runs:**
  - The worker is Grape's own executable, `Grape --grape-pdf-worker`: `main.rs` hands that argument to `grape_engines::pdf::worker_main()` before Tauri starts, so no window, tray, logger or single-instance plugin. So there's no extra file to bundle or build per target, and the app's signature covers it. `grape-engines` also builds a `grape-pdf-worker` bin, only for the `pdf-it` tests.
  - The request is JSON on stdin. The worker binds PDFium with `Pdfium::bind_to_library(<absolute path from the request>)`, never `bind_to_system_library` or `Pdfium::default()` (which tries `./` and then the system). It does one operation, reads sources read-only, writes only new files into the temp folder the request names, and prints JSON lines: the page count once it knows it, progress, then `done` or `error`. It exits 0 or 1. The job runs on a thread with a 64 MiB stack; a panic there is caught and reported as an error, and an abort or a stack overflow just ends the process. It never logs, and no line it prints holds a path, a name or file contents. Both sides refuse unknown JSON fields, so a worker from another version fails cleanly.
  - The parent sniffs every source, makes a hidden `.grape-tmp-` folder in the destination folder, starts the worker (stdin and stdout piped, stderr discarded, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` on Windows), passes progress on (never backwards), and kills it on cancel (checked every 100 ms) or after a timeout (10 minutes, plus 5 s for each page it reports, counting at most 2,000 pages: a file's `/Count` can claim a million, so at most about 2.9 hours). It publishes with the no-clobber helpers (`publish.rs`'s `finalize` for a file, and D27's folder publish, re-planned with `pages_folder_name` for a render's folder) and removes the temp folder on every path.
  - **Heap bound:** the worker's own heap holds at most 4 GiB. lopdf can be made to hold far more than a file's size, and no decompression bound stops it: an object stream whose entries all point at one offset parses the object there once per entry (8 entries over a million-zero array, a file of a few KiB, peaked at 924 MiB; 1,000 entries would be about 110 GiB), and parsed objects take 30 to 60 times their bytes. So both worker executables (the app's `main.rs`, and the `grape-pdf-worker` test bin) install a global allocator, `WorkerHeap`: `System` with every change reported to `grape_engines::pdf::heap`, which counts nothing until the worker arms it with the request's `maxHeapBytes`. Past the bound the worker exits at once with code 86, and the parent says "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped." In the app's own process the allocator only passes calls through (one atomic load each). That allocator is the only `unsafe` code here, in the two binaries, with SAFETY comments; it needs no `unsafe` in `grape-engines`. It counts only Rust's heap (lopdf, Grape's buffers): PDFium allocates with its own `malloc`.
  - Apart from that allocator, no `unsafe`: `bind_to_library` is a safe function, so `grape-engines` keeps `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.
- **Crates** (grape-engines only):

| Crate | Version | License | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| pdfium-render | =0.9.4 (2026-09-06), no default features, `pdfium_7881` and `thread_safe` | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | Safe bindings to PDFium, loaded at run time |
| lopdf | =0.45.0 (2026-09-08), no default features | MIT | Reads and writes PDF objects for split, merge and metadata |
| libloading | 0.7.4, through pdfium-render | ISC | Loads the library. Already locked for Linux (libappindicator); now shipped on Windows and macOS too, with winapi 0.3.9 on Windows |

  - pdfium-render's API version is pinned by name: `pdfium_latest` moves with crate releases and would silently change which functions it binds. Never enable `image_*`, `pdfium_enable_v8`, `pdfium_enable_xfa`, `pdfium_use_skia`, `pdfium_use_win32`, `static` or `bindings`. Most of its own dependencies are `version = "0"`, so they float on every `cargo update`: check them one by one (D9).
  - lopdf without defaults has no rayon and no chrono clock. Never enable `embed_image`: it pulls `image` with default features, which would switch on rayon, AVIF (rav1e, BSD-2-Clause) and EXR in Grape's own `image`. 0.46.0 (2026-10-21) can't come in before 2026-11-04.
  - **Lock pins** for D9's 14 days: `cargo update -p inout --precise 0.2.2`, `-p unicode-normalization --precise 0.1.25` (0.1.26 is from 2026-10-16), and `-p sha2@0.11.1 --precise 0.11.0` (0.11.1 was published 2026-10-12T18:58Z, so it passes only from 18:58Z today). inout 0.2.3 is yanked, and 0.2.4 and 0.2.5 (2026-10-16 and -19) fix soundness bugs: a Stacked Borrows violation, and a missing output-length check in `into_padded_blocks`. There's no RustSec advisory for them, so the pin is allowed, but take 0.2.5 once it passes (2026-11-02). Grape reaches that code only when lopdf decrypts an encrypted file as it loads it, before Grape refuses the file (below).
  - The lock gains 42 packages, all at least 14 days old (the newest is either 1.19.0, 2026-10-06). Resolving also moved three existing edges onto versions the lock already had, with no new version: num_enum_derive now takes proc-macro-crate 3.5.0 (was 1.3.1), tempfile takes getrandom 0.4.3 (was 0.3.4), and chrono lists its wasm-only dependencies. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` now counts 312 crates (was 267).
  - `"ISC"` joins `deny.toml`'s `allow` and `about.toml`'s `accepted`, for libloading. ENGINES rule 1 already names ISC. `cargo deny` passes, and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` is regenerated.
- **PDFium itself:** [bblanchon/pdfium-binaries](https://github.com/bblanchon/pdfium-binaries) release `chromium/8086` (PDFium 157.0.8086.0, 2026-10-05): `pdfium-win-x64.tgz` and the universal `pdfium-mac-univ.tgz`, never the `pdfium-v8-*` assets.
  - Built without V8 (no JavaScript) and without XFA (`args.gn`). The DLL imports only KERNEL32, ADVAPI32, GDI32 and USER32: no network library and no Visual C++ runtime. The dylib is arm64 + x86_64, minimum macOS 13.0 like Grape, and links only system frameworks.
  - There's no version handshake: a library fits when it exports every function pdfium-render binds, with the same prototypes. 8086's DLL and both Mac slices export all 451 that `pdfium_7881` with `thread_safe` binds, and 8086's headers only add functions to 7881's (checked 2026-10-26, as for 8100). The Windows and Mac libraries haven't been loaded yet: the `pdf-it` tests in CI are their first run. The Linux build of the same release (`pdfium-linux-x64.tgz` `588577cf52dabc1a444988bac841920df54cc2f141801424de97ab04f4fbb935`, listed in the release's attestation) passes every `pdf-it` test. Check the exports again whenever either side moves.
  - **Bump to chromium/8100** (2026-10-12T20:25Z) once it passes D9, from 2026-10-26T20:25Z: win-x64.tgz `51621bcc1d9be9ea448f4b84b0516862b928e3968d3fca899af1a6d6e2848d6d` (3,875,218 bytes, `bin/pdfium.dll` `ae913f407e8d3f5cdbf08a9869564d647b13e132e01eb376c0a5a33b7a227a45`), mac-univ.tgz `f1c4b41e4b3d875b35ab29dbb366277af3ff99db13b7b1aaf0295a4e9da4e1d0` (7,130,030 bytes, `lib/libpdfium.dylib` `57434000a47b6d25c8fe5d18c2774833311eefb1a4a9af1c5ca858b309934655`), PDFium commit `f51279699cb59754d1f49aef10eb8e825fe56532`, builder commit `5b4d6b2`. Redo `licenses/pdfium/` from its Windows archive.
  - **Licenses**, all permissive (no GPL, LGPL or MPL; golden rule 6): PDFium is BSD-3-Clause (some files Apache-2.0), the packaging MIT, and the libraries built in are under Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, Zlib, Unicode-3.0, IJG, the libpng license v2, the FreeType License, HarfBuzz's Old MIT, the Anti-Grain Geometry notice, and Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception (LLVM libc, libc++, and Dragonbox, which Grape takes over its BSL-1.0 option). FreeType is FTL or GPL-2.0, and Grape takes the FTL, whose credit line is in the notices. ICU's `icu.txt` also has GPL texts, for ICU4C's `aclocal.m4` and `config.guess`: build scripts that aren't compiled in. ENGINES rule 1 doesn't name FTL, IJG, libpng-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, Old MIT or the AGG notice; widening its list is Maxwell's call, with O6.
  - **`licenses/pdfium/`** holds the Windows archive's 17 license texts and its `LICENSE` (the Mac archive's `pdfium.txt` has `// ` on every line), and `SOURCE.md`: the hashes, the SLSA attestation, the builder commit `5325fa6` and its patches, and the PDFium commit `2fd6cff57d9412cc42ef1a7e4e0a59b13a1e7cec`. The builder checks out the branch head and doesn't record it; that commit was the head before the build and still is, so it's almost certainly the source (an inference). libc++ is built in with no text of its own in the archive, so `SOURCE.md` and `about.hbs` name it. `about.hbs` has a hand-written PDFium section before the Rust crates.
- **Fetching** (D25, extended): `scripts/sidecars.lock` is format 2, with a `libraries` section next to `sidecars`. A library has a version (shown only) and, per target, its archive, its path inside it and that file's SHA-256.
  - A library can't be run, so instead of `-version` the extracted file's SHA-256 must match the lock, after the archive's own size and hash. The URL's ending says how to extract: `.zip`, or `.tgz` with the OS's own tar by full path (`%SystemRoot%\System32\tar.exe`, which Windows 10 1803 and later have; `/usr/bin/tar`), never one on `PATH`.
  - It lands as `binaries/pdfium-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.dll` and `binaries/libpdfium-universal-apple-darwin.dylib`. The Mac library is given as `universal-apple-darwin` (no lipo for libraries); the script checks that `lipo -archs` has arm64 and x86_64, signs it ad hoc again (the build signs only its arm64 half) and verifies that, and copies it to `binaries/macos/libpdfium.dylib`, because `bundle.macOS.frameworks` keeps a file's name.
  - `-Only <name>` / `--only <name>` fetches just one sidecar or library; CI's test and build jobs fetch only `pdfium`. The stamp covers libraries too, and keeps the lines of what a run didn't fetch while those files are there, so `--only pdfium` after a full run doesn't make the next full run download FFmpeg again. A sidecar and a library can't share a name, and no archive path may start with `-`.
- **Bundling:** two overlays, because each OS needs a different setting and tauri-build fails on a listed file that's missing. A build passes the one for its OS with `--config` (from `apps/desktop`). Not `tauri.windows.conf.json`: Tauri loads that by itself, so every plain `cargo` build would need the DLL.
  - `tauri.pdfium.windows.conf.json`: `bundle.resources` maps `binaries/pdfium-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.dll` to `pdfium.dll`, which NSIS installs next to `Grape.exe`, and tauri-build copies next to `target\<profile>\grape.exe` for dev runs. Plus `licenses/pdfium/`.
  - `tauri.pdfium.macos.conf.json`: `bundle.macOS.frameworks` lists `binaries/macos/libpdfium.dylib`. The bundler copies it to `Grape.app/Contents/Frameworks/` and signs it, as a library without the runtime option, before the app. In dev, tauri-build copies it to `target/Frameworks/` (`target/<triple>/Frameworks/` for a cross build) and adds an `@executable_path/../Frameworks` rpath, so `current_exe()/../Frameworks/libpdfium.dylib` works in both. Not `resources` on the Mac: files in `Contents/Resources` are sealed as data, not signed as code. Plus `licenses/pdfium/`.
  - CI's `build-windows` and `build-macos`, and `release.yml`'s Windows build, fetch PDFium and pass these, then check that the files landed. The release fetches it fresh, without CI's cache.
  - From M7, the Windows bundler signs the DLL in place in `binaries/`, which changes its hash, so the next fetch runs again (as D25 says for sidecars).
- **`bundle.macOS.hardenedRuntime: false`** (`tauri.conf.json`) while the DMG is signed ad hoc. The hardened runtime turns on library validation, which loads only code signed by Apple or with the app's own Team ID. Ad-hoc code has no Team ID, so the ad-hoc DMG would most likely refuse the ad-hoc `libpdfium.dylib` (not verified: no Mac here). An ad-hoc app can't be notarized anyway, so turning it off loses nothing now. With Developer ID (M9) the bundler signs the dylib with the app's identity, the Team IDs match, and the hardened runtime goes back on, with no `disable-library-validation` entitlement. CI's DMG job fails if Grape.app has the runtime flag.
- **What the operations do** (`src/pdf/`; the engine's details are in its code and tests):
  - **PDF → JPG / PNG** (PDFium): every page at 300 DPI on white, with its annotations and form appearances, as opaque RGB in both formats. JPEG is quality 90 with the page's DPI in its header; PNG has default compression and no DPI. A page over 100 megapixels, or over 65,535 pixels on a side (JPEG's limit), is drawn at the highest DPI that fits (only that page, never above 300), and the `done` line lists those pages; the toast says how many ("1 page drawn below 300 DPI", D35), and FORMAT_MATRIX's "300 DPI" has a note for it. More than 2,000 pages is refused before anything is drawn. One page gives the planned file; more give the planned folder `{stem}` with `{stem} {n}.{ext}` inside (`page_file_name`, zero-padded to the page count), re-planned with `pages_folder_name` if the folder is taken meanwhile.
  - **PDF → TXT / MD** (PDFium): each page's characters in the order the file holds them, so multi-column pages and tables can interleave. UTF-8 without a byte-order mark, LF line ends. TXT keeps PDFium's line breaks, drops other control characters and trailing spaces, skips pages without text and puts a blank line between the others (no form feed). MD:
    - Characters become lines at a line break or where the baseline jumps.
    - The body size is the most common size by characters. Sizes at least 1.15 times the body size are headings: the largest is `#`, the next `##`, all smaller ones `###`, for lines of at most 160 characters.
    - A line joins the paragraph above when it has the same size and level, isn't a bullet, and sits at most 1.5 times the page's usual line spacing below (the lower median of its same-size gaps). A line ending in `-` joins without a space; a soft hyphen is dropped.
    - A line starting with a bullet (• ◦ ▪ ‣ ● ○ ■ –) and a space starts a new `- ` item. Markdown's special characters are escaped. A page break ends a paragraph (no `---`).
    - Text turned on its side (an arXiv margin stamp) or upside down: a character's size is its font size times the length of its matrix's vertical axis (pdfium-render's `scaled_font_size` multiplies by the matrix's `d` alone, which is 0 at 90° and negative at 180°, and split such text into one character per paragraph). Text that runs more up or down than across makes lines of its own, split where its x jumps; they are always paragraphs of their own, never headings, and don't count towards the body size.
    - No text at all: "… has no text to extract — it may be a scan." (OCR is v2). More than 256 MiB of text, counted in the output's own format, is refused: "… has more than 256 MiB of text, more than Grape extracts."
    - A bullet's text gets the same start escapes as a paragraph (`- \# of users`), and a paragraph starting with `~` is escaped, so `~~~` can't open a code block.
  - **Split PDF and Merge PDFs** (lopdf and Grape's copier, `copy.rs`): inherited MediaBox, CropBox, Resources and Rotate are flattened onto each page (no MediaBox falls back to the CropBox, then Letter). Everything a page references is copied through a worklist with one id map per source (no recursion), so a shared font is copied once per output.
    - Each output gets a new catalog holding only its pages.
