So the outline, AcroForm, structure tree, named destinations, page labels, open action, optional-content properties (`/OCProperties`), Info and XMP of the sources are all dropped. Pages lose `/StructParents` and `/B`; widgets lose `/Parent`, so form fields keep their look but are no longer fields.
    - Annotations on a page stay, except links to a page the output doesn't get or to a named destination. Any other reference to such a page becomes null.
    - lopdf's outputs are PDF 1.5 or later (it writes a cross-reference stream), the highest version of their sources.
    - Split always makes the planned folder `{stem} Pages`, even for one page, with `{stem} {n}.pdf` inside (`page_file_name`). It makes at most 100,000 files, like the archive engine's entry cap (D27); a longer file is refused before anything is written ("… has more than 100,000 pages, more than Grape splits."), in the PDFium fallback too. (The 2,000-page render bound would refuse long splits that work.) Merge writes the planned `{first} Merged.pdf` with the inputs in the planner's order (the panel reorders the paths first, D31).
    - **Falling back to PDFium:** lopdf loads under a 512 MiB decompression cap (`max_decompressed_size`). A file lopdf can't parse, whose page count doesn't match the root's `/Count`, or with objects its table lists but lopdf couldn't read, counts as damaged for lopdf. So does a hybrid-reference file (it has `/XRefStm`) that uses any object lopdf didn't load: lopdf 0.45 reads the extra cross-reference stream only through the newest trailer and only when that trailer has a `/Prev`, so objects listed only there (their classic entries say "free") go missing silently, often the structure tree or a font. A hybrid file lopdf does read whole (Word's layout, `/Prev` with `/XRefStm`) stays with lopdf. Split then uses PDFium's page import (`FPDF_ImportPages`, through pdfium-render's safe API) instead. Merge sends the whole job to PDFium as soon as one input is damaged for lopdf. The `done` line says when PDFium did it.
    - The PDFium fallbacks refuse any input with a security handler (`FPDF_GetSecurityHandlerRevision` other than -1, revisions 5 and 6 included), as "… is password-protected": they write a new file without it, so an owner password's restrictions would go. Render and text don't write PDFs and don't check.
  - **Remove Metadata** (lopdf only): removes the trailer's `/Info` and the catalog's `/Metadata` stream (document level, as ENGINES says), keeps only the objects still reachable, and writes the whole file again with the same object numbers, without the source's `/XRefStm` and `/Prev` (their offsets mean nothing in the new file). Older revisions that held the old Info go too. A file lopdf can't read whole is "damaged or isn't a PDF" (no PDFium fallback: it can't edit metadata). The `done` line lists the keys that had a value: Title, Author, Subject, Keywords, Creator, Producer, CreationDate, ModDate, Other (any other key) and XMP, for the toast.
  - **Every operation:** `%PDF-` must be within the first 1,024 bytes (FORMAT_MATRIX rule 3), checked by the parent and again by the worker. At most 8 GiB is written per job.
    - **How PDFium reads:** pdfium-render can't open by path: `load_pdf_from_file` is `load_pdf_from_reader(File::open(..))`, through `FPDF_LoadCustomDocument`. Its read callback makes one `read` per block and takes any count above zero as the whole block, so the worker hands it a reader that fills each block or fails (PDFium never asks past the length it was given, so a short block means the file changed). It passes the length and offsets as `c_ulong`, 32 bits on Windows, so a source over 4 GiB − 1 there is refused first: "… is too large for Grape's PDF library to read." (`load_pdf_from_byte_vec` has the same cut, and would hold the whole file.)
    - **Encryption:** a PDF that needs a password to open gives "… is password-protected" everywhere. One with only an owner password (permission flags) still renders and gives its text through PDFium, which ignores its no-copy flag; the lopdf tools and the PDFium fallbacks of split and merge refuse it ("… is password-protected"), because a rewrite would drop its restrictions.
    - **Errors:** a file neither library opens gives "… is damaged or isn't a PDF", no pages gives "… has no pages", too many pages, too much text or too much output say so. A worker that crashes, or ends without a consistent result, gives "Grape's PDF helper stopped unexpectedly.", a timeout "… took too long, so Grape stopped." and the heap bound "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped." (all `DecodeFailed`; there's no internal-error kind yet). A missing or unloadable PDFium gives "Grape's PDF library is missing or couldn't be loaded." and a worker that won't start "Grape's PDF helper couldn't start." (both `NotYetSupported`).
- **Not handled yet** (each a follow-up):
  - Mark of the Web isn't copied to PDF outputs (D27's archive code does it).
  - Remove Metadata doesn't refuse signed PDFs (the rewrite breaks their signatures), keeps the trailer `/ID`, and leaves page-level XMP, image metadata, annotation authors and form values.
  - Split and merge drop `/OCProperties`, so optional content may show differently, and each split page carries all the shared resources it references.
  - The heap bound doesn't see PDFium's own allocations. On Windows, a Job Object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_PROCESS_MEMORY` and `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, assigned right after the spawn (the worker waits for its request on stdin, so there's no race), would bound the whole process; that's Win32 `unsafe` for grape-platform or the app, handed to the engine as a spawn hook. On macOS the parent could poll the worker's memory instead.
  - A worker whose parent dies keeps going until its job ends (its lines go nowhere) and leaves its temp folder (the Job Object's kill-on-close would end it on Windows).
- **Tests:** the unit tests always run and need no PDFium. A `pdf-it` feature (not `engines-it`, which needs FFmpeg) holds the tests that do: they fail, not skip, without `GRAPE_TEST_PDFIUM`, and run the `grape-pdf-worker` bin. CI's Rust jobs on `windows-latest` and `macos-latest` fetch PDFium only (cached under `pdfium-<OS>-` and the hashes of the lock and both scripts) and run `cargo test -p grape-engines --features pdf-it --locked`. Test binaries are linker-signed without the hardened runtime, so they never meet library validation.
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26: both fetch scripts' library, `.tgz` and `--only` paths (the `.sh` with stand-ins for `uname`, `lipo`, `codesign` and `arch`; the `.ps1` in PowerShell 7.6.6 with a stand-in `System32\tar.exe`): the real downloads, the hash checks, the stamp and its kept lines, a second run, `--force`, a changed or missing file, a wrong archive or file hash, a wrong size, a missing path, a library that isn't universal, a failed signature, and the new lock rules. The overlays merge into a config that passes the CLI 2.12.1 schema.
  - The engine's 72 PDF unit tests (the worker, cancel and timeout ones run the test binary itself as a fake worker, so they need no PDFium), and the 15 `pdf-it` tests against PDFium chromium/8086's Linux build (the first 11 also against 8100's): 300 DPI file and folder output, rotation, the huge-page cap, the page limit, TXT and MD, the no-text error, password refusal and the owner-only render, split falling back to PDFium, merge and metadata outputs opening in PDFium, cancel killing the worker in under 2 s, a taken folder name; and, after the review: an object-stream bomb stopped at the heap bound (256 MiB in the test), the split bound in the PDFium fallback, the fallbacks refusing an owner-password file PDFium opens, and text turned 90° and 180° in MD. Each of those four failed with its fix taken out.
  - The app's allocator reporting to the heap bound (a `main.rs` unit test).
  - The app's `--grape-pdf-worker` dispatch and PDFium lookup (unit tests), and a `removeMetadata` run through the real `grape` binary started that way. clippy is clean for Linux, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `aarch64-apple-darwin`.
  - Nothing has run on the PC or a Mac.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** the worker started from the installed app (no console window, quits with Grape), `pdfium.dll` loading from the install folder, and Defender's view of it; on the Mac, `libpdfium.dylib` loading from `Contents/Frameworks` in the ad-hoc DMG on Apple silicon and Intel, Gatekeeper on first open, and the worker's process name in Activity Monitor. A `Grape --grape-pdf-worker` self-test in the DMG job would catch a refused dylib before Maxwell does (follow-up).
- **Revisit if:** chromium/8100 passes D9 (today, 20:25Z), pdfium-render moves past `pdfium_7881`, lopdf 0.46 (2026-11-04) or inout 0.2.5 (2026-11-02) pass, Developer ID arrives (M9: hardened runtime back on), Grape ships for Windows ARM64 (`pdfium-win-arm64.tgz`), forms have to survive split and merge (rebuild AcroForm, or qpdf as a v2 sidecar), or the worker needs a real sandbox.
- *2026-10-28 (lane A): the worker is contained.* `pdf/run.rs` starts it on `helper.rs` (`Launch::pdf_worker`, `helper::start`, `Running::poll`), the image helper's and the SVG worker's plumbing, and keeps its own watch: pages stretch the deadline, progress never goes backwards, `done` and `error` must agree with the exit, and exit 86 is the heap bound. What changed:
  - On Windows it starts with `DETACHED_PROCESS`, not `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` ("How it runs" above): a console-subsystem worker (a debug `Grape.exe`, the `grape-pdf-worker` test bin) would get a hidden console, whose host is a second process in a one-process job.
  - Its current folder is the OS temp folder, not Grape's. It opens everything by absolute path and writes only into the job's temp folder, which isn't its current folder: in a deep folder of the user's, that path can be longer than Windows takes for a new process's current folder.
  - The app's containment hook (D33) puts it in a Job Object of its own before its request is written (the worker waits for it, so nothing runs uncontained): **6 GiB** of committed memory (`Limits::worker_memory`), ended when its job ends or Grape exits or crashes, one active process, no error dialogs, and the UI limits unless Grape itself runs in a job. It fails closed: a worker that can't be contained is killed, and the job says "Grape's PDF helper couldn't start.", the words for one that can't start; the hook's reason is only in the detail.
  - **Why 6 GiB:** the heap bound's 4 GiB, which counts only Rust's heap, plus 2 GiB for PDFium's own `malloc`. `render.rs`'s largest planned block is PDFium's BGRA bitmap of a page, 400 MB at the 100 MP bound, alive beside Grape's 400 MB copy of it (about 8 bytes a pixel at the peak); the rest of PDFium's share is decoded images, fonts and page objects, which nothing bounds but the cap. On Linux a 100 MP page to PNG peaked at about 773 MiB resident (JPEG no higher); CI's Windows job prints the Job Object's peak for the same page.
  - **Needs too much memory:** a worker that crashes or ends without a consistent result, or reports a damaged page or an internal failure, after committing 90% of the cap says "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped.", the words the heap bound already had. PDFium reports a bitmap or a text page it couldn't allocate as a page it couldn't read, which the worker calls damaged. Every other failure keeps its own words.
  - In "Not handled yet" above: the heap bound not seeing PDFium's allocations is handled on Windows by the cap (macOS still has only the heap bound). A worker whose parent dies now ends at once on Windows; its temp folder is still left, and on macOS it still runs to the end of its job.
  - **Tests:** unit tests with a fake hook: it's called once, with the 6 GiB cap, and before the request (the fake worker looks for the hook's mark in its folder, which the hook writes after a pause); a refusing hook kills the worker at once (it would write a file 1.5 s later) and leaves nothing; the 90% rule after a crash, a lie and a damaged page, and not after a password-protected file or a success. `tests/pdf.rs` on Windows contains every worker as the app does (each job that succeeds was contained once), renders a 100 MP page under the cap and prints its peak, and, outside cargo with `GRAPE_TEST_JOB_UI=1` (a CI step of its own), starts a child that breaks away from the runner's job and converts a PDF set in Arial, not embedded, to PNG and TXT and splits it, each worker in a job with the UI limits; its page 1 must be the same pixels a worker in no job draws, since PDFium's built-in stand-in for a font it can't get from Windows draws other ones. GitHub's runner allows no breakaway, so there that step only warns, and the PC checks the UI-limited job (`TESTING.md` → "PDF, M3/M4").

## D31: Tool panels (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** Compress, Audio Channels, Normalize Volume and Merge PDFs open a small panel before they run. Remove Metadata and the other v1 tools run at once (grape-core marks Remove Metadata `panel = Y`; the app's `panel::model::has_panel` holds the v1 rule until grape-core has one). A tool this build can't run opens no panel: `jobs::check_runnable` is the one check, for the panel route and the direct path, so wiring an engine changes only it. Merge PDFs' panel opens when PDFium is found (D35); the other three wait for lane B's FFmpeg tools (D26). `GRAPE_PREVIEW_PANELS=1` still opens them for QA, and their Run then shows "Not in this build yet / Compress on JPG is coming."
- **Window:** one `panel` window, declared hidden in `tauri.conf.json` (transparent, undecorated, always on top, skip taskbar, focusable, not focused, `acceptFirstMouse`, the shared browser args), hardened as a tool window that a click activates (D21's shelf bits), refusing drops (D13). One panel at a time: a new request replaces the open one, which counts as cancelled. 352 logical px wide, 224 to 430 tall; beside the wheel's spot (right of it, or left near the right edge), vertically centered on it, inside the work area.
- **IPC:** the page gets a request seq, item ids (1..n in drop order) and display names, never paths; `panel_current`, `panel_run({seq, order, choice})` and `panel_cancel({seq})` are granted only to `panel`. Rust ignores a stale seq and a second run, rejects an order that isn't each id once, a choice for another tool and an out-of-range target, and builds the `Operation` with `ToolParams`. It refuses a `panel_cancel` while a mouse button is down (that Esc belongs to a drag), and the page then takes input again. Merge reorders the paths before planning, so the new first file names the PDF and picks its folder (D29's note).
- **Focus, Windows:** shown with `SetWindowPos(HWND_TOPMOST, SWP_NOACTIVATE | SWP_SHOWWINDOW)`; never Tauri's `show` or `set_focus` (tao falls back to a synthetic Alt press, which is Grape's Actions key and opens Explorer's menus), never `SetForegroundWindow` on the panel, `AttachThreadInput`, synthetic keys or a global hotkey. A drop target doesn't receive "the last input event", so Windows wouldn't let Grape take the foreground anyway. The first click activates it; Enter and Esc are then the page's. Until then Explorer keeps the keyboard (Enter there opens the files), so the panel shows "Click here to use the keyboard." A "grape-panel-esc" thread polls Esc every 30 ms while a panel is open and closes it on a press made with no mouse button down in that sample or the one before (OLE ends a drag on the Esc key-down, so the button can come up before the next sample), never consuming the key, and only until the panel's window first has the focus: after that Esc is the page's, and an Esc pressed in another app (a rename in Explorer, a menu) is that app's, which keeps the "blur keeps it open" promise below. On close, an active panel hands the foreground back to the window that was in front when it showed.
- **Focus, macOS:** `orderFrontRegardless`, `makeKeyWindow`, then Grape's own `activate()`; if macOS declines, the first click activates and reaches the control (`acceptFirstMouse`). Esc and Enter work only while the panel is key (D7); an Esc with a mouse button down is refused (`NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons`), since a file dragged from a Finder window behind the key panel may leave Grape active and send that drag's Esc to the panel. On close, `hand_back` to the app that was in front, if the panel was key. Level 99, under the toast (100) and the overlay (101).
- **Blur keeps it open:** on Windows it starts unfocused, so a cancel on blur would mean nothing until the first click and then behave differently; and a glance at Explorer or Finder must not lose a merge order. It closes on Run, Cancel, ×, Esc, Alt+F4, a new panel, Pause or Quit.
- **The next drag:** the panel stays open, and the wheel opens over it. On Windows `raise_topmost` lifts the overlay to the top of the topmost band on every show (which also covers the float shelf); on macOS the levels do it.
- **Contents:** Compress is a segmented control, not a slider (three named choices, all visible, one click, native radio keys), remembered in `tools.compress` (default `balanced`). Normalize is −24…−10 LUFS in steps of 1, −16 by default, remembered in `tools.normalize_lufs`. Both are saved only when a Run is accepted. Audio Channels starts at Mono every time. Merge is a listbox with `aria-activedescendant`: pointer drag (HTML5 drag and drop can't work, D13), Alt+↑/↓, Move up/down buttons, and the output's name.
- **Tested:** `cargo test` on Linux (the model, placement, sizes, the check, settings, the fixtures both ways), clippy for Windows and both Macs, and the UI's Vitest suite (the guards, the model, the page on the fake DOM). The Windows panel functions' tests run on CI's `windows-latest` runner. Nothing has run on the PC or a Mac.
- **Needs the PC** (`TESTING.md` → "Tool panels, M4"): whether the first click reaches the page (the press lands in WebView2's child window, of another process); the hand-back; the Esc poll; the overlay over an open panel; two monitors at different DPI. **Needs a Mac:** whether activation is granted right after a drop; the first click; the hand-back; where Esc goes during a drag from a Finder window behind a key panel.
- **Revisit if:** a plain `SetForegroundWindow` after a drop turns out to work (the panel could then take the keyboard at once); the first click doesn't reach the page; the overlay ever opens under the panel (then hide the panel while a wheel is open); the Esc poll misfires; or users want the panel to move.
- **Amended 2026-10-28 (#38, with D26):**
  - *The rule moves to grape-core.* `ToolKey::has_v1_panel()` replaces the app's `panel::model::has_panel`, and `ToolParams::LUFS_MIN`, `LUFS_MAX`, `LUFS_STEP` and `lufs_ok` replace the app's own LUFS constants: in the Normalize panel's spec, in `panel_run`'s check and in loading `tools.normalize_lufs`. The range, the step and every message stay the same (a test builds the settings warning's range from the constants).
  - *Lane B's tools run (D26),* so Compress (JPG and PNG with no sidecar; MP3, M4A, WAV, FLAC, MP4, MOV and MKV with FFmpeg), Audio Channels and Normalize Volume (MP3, M4A, WAV and FLAC) open their panels in a normal build. `GRAPE_PREVIEW_PANELS=1` matters only where a panel's tool can't run: Compress on WebP and HEIC until lane B's D28 runs them, and wherever a helper is missing (Compress on MP3s without FFmpeg).
  - *Multi-OK.* `panel::route_for` also checks that the wheel offers the tool for the drop (`model::offered`: `grape_core::wheel_content` with `settings::planning_features()`, the features `plan` uses), so a panel never opens for a run `plan` would refuse as `NotOffered`: Audio Channels on two files (it isn't multi-OK), Merge PDFs on one, Compress beside a PDF (v2 there). The preview keeps that rule and skips only the runnable check. A drop from the wheel always passes; the check keeps the route and the plan from disagreeing.
  - *Compress's notes.* Small is "Smallest file. Large JPEGs and videos are scaled down." ("Some quality is lost." isn't true for PNG, lossless at every preset, nor for a 16-bit FLAC at 48 kHz or less; and Small never scales a PNG, so the line names JPEGs, not photos), Balanced "Smaller, and hard to tell apart." ("Much smaller" isn't true for PNG or for FLAC already at 24 bits and 48 kHz or less, which may save only the 5 % D26 requires), and High stays "Best quality. Saves the least space.". Small's line is 313.7 px in Inter at 12 px, wider than the note's 312, so Compress's note takes up to two lines (`panel.css`) and its window is 18 px taller: 242 logical px (`COMPRESS_BODY`, 118). Audio Channels stays 224, so "224 to 430 tall" holds.
  - *Tested* on Linux, 2026-10-28: `cargo test -p grape` (289), clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux, Windows and Apple silicon, and the UI's Vitest suite (1082). Not on the PC or a Mac: `TESTING.md` → "Tool panels, M4".

## D35: Archives and PDF in the dispatch; job notes; the not-runnable toast (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `run_job` runs the archive engine (D27) and the PDF engine (D29), and the app sends them drops. In this build that's 37 of the 178 v1 pairs (20 raster, 4 subtitle, 6 among ZIP, TAR and GZIP, 3 from RAR to those, and PDF → JPG, PNG, TXT and MD), plus Extract Archive (ZIP, TAR, GZIP, RAR), Split PDF, Merge PDFs and Remove Metadata on PDFs. Lane B owns `lib.rs` and OK'd every change there (2026-10-26).
- **Dispatch** (`grape-engines`' `lib.rs`, lane B's shapes):
  - `supports_with(op, formats, sidecars)` is true when `formats` isn't empty and every input has an engine for `op`. `engine_for(from, to, sidecars)` picks a conversion's engine and `tool_engine(tool, from, sidecars)` a tool's. `supports(op, from)` stays, as `supports_with` with no sidecars, so it's true for the six ZIP/TAR/GZIP pairs and Extract Archive on them, and false for RAR and PDF.
  - RAR needs `SidecarPaths::bsdtar`; PDF needs both `pdfium` and `pdf_worker`. Without them `run_job` returns `NotYetSupported` before anything is read.
  - `run_job` hands one input with a file output to `engine_for`'s engine; PDF → JPG or PNG (one page writes the file, more the folder) to `pdf::run`; Extract Archive (a folder) to the archive engine; Split PDF, Merge PDFs (every input) and Remove Metadata to `pdf::run`, which checks the job's shape. The engine's own "not supported" words are unchanged; the app's check speaks first.
  - `#[allow(dead_code)]` is gone from `mod archive` and `mod pdf`.
- **Job notes:** `JobOutcome::notes: Vec<Note>` carries facts, never words, names, paths or file contents. `Note` is `LeftOut(LeftOut)` (links, special files, macOS metadata and RAR5 file copies left out of an archive), `PagesScaled(n)` (pages drawn below 300 DPI) or `MetadataRemoved(Vec<MetaField>)` (sorted, without repeats, empty when the file had none). Notes come in the order the engine found them, at most one `MetadataRemoved` per job. `Note` and `MetaField` are `#[non_exhaustive]`, so lane B can add fields (GPS, camera, …) and notes without breaking the app. `JobOutcome` derives `Default`. Lane B builds its own Remove Metadata report on the same field.
- **The done toast** words the notes in its title, since the detail starts with the output's name and is cut at about 40 characters:
  - links, special files and RAR5 file copies, added up over the drop's jobs: "1 link left out", "2 special files left out", "1 repeated file left out", or "3 items left out" for more than one kind;
  - "1 page drawn below 300 DPI" / "N pages drawn below 300 DPI";
  - Remove Metadata: "Removed author", "Removed author and title", "Removed author, title and 3 more" (author, title, subject, keywords, creator, producer, dates, XMP, other fields, in that order; past 32 characters, one name and a count, "Removed keywords and 4 more", so the title fits its 246 px; 33 and 248 until the 2026-10-28 amendment below), or "No document metadata found" (page-level XMP and the images' own metadata stay, ENGINES.md → Metadata removal, so it never says the file has none); over several jobs, the union.
  - An error's words (the engine's `user_message`, often long for a PDF or an archive: "scan.pdf has no text to extract — it may be a scan.") take up to two lines, the title when it has no detail or the detail under "{ok} of {n} done", so the reason isn't cut off (`toast.css`; the window stays 320×64).
  - macOS metadata (`__MACOSX/`, `._`) is only logged: it's Finder's bookkeeping, never the user's content, and a zip made on a Mac would otherwise report dozens of items left out.
  - A fallback that every output took ("Saved in Downloads\Grape") or a failure ("{ok} of {n} done", the error) keeps its title, and the notes are only logged. When only some outputs fell back, the detail says how many ("2 folders; 1 in Downloads\Grape") and the notes keep the title. The log has counts and kinds, never a name or path.
  - Several outputs are counted as "files", "folders" (Extract, Split) or "items" (mixed, or PDF → JPG/PNG, which makes a file for one page and a folder for more), in "Making 2 folders…" and in the done detail. One PDF → JPG/PNG job is "Making doc…" while it runs.
- **A drop this build can't run** says "Not in this build yet" with "{FROM} to {TO} is coming." or "{Tool} on {FROM} is coming." (the app's check runs first, so the engine's "Tools arrive in a later build." no longer reaches a toast). The item named is the first that couldn't run even with every shipped helper present, so a missing PDFium never blames the PDF in a PDF + PNG drop.
  - **A missing helper** that the bundles carry, and that is the only reason, says "Grape's PDF library is missing" / "Reinstalling Grape brings it back." instead. That's PDFium on Windows and macOS now (every CI installer and DMG carries it), and bsdtar once the bundles do (D27; until then a RAR drop says "RAR to ZIP is coming.", which is true). Each helper has its own title and detail, so the HEIC helper can add its row. When no one missing helper is enough but all the shipped ones are (a PDF and a HEIC with PDFium and grape-heif both missing), it says "Some of Grape's helpers are missing" / "Reinstalling Grape brings them back." (added with D33's review fixes; before, that drop said "PDF to PNG is coming."). It's an info toast like the rest, and the probe is the same `supports_with`, so it can't disagree with the engines. A `tauri dev` run without the PDFium overlay shows it too, unless an earlier run with the overlay left PDFium in `target` (PLATFORM_WINDOWS, PLATFORM_MACOS).
- **The app:** `sidecars.rs` folds PDFium and the worker into `SidecarPaths` (`PdfSidecars` is gone) and finds `bsdtar` next to Grape's executable on every OS, like FFmpeg, never on `PATH`; the log names it. `SidecarPaths::heif` stays `None` until the HEIC build wires it. `jobs::check_runnable(op, items, sidecars)` is the one check, for the direct path and for `panel::route_for`, so Merge PDFs' panel opens in a normal run when PDFium is found. Compress, Audio Channels and Normalize Volume still wait for lane B's FFmpeg tools (D26) (`GRAPE_PREVIEW_PANELS=1` opens them for QA).
- **Tests:**
  - `tests/dispatch.rs` (always): exactly 37 of every format pair run with all the sidecars, each one a v1 pair of grape-core's; exactly 7 tool and format pairs, each one grape-core offers; no PDF pair or tool without `pdfium` or without `pdf_worker`; and the 30 pairs `supports` allows with no sidecars.
  - `tests/archive.rs` (always): the six pairs and Extract Archive through `run_job` with no sidecars, the left-out counts from a conversion and from an extract, and RAR refused without bsdtar, writing nothing.
  - `tests/pdf.rs` (`pdf-it`, with PDFium and the worker): every PDF job through `run_job`, Remove Metadata's note, and no PDF job without either sidecar. `archive::rar::it` (`rar-it`, with bsdtar): RAR → ZIP, TAR and GZIP and Extract through `run_job`, each counting a RAR5 file copy.
  - Unit tests of both `into_outcome`s, and the app's: `check_runnable` with and without each helper, the toasts' wording and precedence, bsdtar found and `heif` left `None`, and only Merge PDFs opening its panel.
  - Every new test that runs a job checks that its sources are unchanged and nothing is left behind. No test can see that `submit_op` and `route_for` hand in the sidecars Grape found (that needs an `AppHandle`); the QA items for the Merge panel and a renamed `pdfium.dll` cover it.
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26, again after the review fixes: `cargo test --workspace` (the app's 262 unit tests; `grape-engines`' 336, `archive` 4, `dispatch` 3, `image` 31 and `subtitle` 23), `pdf-it` with PDFium chromium/8086 for Linux (`tests/pdf.rs` 17), `rar-it` with a Linux bsdtar (`archive::rar`, 64), and clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux (also with `pdf-it` and `rar-it`), Windows and Apple silicon. The app tests' Windows and macOS branches (the PDF library missing, "Remove Metadata on PNG is coming.") first run in CI's Windows and macOS jobs. The two-line error toast was rendered in Chromium on Linux (Inter), light and dark; Segoe UI and SF Pro are the QA items'.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "Archives, M3/M4" and "PDF, M3/M4". Nothing has run on either yet; on a Mac, the PDF items are also the first run of `libpdfium.dylib` from `Contents/Frameworks` (D29).
- **Revisit if:** the bundles carry bsdtar (turn on its `ships`, and the RAR missing-helper toast with it); FFmpeg ships (it can get a helper row too); a drop with more than one kind of note turns out to be common (the title shows only the first: left out, then scaled pages, then metadata).
- **Amended 2026-10-26 (#27):**
  - *The counts.* "37 of the 178" and the dispatch test's "exactly 37" counted the pairs that ran when this was decided (raster, subtitles, archives and PDF). Lane B's FFmpeg engine (D22, the 104 audio and video pairs) and HEIC (D33, 10) joined the same dispatch since, so with every helper there 37 + 104 + 10 = **151** of the 178 v1 pairs run. `tests/dispatch.rs` counts 37 + 104 with every sidecar but the HEIC helper; `heif.rs`'s tests count HEIC's 10.
  - *bsdtar and FFmpeg ship.* Both revisit items above happened: CI builds Grape's own FFmpeg (D24), and every installer and DMG carries it and Grape's bsdtar (D25's and D27's updates). So `jobs::HELPERS` turns on bsdtar's `ships` on Windows and macOS ("Grape's RAR helper is missing" / "Reinstalling Grape brings it back."), and adds FFmpeg's row there: "Grape's media converter is missing" / "Reinstalling Grape brings it back.", when `ffmpeg` or `ffprobe` (or both) is missing and that's the only reason, for MP4 → MOV say. The title is 34 characters, 221 px in the title's 13 px semibold (Inter), so it fits the toast's 248 px. Linux ships neither, so there those drops stay "RAR to ZIP is coming." and "MP4 to MOV is coming.". The app's tests check each case on every OS (`runnable_with` with the row shipped and not) and the real rows per OS; their `installed()` now has FFmpeg and ffprobe too, and Compress, Audio Channels and Normalize Volume still open no panel with it, since they wait for lane B's FFmpeg tools (D26).
- **Amended 2026-10-28 (#38, with D26):**
  - *Remove Metadata's words* for D26's ten new fields, one per `MetaField` in its order: location, camera, author, title, subject, keywords, comments, copyright, creator, producer, software, dates (both dates), XMP, IPTC, thumbnail, cover art, chapters, embedded media, and other fields (also any field lane B adds before it gets a word). The rule is now 32 characters: the title has 246 px, not 248 (320 − 8 for the pill's inset, 2 for its border, which the old sum left out, 42 for its padding and 22 for the dot and its gap), and "Removed camera and embedded media" (33) is 247.9 px in Inter's 13 px semibold, so it reads "Removed camera and 1 more". A photo's usual "Removed location, camera and 3 more" is 35 characters, so it reads "Removed location and 4 more". The widest title left is the fallback "Removed embedded media and 1 more" (33 characters, which only a fallback reaches), 241.8 px (the widest was 231 px before D26); Segoe UI and SF Pro are QA items (`TESTING.md` → "Image tools, M4"). With none: "No metadata found" for images and media, "No document metadata found" whenever the drop has a PDF.
  - *Compress* (`Note::Compressed { before, after }`) titles the done toast "62% smaller": the sizes are summed over the drop's jobs (in `u128`) and the saving rounded half up to a whole percent. Nothing smaller, or less than half a percent, makes no claim ("Done"), and it never says 100 % while a byte is left. The titles' order: left out, scaled pages, metadata, size. The log has the two sizes in bytes.
  - *Nothing to change* (`ErrorKind::NoChange`, D26) is no failure. A drop of one such job is an info toast, "Nothing to change", with the engine's sentence as its detail ("song.mp3 is already compact. Try Small."); several, all such, "3 files already compact"; beside written outputs a done toast, "6 files; 2 already compact" (after "1 in Downloads\Grape" when some fell back, and instead of "their folders are read-only" when all did); beside a real failure it counts as done in "{ok} of {n} done". The words come from the drop's tool and params, never from the sentence: "already compact" (Compress), "already mono" or "already stereo" (Audio Channels), "had no sound" (Remove Audio), "unchanged" (Normalize Volume, which ends so both at its target and when a file is too quiet, and anything else). An info toast's detail now takes two lines, like an error's (`toast.css`), so the sentence's reason after a name of up to about 40 characters isn't cut off (a longer one still ends in "…"), and so does a done toast's, so "IMG_20261028_101530 Compressed.jpg; 1 already compact" (336 px in Inter's 12 px) shows its count. The log says "nothing to change" with the kind and the input count, never the detail.
  - *Tested* with D31's amendment above (the app's tests: the word table in the enum's order, every set of fields within 32 characters with two names and 33 with one, the PDF and non-PDF empties, the percent's rounding, sums and edges, and the three NoChange tones with fallbacks and failures).
- **Amended 2026-10-28 (D39):** two more notes. `Note::ImagesLeftOut(n)` is the references an SVG left out (linked images never read, embedded data over its budget): "1 image left out" / "3 images left out". `Note::DrawnSmaller` is an SVG drawn smaller than its size to fit an image target: "Drawn smaller to fit". `Note::PagesScaled` also counts SVG pages in a PDF drawn below 300 DPI. The titles' order is now: an archive's left-out, images left out, scaled pages, drawn smaller, metadata, size. The log adds `; 3 image(s) left out` and `; drawn smaller`.

## D33: HEIC (2026-10-26, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** HEIC ⇄ JPG, PNG, WEBP, TIFF and BMP (10 pairs) convert through a helper process on both OSes, speaking one protocol (`crates/grape-heif-proto`). Windows runs `grape-heif.exe`, Grape's own sidecar built on libheif. macOS runs `Grape --grape-heif-worker`, which uses ImageIO through `grape-platform`. The parent side is `grape-engines/src/heif.rs` (with `helper.rs`), lane A's modules by agreement with lane B (as D27 and D29 were). It replaces ENGINES.md's "libheif + libde265 (LGPL DLLs) to decode; `heif-enc` sidecar to encode", and D25's heif-enc pin that never happened. Entered after D35 because this log is append-only; the number was taken first.
- **Why a helper process on both OSes:** libheif had 37 CVEs in the last two years and six security releases from 2026-08-25 to 2026-10-26 (its own `SECURITY.md` in 1.23.7 counts 89 advisories and eleven security-driven releases from January to October 2026); libde265 had about 12. ImageIO had an exploit used in the wild (CVE-2025-43300). Neither decoder can be cancelled partway through. A crash in Grape's own process would take the tray and the drag hooks with it (golden rule 4). It's the same reasoning as D29. It's not a sandbox: the helper has the user's rights.
- **Protocol** (version 1): one JSON request on stdin, `{protocol, workDir, op, limits}`, where `op` is `decode {source}`, `encode {quality?}` or `probe`. JSON lines on stdout: `image` (size, channels and ICC size, before any pixel is written: libheif sends it before decoding, ImageIO right after its own decode, which the worker first checks against the decoder budget), `progress`, then exactly one `done` (exit 0) or `error {kind, detail}` (exit 1). Pixels go through files in a fresh, private `grape-heif-…` folder (0700 on Unix) under the OS temp folder: `pixels.rgba` (decode output), `input.rgba` (encode input) and `out.heic` (encode output). Each is a 24-byte header, the ICC profile, then 8-bit RGB or straight-alpha RGBA rows. No message holds a path, a name or file contents. Both sides refuse unknown fields.
- **Windows: `grape-heif.exe`** (`tools/grape-heif`, a Rust crate outside the workspace with its own `Cargo.lock`): libheif-rs 3.0.0 and libheif-sys 5.3.1+1.23.1 (MIT), on libheif **1.23.7** (2026-10-26, a security release, taken under D9's override), libde265 **1.1.3** (2026-09-14) and x265 **4.3** (tag 2026-07-31, commit `e9b88125dc21393b3fd8d68e98083bdfb89778a8`).
  - They're built by vcpkg at commit `e456309491fd875487bf02b8a0dca76c1b1b00cc` (2026-10-10, the last master commit before D9's 2026-10-12 cutoff), triplet `x64-windows-static` (static CRT), as `libheif[core,hevc]`. At that commit libde265 is a hard dependency of the port, not a feature.
  - An overlay port takes that commit's libheif 1.23.5 recipe to 1.23.7. It fetches the release asset with `vcpkg_download_distfile` (SHA-256 `f7483e81…2475`, SHA-512 `953f2a59…e62d`), so the archive that is built is the one in the source bundle, and sets `-DENABLE_PLUGIN_LOADING=OFF`: with loading on, libheif scans its compiled-in plugin folder even without `LIBHEIF_PLUGIN_PATH`. x265 comes from vcpkg's archive of tag 4.3 (SHA-512 `270d0db1…ed75`; no release tarball exists), and libde265 from vcpkg's archive of `v1.1.3` (SHA-512 `58eabdac…4471`).
  - The binary is **GPL-3.0-or-later** as a whole. It's allowed as a separate executable (golden rule 6, ENGINES rule 2). Its texts are in `licenses/grape-heif/`, and every CI run and release carries its source bundle.
  - It prints an FFmpeg-style banner for the bundling gate (D25): `grape-heif version 1.23.7-grape.1 …`. Its configuration line reports `plugins=disabled` from libheif itself.
- **macOS: ImageIO** through `objc2-image-io` 0.3.2 (2025-10-04, Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT), the only new package in `Cargo.lock`. Its calls are `unsafe`, so the code lives in `grape-platform` (`macos/heif.rs`). `main.rs` dispatches `--grape-heif-worker` before anything else starts, as it does `--grape-pdf-worker`; on Windows `Grape.exe --grape-heif-worker` exits 2 and never starts a second tray app.
  - Decode takes the primary image, upright, from `CGImageSourceCreateThumbnailAtIndex` (from the full image, with the transform, at full size), after checking its size from the image's properties, and that the decode fits the request's decoder budget (`max_decoder_memory`, 1 GiB, as libheif's on Windows; about 6 bytes a pixel at 8 bits and 11 above). The `image` line goes out after ImageIO's decode, the first moment the upright size is certain, and before Grape's bitmap is made; ImageIO's picture is let go once it's drawn. It's drawn into an 8-bit bitmap in the image's own RGB color space with that space's ICC profile (Display P3 for PQ or HLG; sRGB with no profile for anything else), un-premultiplied. `CGImageSourceSetAllowableTypes` limits the worker to HEIF types on 14.2+, through `dlsym` so Grape still launches on macOS 13.
  - Encode first checks `CGImageDestinationCopyTypeIdentifiers()` for `public.heic`. Macs without a hardware HEVC encoder, and GitHub's macOS VMs, get "This Mac can't write HEIC files.", and so does a `CGImageDestinationFinalize` that fails, which is how ImageIO fails on hardware that lists the type but can't encode it.
- **Engine** (`heif.rs`): it sniffs `ftyp` before starting anything.
  - A file named `.heic` that's really a JPEG, PNG and so on goes to the raster engine, `image::convert`, with that engine's own limits (FORMAT_MATRIX rule 3).
  - HEIF with another codec (AVIF, AVC, JPEG, VVC) is refused as "a kind of HEIF image Grape can't read yet", on both OSes, though ImageIO could read some of it.
  - *2026-10-26, after CI's first run:* a file whose `ftyp` has a structural major brand (`mif1`, `msf1`, `miaf`, `MiHE`) and no codec brand at all goes to the helper, which decodes it or says it isn't readable. libheif 1.23 writes a grid of HEVC tiles as `mif1` + `[mif1, miaf]` when it can't find its first tile's brand, so the grid fixture (and other libheif apps' grids) would otherwise be refused.
  - It reuses `image.rs`'s `decode`, `prepare`, `icc_fits`, `publish_pixels` and `pixels_fit` (lane B), and publishes with the no-clobber helpers. HEIC → raster holds one copy of the pixels, the buffer `pixels_fit` counted, and writes no EXIF, so nothing is rotated twice. Raster → HEIC streams the rows into `input.rgba` and copies `out.heic` into a temp file in the destination folder only after checking its size and that it's HEIC.
  - `SidecarPaths::heif` replaces `SidecarPaths::heif_enc`, which is gone. Without `heif`, the dispatch offers no HEIC pair; the app shows "Grape's HEIC helper is missing" / "Reinstall Grape to convert HEIC files." on Windows when that's the only reason (D35's helper rows).
  - **Containment** is a process-wide hook, `grape_engines::set_contain`, that the app sets once at startup (`contain.rs`), not an `EngineCtx` field, which would have broken lane B's struct literals and D16's API. On Windows it puts every helper into a Job Object of its own (`grape_platform::windows::job`) before the helper gets its request: 2 GiB of committed memory, killed when the job closes or Grape exits, one active process, no error dialogs, UI limits. When Grape itself runs inside a job (under cargo, so `pnpm tauri dev` and `cargo test`, or a launcher's job), the helper starts in that job too, and Windows nests a job only if neither sets UI limits, so the helper's job then has every limit but the UI ones (`JobGuard::has_ui_limits`; the log says so once). Breaking away isn't possible there: cargo's job doesn't allow it. A helper that can't be contained is killed and the job says the helper couldn't start (fail closed). The PDF worker and bsdtar can take the same hook later.
- **Limits:**
  - source file ≤ 512 MiB, for HEIF content;
  - decoded image ≤ 134,217,728 pixels (512 MiB of RGBA8);
  - the conversion's working set ≤ 1 GiB (`pixels_fit`, checked as soon as the helper reports the size; the helper is killed if it doesn't fit);
  - libheif's own limits set to the same, with its total memory at 1 GiB, built from libheif's compiled-in defaults (`heif_get_global_security_limits`), so an inherited `LIBHEIF_SECURITY_LIMITS=off` can't switch the other limits off;
  - encode ≤ 35,651,584 pixels and ≤ 16,888 pixels a side (HEVC level 6.2's largest picture; Grape encodes one picture, not a grid), checked before the helper starts;
  - ICC ≤ 255 × 65,519 bytes;
  - timeouts of 120 s plus 1 s per started megapixel to decode, and 120 s plus 3 s per megapixel to encode; a cancel kills the helper within the 100 ms poll;
  - on Windows, a Job Object with 2 GiB of committed memory. A helper that ends without a result after committing 90% of that says "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped."
- **Output:** 8-bit only; the primary image only (like D16's first frame); no EXIF or XMP is carried; the ICC profile is kept when it's an RGB profile (HEIC input is RGBA8, so a gray source's gray profile is dropped and the HEIC reads as sRGB gray). A HEIC's `nclx`-only color (no ICC) is treated as sRGB on Windows.
- **Quality:** `quality` is absent for conversions, and each helper uses its own default.
  - Windows: libheif quality **50**, which is CRF 25 with x265's `slow` preset, `ssim` tune and 4:2:0. That's heif-enc's default, read in libheif's source.
  - macOS: ImageIO lossy quality **0.8**. Apple doesn't document ImageIO's own default.
  - An explicit quality (for later tools) is 1–100: libheif takes it as is, ImageIO as quality/100. The two scales aren't the same perceptually. Manual QA compares them, and M4's Compress presets retune them.
- **Isolation:**
  - Both: the helper's stdin and stdout are pipes and its stderr is discarded; its current folder is its work folder; `LIBHEIF_PLUGIN_PATH` and `LIBHEIF_SECURITY_LIMITS` (the only two variables libheif reads) are removed from the environment it inherits, which is otherwise kept, because the Mac worker is Grape with ImageIO, where a cleared environment is untested; a timeout and kill on cancel; the work folder removed on every path, and what a crash, a kill or a power cut leaves behind removed at Grape's next start (`grape_engines::remove_stale_work_dirs`, from `contain.rs`, off the main thread: every `grape-heif-{pid}-{nanos}-{n}` folder in the temp folder whose pid isn't Grape's own, which is safe because Grape runs once per user).
  - Windows: `DETACHED_PROCESS`, so the helper has no console at all and Windows starts no console host (`conhost.exe`), which would be a second process in a job that allows one (`CREATE_NO_WINDOW` still makes a hidden console); the Job Object above, assigned right after the spawn and before the request is written (the helper waits for its request, so there's no race); inside `grape-heif.exe`, `SetDefaultDllDirectories(LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32)` and `SetErrorMode` at start.
  - macOS: `CGImageSourceSetAllowableTypes(["public.heic","public.heif","public.heics"])` through `dlsym` on 14.2 and later, the pixel and decoder-budget checks before decoding, and the worker exits if Grape goes away (it watches its parent).
    - *2026-10-28, after CI's first ImageIO runs:* the restriction is set first thing in the worker (`ImageIo::restrict_process`, called by `main.rs` and the test bin before `worker_main`), while it has one thread, never inside the codec's calls. Set while another thread used ImageIO, it crashed the test process (SIGSEGV) on GitHub's macOS runners. The full-size thumbnail ImageIO draws has alpha even for an opaque HEIC, and ImageIO gives HEICs no `kCGImagePropertyHasAlpha`, so the worker draws first and sends 3 channels when every pixel drawn is opaque (the `image` line now follows the draw, still before any pixel is written). Its decoder budget counts Grape's bitmap too: 8 bytes a pixel at 8 bits, 12 above.
- **Bundling:** `tauri.heif.windows.conf.json` adds `binaries/grape-heif` to `bundle.externalBin` and `licenses/grape-heif/` to `bundle.resources`, and runs the gate as its `beforeBuildCommand`. The gate reads the merged overlays from `TAURI_CONFIG`. Overlays merge as RFC 7396, so arrays replace: no build passes two overlays that both set `externalBin`, and when FFmpeg ships, per-OS `tauri.sidecars.windows.conf.json` (ffmpeg, ffprobe, bsdtar, grape-heif) and `tauri.sidecars.macos.conf.json` (ffmpeg, ffprobe, bsdtar) replace both `tauri.sidecars.conf.json` and this overlay. CI's `grape-heif` workflow builds it; the installer job takes it from the same run, and the release attaches the GPL source bundle to the draft. **Done 2026-10-26 (#27):** `tauri.sidecars.windows.conf.json` now bundles grape-heif with FFmpeg and bsdtar, and replaced this overlay (D25's update).
- **Aliases:** `.heif` and `.hif` (Canon, Sony and Fujifilm cameras) should open as HEIC, and `.heics` shouldn't; that's grape-core's change (lane B), not in this one yet. *Done 2026-10-28 (lane B): `Format::from_extension` takes `heif` and `hif` as HEIC, and `heics` stays unknown.*
- **New dependencies:**
  - `objc2-image-io` 0.3.2 (grape-platform, macOS).
  - `libc` direct in grape-platform on macOS (already locked, 0.2.190).
  - `windows` 0.62.2 features `Win32_Security` and `Win32_System_JobObjects` (bindings only, no new package).
  - `grape-heif-proto` (Grape's own: serde and serde_json only), used by grape-engines, grape-platform, the app and `tools/grape-heif`.
  - `grape-platform` as a Windows-only dev-dependency of grape-engines (Grape's own; one `Cargo.lock` edge), so `tests/heif.rs` contains the helper with the app's Job Object.
  - In `tools/grape-heif/Cargo.lock` only: libheif-rs 3.0.0 (2026-08-18), libheif-sys 5.3.1+1.23.1 (2026-08-13), windows-sys 0.61.2, serde 1.0.229, serde_json 1.0.151, and their dependencies, each checked against D9. libheif-sys's build script needs system-deps 8 → cfg-expr → target-lexicon, which is `Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception`. It's a build tool, never in the exe, and is allowed by a per-crate exception in `tools/grape-heif/deny.toml`.
  - Native: libheif 1.23.7 and libde265 1.1.3 (LGPL-3.0-or-later per their headers; vcpkg's "-only" is metadata), x265 4.3 (GPL-2.0-or-later).
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-26 (nothing ran on Windows or a Mac; the cross-target checks were clippy only):
  - `grape-heif-proto`'s 56 unit tests: the exact JSON, unknown fields refused, the sniff table, the pixel file, and `serve` with a fake codec.
  - `grape-engines`' `heif` unit tests (26) with the test binary as a fake helper: all 10 pairs, alpha and 3 or 4 channels, the ICC profile both ways (a gray one dropped), the raster fallback and other HEIF with nothing started, the source and encode caps, an `image` line over a limit killing the helper, cancel within 2 s, timeouts, every failure kind's message, crashes, lies and spoiled files, noise on stdout, the `LIBHEIF_*` variables never arriving, containment before the request, a containment failure, the 90% memory rule, the dispatch with and without `heif`, and that the source is unchanged and nothing is left in the folder or the work folder. `helper.rs`' 4.
  - `tests/heif.rs` (`heif-it`) ran here against a stand-in helper (the same fake, with fake fixtures), which checks the harness, not libheif or ImageIO.
  - The app's tests (the worker flags, `grape-heif.exe` found, the J26 toast), clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux, Windows and Apple silicon (with `heif-it` too), `cargo deny`, the gate's tests and the notices.
- **CI runs:** `grape-heif` (build, the tool's own tests, the banner, no plugin loader, the DLL list, the license texts, the fixtures, the source bundle; its vcpkg and cargo caches are keyed on Visual Studio's version and the default MSVC toolset as well, and its exe artifact carries `licenses/grape-heif/`), `heif-it-windows` (`tests/heif.rs` with the exe it just built, contained as the app contains it, and the Job Object tests, also run outside cargo so the UI-limited job is tested too) and `heif-it-macos` (ImageIO decoding the Windows job's fixtures, and `tests/heif.rs` through `grape-heif-worker`). Mac encode is manual QA, because GitHub's macOS VMs can't encode HEVC.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "HEIC, D33".
- **Not handled yet** (follow-ups):
  - grid encoding over 35.6 MP (libheif-sys `heif_context_add_grid_image` and `add_image_tile`);
  - 10-bit or HDR output;
  - `nclx`-only P3 or BT.2020 HEICs on Windows (they need a synthesized ICC);
  - a memory cap on macOS (poll `proc_pid_rusage` from the same hook); until then the worker holds ImageIO's decode to the decoder budget from the image's properties;
  - HEIC → BMP from a Display P3 (or any non-sRGB) HEIC: BMP can't carry the profile, so `publish_pixels` drops it and the colors look duller. The fix is a conversion to sRGB first, in `image.rs` (lane B's) or through a color-management library (a new dependency, so a decision first);
  - HEIC content under a raster extension (`photo.jpg` that's really HEIC);
  - Mark of the Web on outputs;
  - the PDF worker and bsdtar moving onto `helper.rs` and the containment hook. *Done 2026-10-28 (lane A): the PDF worker starts on `helper.rs`; bsdtar keeps its own pump but goes through the same hook right after its spawn; both fail closed (D27's and D29's amendments).*
- **Revisit if:** libheif or libde265 ship a security release (bump under D9's override, which is likely given the pace; it has happened once already, 1.23.6 → 1.23.7 on 2026-10-26); the vcpkg pin's ports move; x265 4.4; Windows ARM64 ships; a Mac model fails the encode check while users expect HEIC; or Microsoft's HEIF extension becomes universal.
- *2026-10-28 (D41, AVIF):* the helper speaks **protocol 2**: an encode names its format (`heic` or `avif`), the probe's caps are per format, the encode's output is `out.heic` or `out.avif`, and the sniff tells AVIF from HEIC (D41 has the rules). `grape-heif.exe` is `1.23.7-grape.2`. The parts of this entry they change:
  - AVIF is no longer "HEIF with another codec": it decodes, whatever the name says (a `.heic` that's AVIF too). AVC, JPEG, JPEG 2000, VVC and uncompressed HEIF are still refused as "a kind of HEIF image Grape can't read yet". The fixture for that refusal is now `avci-brand.heic` (an AVC `ftyp`), which replaces `avif-brand.heic`, since protocol 2 sniffs that one as AVIF.
  - Every user message calls it **"Grape's image helper"**, not "HEIC helper", and the app's row is "Grape's image helper is missing" / "Reinstall Grape to convert HEIC and AVIF files.". *Amended by D28 (2026-10-28): the detail is "Reinstall Grape to work with HEIC and AVIF files.", since HEIC Compress needs the helper too.*
  - **The Mac worker now caps its own memory:** it arms the PDF worker's 2 GiB heap bound (`pdf::heap`, at `grape_heif_proto::WORKER_HEAP_BYTES`) for every job, so "a memory cap on macOS" above is done for Rust's heap (rav1e, Grape's buffers), and exit 86 is "… needs too much memory" on macOS. ImageIO's own `malloc` still isn't counted; the decoder budget bounds it. "It never arms the PDF worker's heap bound" no longer holds.
  - `CGImageSourceSetAllowableTypes` takes `public.avif` and `public.avis` too, and the encode limit per side is per format (16,888 for HEIC, 16,384 for AVIF).
- *2026-10-28 (D28, HEIC Compress):* lane B's Compress on HEIC runs the helper through `heif.rs`, at protocol 2. The protocol doesn't change: the probe, the encode's `quality`, `Limits::for_encode` and the `image` and `done` lines were all on the wire already.
  - **The probe:** `Encoder::check_encode` sends `probe {}` once per process for each helper path and keeps its caps; a probe that fails isn't kept, so the next job asks again. `caps.heic.encode` false is `noEncoder`'s words ("This Mac can't write HEIC files." on a Mac), before anything is decoded. On a Mac that answer only says whether ImageIO lists `public.heic` as a type it writes (`CGImageDestinationCopyTypeIdentifiers`): a Mac or VM that lists it but has no HEVC encoder passes the probe, decodes, and gets the same words from the encode, when `CGImageDestinationFinalize` fails. So D28's "told before anything is decoded" holds for every Mac whose ImageIO doesn't list the type, not for one that lists it and can't finish. A probe that tries a real encode would close that gap; it waits for a Mac that shows it (`TESTING.md` → "HEIC, D33" has the Intel and T2 item).
  - **The decode:** `Decoder::decode_within` is a conversion's decode, except that the `image` line is held to Compress's own peak (lane B's `image::ToolPeak`) plus the ICC bytes, within the 1 GiB working set, instead of `pixels_fit`. Over it, the helper is killed before any pixel is written, and the job says "… is too large to convert.".
  - **The encode:** `Encoder::encode` sends `encode {format: "heic", quality}` (1–100; the values per preset and per OS are D28's) with the pixels as they are (upright 8-bit RGB or RGBA, streamed into `input.rgba` with no RGBA copy), the ICC profile if it's an RGB one the helper keeps, and the encode deadline, and hands `out.heic` back as bytes, read back no larger than the 512 MiB source cap. It's the conversions' encode: `encode_pixels` runs the same steps (`stage`, then `run_encode`) with no quality, so there's one encode path.
  - **What lane B's metadata graft relies on in `out.heic`**, as both helpers write it today (libheif 1.23.7, and ImageIO): one file-level `meta` box; every item stored by construction method 0 (in the file) or 1 (in `idat`); every metadata item the helper writes itself (Exif, XMP or another `mime` item) is one the graft finds, so the source's replaces it and is never kept beside it. These describe today's helpers; nothing promises them, and a macOS update brings a new ImageIO with no Grape release. So the graft checks each one on every `out.heic`: when one doesn't hold, the job fails as an encode failure (`EncodeFailed`, "Grape couldn't write a HEIC file.") and nothing is published. A `grape-heif.exe` bump (libheif or one of its codecs) re-runs the graft's tests against the real helper (`heif-it`, which `heif-it-windows` runs whole) before it ships. CI's Mac VMs can't encode HEVC, so ImageIO's `out.heic` is checked by hand on each new macOS (`TESTING.md` → "HEIC, D33").
  - **Tests:** the fake helper in `heif.rs`'s tests now writes real HEIF (`ftyp heic`; `meta` with `hdlr pict`, `pitm` 1, `iinf` with one `infe` v2 of type `hvc1`, `iloc` v1 with one extent, `iprp` with an 8-bit 23-byte `hvcC` and `ispe`, and `ipma` 1 → [1, 2]; then `mdat` with the raw pixels; AVIF the same with `av01` and `av1C`). It reads the primary item through `pitm`, `iinf` and `iloc` (versions 0 to 2, `idat` too), so a file lane B rewrote around it still decodes, and records the quality it got (`heif::tests::fake_quality`). It answers a probe with none of its mode's misbehavior (`probe-<mode>` misbehaves on the probe only), so a test that calls `check_encode` first still reaches the decode or encode its mode is for. CI's `grape-heif` job writes four more fixtures: `exif-xmp.heic` (Exif as an iPhone stores it, with Orientation 6, GPS, Make, Model, ExifVersion, DateTimeOriginal and PixelXDimension and PixelYDimension of the stored 96 × 64, so the graft has dimensions to patch; XMP with `tiff:Orientation="6"`; an `irot` turning it 90° clockwise), `thumb.heic` (a thumbnail item), `nclx-p3.heic` (a `colr nclx` with primaries 12 and transfer 13, no ICC profile) and `q95.heic` (1600 × 1200 of fine detail at quality 95). Each is read back with libheif before it's written, must start with HEIC's own `ftyp`, and must decode through `grape-heif.exe` to its size.
  - **The rest of lane A's part:** the app's helper row says "Reinstall Grape to work with HEIC and AVIF files.", which answers a HEIC Compress drop too, and CI's installer, DMG and release builds stop before building when `TARGET_CPU` is set (D28: libwebp-sys's build script passes it to `-march`).
  - *Tested* on Linux, 2026-10-28 (nothing ran on Windows or a Mac): `heif`'s 49 unit tests (15 new: the encoder's RGB and RGBA rows as they are, the quality sent and read back, other color types and qualities refused before anything starts, its picture cap, an RGB profile kept and one past the cap or not RGB dropped, its failures read as a conversion's, the cap on the bytes read back, cancel; `check_encode` asking each helper once, keeping no failed probe, reading a failed probe as an encode's failure and saying `noEncoder`'s words from the kept caps; the fake's modes leaving the probe alone, so a probe first still reaches the encode's own output check and the decode's own peak; `decode_within` at its peak and one byte over; the fake's HEIF walked box by box and sniffing as HEIC and AVIF; a rewritten HEIC decoding through the fake), 659 library tests in all, every integration suite; the app's 293 + 7; clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux, Windows and Apple silicon, and `grape-engines` with `pdf-it,engines-it,heif-it` on Linux and Windows; `tools/grape-heif`'s clippy for Windows (types only: libheif doesn't link here), with the fixtures' Exif and XMP built apart and read back (Pillow found Make, Model, Orientation 6, ExifVersion 0232, DateTimeOriginal, PixelXDimension 96, PixelYDimension 64 and the GPS IFD; the program's own check of the stored Exif block passed on a stand-in block and failed on a changed one; the XMP parses); actionlint; the changed PowerShell parsed with pwsh 7, and the fixture check and the `TARGET_CPU` guards run against stand-ins; both `cargo deny` runs. **CI must prove:** that libheif 1.23.7 writes the four fixtures as the program checks them (the `nclx` box kept with no ICC beside it, a thumbnail encoded and assigned, one Exif and one XMP block, the Exif stored as given behind its offset of 6), that each decodes through `grape-heif.exe` (`exif-xmp.heic` as 64 × 96), that the `heif-it` jobs stay green, and, once lane B lands HEIC Compress, its graft tests against `grape-heif.exe`'s `out.heic`.

- *2026-10-28 (lane A): the hook contains the PDF worker and bsdtar too.* "The PDF worker and bsdtar can take the same hook later" above is done: on Windows each runs in a Job Object of its own, the PDF worker at 6 GiB and bsdtar at 1 GiB (D29's and D27's amendments), with this entry's limits, and both fail closed like the image helper and the SVG worker. The 90% rule is one function now, `helper::near_memory_cap`, which `heif.rs`, the SVG worker, the PDF worker and bsdtar share, and `helper.rs`'s `DETACHED_PROCESS` starts all four.

## D37: Images to PDF and Create PDF (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `grape-engines` converts JPG, PNG, WebP, TIFF and BMP to PDF in Grape's own process, and HEIC to PDF with the HEIC decoded by Grape's HEIC helper, exactly as HEIC → JPG decodes it (D33). That's 6 of the 8 image → PDF pairs. SVG and AVIF join through the same page writer when their decoders land. **Create PDF** runs too: one page per input, in the job's input order (the selection's), mixed formats in one file, named `{first stem} Combined.pdf` by grape-core. It takes the images FORMAT_MATRIX gives Create PDF that this build reads, JPG, PNG, WebP and TIFF always and HEIC with the helper, and runs when every input is one of them. BMP converts to PDF, but FORMAT_MATRIX gives it no tools, so the wheel never offers Create PDF with a BMP and the engine doesn't take one either. The code is `src/pdf/create.rs` and the helpers next to it (`writer.rs`, `jpeg.rs`, `flate.rs`), lane A's module by agreement with lane B (2026-10-26), as D29 was. No lane B source file changes: `image.rs`'s `decode` and `icc_fits` (and, through `heif.rs`, `pixels_fit`) are called as they are. Two of lane B's tests change only because these pairs now run: `tests/dispatch.rs`'s counts, and the "PNG to PDF isn't in this build yet." row of `tests/image.rs`, which is removed.
- **Why in Grape's own process:** no PDF is read, so there's nothing for PDFium or the PDF worker to do. The only untrusted input is the images. The raster engine already decodes them in this process under D16's limits, and HEIC is decoded in its helper process (D33). So images → PDF adds no decoder to Grape's process.
- **Why Grape's own writer:** lopdf's `Document` holds every object in memory until it saves, so a 50-photo PDF would hold all 50 photos at once. printpdf would be a new dependency for what a short writer does. Grape writes the file itself, one page at a time, into a temp file. No new crate: `flate2` (`rust_backend`) and `image`'s `JpegEncoder` are already dependencies. lopdf stays for reading PDFs (D29) and in the tests, which parse every output with it.
- **The file:**
  - PDF 1.4: a catalog, the page tree (written last), and for each page its image (with its ICC profile and soft mask, if any; or its strips, below), a tiny content stream and the page. A classic cross-reference table and trailer.
  - No Info dictionary, no XMP, no dates, no Producer and no `/ID`. Nothing in the output names the source, its folder, the user or the time.
  - It's written into a `.grape-tmp-….pdf` in the destination folder, synced, then published no-clobber (`publish::finalize`: a name taken meanwhile gets the next free name and `renamed`), like every engine's output. On an error or a cancel the temp file is removed.
- **Page size:** one pixel is one point (72 dpi), and the page is the upright image's size. If the longer side is over 14,400 pt (200 inches, Acrobat's limit), the page is scaled down evenly so that side is 14,400 pt. The pixels are never resampled. The shorter side is at least 1 pt. The DPI stored in the image is ignored, so a 300 dpi Letter scan (2550 × 3300 px) makes a page of about 35 × 46 inches. No image object in the file has a side over 65,535 px, JPEG's own limit: PDFium (Chrome's and Edge's PDF viewer) draws nothing at all for an image with a side over 131,071 px. So a longer picture, such as a long scrolling screenshot, is stored losslessly as strips of at most 65,535 rows (or columns), each with its own soft mask, drawn edge to edge on its one page.
- **How each image is stored:**
  - **A JPEG passes through** (`DCTDecode`, its own compressed data, no generation loss) when its content is JPEG (sniffed: a `.jpg` that's really a PNG is stored as a PNG), it's baseline, extended or progressive Huffman at 8 bits with 1 or 3 components, and its color transform is known, the same way to Grape's decoder and to PDF readers (an Adobe or JFIF marker, or component ids 1, 2 and 3, but not, say, an Adobe RGB marker beside a JFIF one, which readers take differently). It's decoded once first with `image::decode`, which checks it under D16's limits and gives its ICC profile. Those pixels are kept only until the passthrough is settled: the JPEG's own frame must match them (the same upright size, gray or RGB), and a JPEG that can't pass through is re-encoded from them, with no second decode.
    - Its markers are copied without APP1 (EXIF, XMP), APP3 to APP13, APP15 and comments, so no GPS, camera, thumbnail or XMP data reaches the PDF. The JFIF APP0 (unless it holds a thumbnail), the ICC profile's APP2s, the Adobe APP14 and the tables and frame header stay; other APP0, APP2 and APP14 segments (JFXX thumbnails, MPF, FlashPix) go too. From the first scan to the end marker everything is copied byte for byte, and anything after the end marker is dropped.
    - Its EXIF orientation is read before the EXIF is dropped and applied by the page's matrix, so a portrait phone photo is upright and the page has the upright size.
    - CMYK and YCCK, 12-bit, arithmetic, lossless and hierarchical JPEGs, an unknown color transform, a Motion-JPEG frame (an `AVI1` marker, from a webcam or an IP camera) or a JPEG with no Huffman table before its first scan (such a frame may leave out its tables and rely on the standard ones, which Grape's decoder supplies and pdf.js doesn't), and markers the copier can't follow (one it doesn't know before the first scan, an APPn or comment between scans, more than 1,024 segments before the first scan, a length past the end, a missing end marker) are re-encoded (the next item), not refused. The cap keeps a crafted file of millions of tiny segments, which the decoder takes, from growing the list of kept byte ranges past the working set: real JPEGs have a few dozen segments.
  - **HEIC, lossy WebP and JPEGs that can't pass through** are encoded as JPEG at quality 90 (D16's JPEG quality) from the upright pixels, as 8-bit RGB or gray. The JPEG is written into a buffer the size of the lossless stream's bound, which keeps memory bounded: if it would come out larger (in practice only a tiny image, where JPEG's tables outweigh the pixels) or the encoder refuses it (a side over 65,535 px), the picture is stored losslessly instead, as in the next item.
  - **PNG, BMP, TIFF and lossless WebP** stay lossless: 8-bit gray or RGB, Flate-compressed with PNG predictors (each row gets the filter whose output has the smallest sum of absolute values). 16-bit and float images are reduced to 8 bits. A gray image with only black and white pixels is stored at 1 bit per pixel, so a text scan's pixels take an eighth of the bytes.
  - **Alpha**, re-encoded or lossless, becomes an 8-bit soft mask (Flate with the same predictors), written only when some pixel isn't opaque. It's never flattened.
  - **Color:** the ICC profile is kept (ICCBased) when it fits the pixels written, gray or RGB; otherwise the image is DeviceRGB or DeviceGray. Never CMYK.
- **Memory:** one input at a time: decode it, build its streams, write them, drop them, then the next. The peak is one image's working set, held to D16's 1 GiB (`image::WORKING_SET_LIMIT`). So a 50-photo Create PDF needs about as much memory as its largest photo.
  - Writing a page holds the decoded pixels and one stream at a time, at most about the raw size of the 8-bit pixels: rows are converted and filtered one at a time (a whole 8-bit copy is made only for a re-encoded picture that isn't 8-bit), and each stream's buffer is reserved once at its bound and never grows.
  - The decoders (`image::decode`, and the HEIC helper's size check, `image::pixels_fit`) are held to the raster engine's estimate for PNG output, not PDF's: `image.rs` counts no encoder buffers for PDF, so PDF's estimate would be the decoded image alone. PNG's covers the decoded image plus one compressed copy of the 8-bit pixels. The small rest (a filter byte a row, Deflate's block overhead, the profile) is checked exactly once the pixels are decoded, before any stream's buffer is reserved, so nothing over the limit is ever allocated.
- **Cancel, progress, errors:**
  - Cancel is checked before each input, at least every 64 rows while compressing, and in every read and write. A cancel gives "Cancelled. Nothing was saved." and removes the temp file.
  - Progress for input i of n: (i + 0.5) / n once it's decoded and (i + 1) / n once it's written, with the HEIC helper's own fractions inside its share; the last input's 1.0 waits until the file is published.
  - A file Grape can't read stops the whole PDF, in Create PDF too: nothing is published, and the toast names that file in the raster or HEIC engine's own words. A write failure names the destination folder ("Grape couldn't save into …"), as the other engines do. The log-only details that `pdf/create.rs` writes itself (the writer, Flate, the memory check) hold no path or name: an `io` error keeps only its kind. Details passed through from the raster engine's decode (`image::decode`: open, read, too large, decode) and from the shared folder and publish steps (`publish.rs`) do name the source's path or the destination folder, as in every engine (`GrapeError::detail` says so). The app's log replaces each job's paths, folders and file names before it writes a failure (`jobs::failure_log`), so nothing that logs a detail may skip that. There are no job notes.
- **Dispatch** (`lib.rs`, only the arms lane B agreed to): a new `Engine::PdfCreate`. `pdf::create::reads(from, sidecars)` is true for the five raster formats, and for HEIC with `SidecarPaths::heif`. `engine_for` picks it for `to == Pdf`, after the PDF arm; `tool_engine` picks it for Create PDF when `pdf::create::combines(from, sidecars)`: JPG, PNG, WebP and TIFF, and HEIC with the helper (not BMP, above). `run_job` hands a conversion to `pdf::create::convert`, and a Create PDF whose inputs all have it to `pdf::create::combine`, before the PDF tools' arm. None of it needs PDFium, so a build without PDFium still runs these.
- **The app:** no code change. `jobs::check_runnable` asks `supports_with`, so these drops run as they are. HEIC → PDF, or a Create PDF with a HEIC in it, without the helper says "Grape's HEIC helper is missing" / "Reinstall Grape to convert HEIC files." on Windows (D35's helper rows); where the helper doesn't ship it says "HEIC to PDF is coming." or "Create PDF on HEIC is coming.". No images → PDF drop needs two helpers, so none gets "Some of Grape's helpers are missing". Create PDF opens no panel: `panel::route_for` sends it straight to `jobs::submit`. The done toast names its one output, "Done / a Combined.pdf".
- **Counts:** with every helper there, 151 + 6 = **157** of the 178 v1 pairs run. Left in M3: AVIF and SVG (21 pairs, AVIF → PDF and SVG → PDF among them).
- **Tests:**
  - Unit tests (always on): the writer (one and three pages parse with lopdf; every cross-reference offset points at its object; `/Size`; a reserved object never written fails), the JPEG marker filter (scan data byte for byte, metadata gone, which frames and color transforms pass, truncated and overrunning lengths), the 8 orientation matrices, the predictors (every stream decoded by lopdf's own Flate and PNG predictor decoders, which share no code with Grape's, Paeth's ties among them) and 1-bit packing, the page-size cap and the matrix of a scaled page, the memory figure, 16-bit and float rows, the JPEG → lossless fallback, and lossy or lossless WebP told apart on hand-built files (`image` has no lossy WebP encoder). HEIC → PDF and a Create PDF with a HEIC run there too, through `heif.rs`'s fake helper, which is the library's own test binary, so `tests/` can't reach it.
  - Integration tests (`crates/grape-engines/tests/create_pdf.rs`, always on) run JPG, PNG, WebP, TIFF and BMP → PDF and Create PDF through `run_job`: page count and sizes, a JPEG with EXIF orientation 6 and GPS whose PDF holds neither and keeps its scan data byte for byte, a JPEG passed through in all 8 orientations (its stored size, its scan data and the page's matrix), soft masks, 1-bit pages, a taken name, a damaged middle input, a missing input, a cancel before and during, which formats each runs on, no Info or XMP, the source unchanged and nothing left behind.
  - `pdf-it` (`tests/create_pdf_render.rs`, `GRAPE_TEST_PDFIUM`) renders the pages with PDFium and compares them with the upright decode: a JPEG in all 8 orientations (each checked as passed through, not re-encoded), an RGB JPEG (Adobe transform 0, passed through with DCTDecode's `/ColorTransform 0`), a transparent PNG over white, 1-bit and TIFF pages, a 20,000 × 10 image's 14,400 × 7.2 pt page (checked by its size, its matrix and its last columns, which must show the image's last ones), and an 8 × 140,000 ramp written as three strips, drawn whole and in order.
  - The app's tests: these drops pass `check_runnable` with and without PDFium, a HEIC among them needs the helper on each OS's rules, Create PDF goes straight to `jobs::submit`, and its toast and log line.
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-28: 41 unit tests in `pdf::create` and its helpers (5 of them through the fake HEIC helper), 15 in `tests/create_pdf.rs`, 5 PDFium render tests (the JPEG and lossless pages within a mean error of 1 in 255 of the upright decode, the 8 × 140,000 picture's strips row by row within 3 in 255, and the 20,000 × 10 page by its size, its matrix and its last columns), and the app's 276. Sample PDFs from a phone-style JPEG (orientation 6, GPS), a transparent PNG and a black-and-white PNG, and a Create PDF of all three, render upright in PDFium.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "Images to PDF and Create PDF, M3/M4". Nothing has run on either.
- **Not handled yet:** SVG and AVIF input; the image's DPI; paper sizes and margins; PDF/A; Mark of the Web on the output. A multi-page TIFF or an animated WebP gives its first frame, as in D16.
- **Revisit if:** users want the image's DPI honored, or paper sizes and margins (A4, Letter, fit to page); SVG or AVIF join (same writer, their own decoders); Create PDF needs a panel for its order or page size (it would be a fifth panel tool, D31).
- *2026-10-28 (D39, SVG):* SVG → PDF runs, and Create PDF takes SVG with Grape's SVG worker (`reads` and `combines` check `SidecarPaths::svg_worker`). An SVG's page is its own size, `css × 0.75` pt, not 1 px = 1 pt, drawn at 300 DPI and stored lossless (`Picture::Pixels` gains `page: Option<(f64, f64)>` in points, clamped as `page_size` clamps). So `write_pdf` now returns notes, `ImagesLeftOut` and `PagesScaled`, and "There are no job notes" above no longer holds. A `.svg` that's really a raster image is read by the raster engine, as a `.heic` that isn't HEIF is. With SVG and AVIF in, every image → PDF pair runs.

## D41: AVIF (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** AVIF ⇄ JPG, PNG, WEBP, TIFF and BMP (10 pairs), HEIC ⇄ AVIF (2) and AVIF → PDF (1) convert through Grape's image helper, the same helper process and protocol as HEIC (D33), bumped to **protocol 2**. On Windows `grape-heif.exe` gains **dav1d 1.5.4** to decode and **libaom 3.15.2** (its encoder only) to encode, both built into its libheif. On macOS the worker (`Grape --grape-heif-worker`) decodes AVIF with **ImageIO** and encodes it with **rav1e 0.8.1 through ravif 0.13.0**, compiled into Grape's own macOS executable. HEIC ⇄ AVIF is two helper runs, a decode and then an encode. SVG → AVIF waits for D39's worker. AVIF has no tools in v1 (FORMAT_MATRIX: "[Grape] … avif bmp get none"), so Create PDF never takes one and AVIF → PDF is a conversion only. The code is lane A's: `heif.rs`, `helper.rs`, `pdf/`, `grape-heif-proto`, `tools/grape-heif`, `grape-platform` and the app. In `grape-engines`' `lib.rs` only doc comments change, which lane B agreed to; no dispatch arm changes, because `engine_for`'s `heif::supports(..) && sidecars.heif` arm and `pdf::create::reads` already route a format `heif.rs` says it takes. `image.rs`, `headers.rs` and grape-core don't change: `image::{decode, prepare, pixels_fit, publish_pixels, icc_fits, RASTER}` are called as they are, and lane B's `tests/dispatch.rs` and `tests/image.rs` keep their counts (their sidecar sets have no `heif`). It replaces ENGINES.md's "`ravif` (AVIF encode)" in the raster engine. The spec is `avif-spec.md` (2026-10-28).
- **Why a helper, on both OSes:** an AV1 decoder is a large C and assembly parser fed untrusted files, and an AV1 frame encode is one blocking call with no cancel. So, as for HEIC, a crash or a hang ends only the helper, and a cancel is a kill. No in-process pure-Rust decoder was good enough: avif-decode 3.0.0 (rav1d 1.1.0) refuses grids and ignores ICC, and rav1d brings about 600 `unsafe` sites and dav1d's assembly, plus MPL-2.0 and CC0-only crates; `image` 0.25.10's AVIF decoder binds C dav1d and mp4parse (MPL-2.0, no grids); rav1d-safe and zenavif are AGPL.
- **Why these codecs:**

| | Windows (`grape-heif.exe`) | macOS (the worker) |
|---|---|---|
| Decode | dav1d 1.5.4 in libheif; libheif's aom decoder off | ImageIO (macOS 13.0+, Grape's minimum) |
| Encode | libaom 3.15.2 in libheif, encoder only | ravif 0.13.0 / rav1e 0.8.1, in Grape's executable |

  - **dav1d on Windows:** libheif ranks it first (priority 150 against aom's 100); Grape's `max_pixels` reaches dav1d's frame size limit before a frame is allocated (libaom's decoder allocates first); it was about 3× faster with a third of the memory (0.185 s and 73 MB against 0.58 s and 255 MB for 12 MP on one thread, measured on Linux with older builds); and Grape's FFmpeg already ships the same release, with the same `licenses/ffmpeg/dav1d.txt` and `dav1d-PATENTS.txt`.
  - **libaom on Windows:** libheif's preferred AV1 encoder (priority 60, against SVT-AV1's 40 and rav1e's 20), and the only one with the whole still-picture path (all-intra, still_picture, tune IQ from 3.13, 4:2:0 to 4:4:4, alpha). At speed 6 it matched today's HEIC (1.28 MB and SSIM 0.952 for a 12 MP photo) about 3.6× as fast as x265 (1.82 s against 6.51 s), with a 256 MB peak (libaom 3.8.2 and FFmpeg 6.1.1 on Linux; the pins weren't buildable there). 3.15.2's fixes are all in the encoder: a still-picture one, two that OSS-Fuzz found, and two more. Rejected: SVT-AV1 (1.9 GB for one 12 MP frame, over the 2 GiB Job Object; 4:2:0 only; no license in vcpkg's port) and rav1e (no vcpkg port at the pin, Rust and cargo-c in the C build, about 6× slower).
