  - **ImageIO on the Mac:** `kCGImagePropertyAVISDictionary` is documented from macOS 13.0, WebKit gates AVIF on macOS 13, and Safari 16.1 added it on Ventura. It handles grids, alpha, 10 and 12 bits and nclx or ICC color, and ships nothing new.
  - **ravif on the Mac, not ImageIO and not a libheif helper:** ImageIO can't encode AVIF on macOS 13 to 15; on 26 developers report it can on Apple silicon (Intel Macs and GitHub's VMs are unknown) and that it tiles anything over 512 px into a grid, which Firefox can't show. ravif works on every Mac Grape supports and on CI's VMs, so CI tests Mac AVIF output end to end, which it can't for HEIC. Measured (12.2 MP, quality 80, 4:4:4, Linux): speed 6 with assembly on 4 threads 4.0 s (493 KB, SSIM 0.950), on 1 thread 11.3 s, and without assembly on 1 thread 29.6 s; about 300 MB at the peak. Its costs, accepted: about 3.5× libaom's CPU, always 4:4:4, no ICC (below), BSD-2-Clause and the AOM Patent License inside Grape's own Mac binary (the D23 amendment), and about 8 MB more in the universal binary. One libaom on both OSes would need a universal libheif and libaom build and a second sidecar on the Mac.
  - So there are **two AVIF encoders**, as HEIC already has (x265 against ImageIO), and their outputs differ: 4:2:0 with the ICC profile on Windows, 4:4:4 converted to sRGB on the Mac.
- **Protocol 2** (`crates/grape-heif-proto`, `PROTOCOL` 1 → 2; the parent, `grape-heif.exe` and the Mac worker change in one commit, D33's rule):
  - `HeifFormat { Heic, Avif }` (`"heic"`, `"avif"` on the wire). `Op::Encode { format, quality? }`: `format` is required, so protocol 1's `{"kind":"encode"}` is refused. `Op::Decode` and `Op::Probe` are unchanged: libheif picks the decoder from the image item, and the worker passes the sniffed format to its codec.
  - `names::OUTPUT` becomes `OUTPUT_HEIC` (`out.heic`) and `OUTPUT_AVIF` (`out.avif`), with `names::output(format)`. `PIXELS` and `INPUT` are unchanged. `serve` writes the format's output with `create_new` and requires it to sniff as the format asked for.
  - `Caps { heic: FormatCaps {decode, encode}, avif: FormatCaps {decode, encode}, codec }`. Windows answers `{"heic":{"decode":true,"encode":true},"avif":{"decode":true,"encode":true},"codec":"libheif 1.23.7 (libde265 1.1.3, x265 4.3, dav1d 1.5.4, aom 3.15.2)"}`; the Mac `"codec":"ImageIO, ravif"` (or `"ImageIO"` in a build without the encoder).
  - `Limits` is unchanged on the wire; `Limits::for_encode(format)` is `DEFAULT` with `max_encode_side` 16,384 for AVIF (AV1 level 6's MaxHSize) and 16,888 for HEIC. `max_encode_pixels` stays 35,651,584 for both: level 6's MaxPicSize is HEVC 6.2's. There's no separate height cap at level 6's 8,704: a transposed picture gets `seq_level_idx` 31, which dav1d, libaom and ImageIO decode.
  - `WORKER_HEAP_BYTES` (2 GiB) is new and not on the wire: the bound the Mac worker arms, and `heif::Limits::default().helper_memory`.
  - **The sniff** gains `Avif`, in this order: a major HEVC brand is HEIC; a major `avif` or `avis` is AVIF; a structural major (`mif1 msf1 miaf MiHE`) with a compatible HEVC brand is HEIC (HEVC wins a tie); a structural major with a compatible AV1 brand (`avif avis avio MA1B MA1A`) and no HEVC one is AVIF; a structural major with no codec brand is HEIC, and the helper decides (D33's 2026-10-26 note; libheif writes AV1 grids so too); any other HEIF brand is `OtherHeif`, which now means AVC, JPEG, JPEG 2000, VVC or uncompressed. `isom` with a compatible `avif` stays `OtherHeif`, as `isom` with `heic` is `NotHeif`. `mif3` (the low-overhead form) stays `NotHeif`.
  - `FailKind` gains no kind. `Unsupported` is now "a codec or profile this helper can't read", `NoEncoder` "this machine can't write that format".
- **Windows (`tools/grape-heif`, version `1.23.7-grape.2`):**
  - vcpkg stays at `e456309` (2026-10-10). The install spec is `libheif[core,hevc,aom,dav1d]`. No new Rust crate, so `tools/grape-heif/Cargo.lock` gains only its own version.
  - The libheif overlay gains a `dav1d` feature (`WITH_DAV1D`, dav1d as its dependency; vcpkg's recipe forces dav1d off) and builds libaom's encoder only (`-DWITH_AOM_DECODER=OFF`; the `aom` feature now sets `WITH_AOM_ENCODER` alone). With plugin loading off every codec found is built in, and `VCPKG_LOCK_FIND_PACKAGE_DAV1D` makes a missing dav1d fail at configure time.
  - A second overlay, `vcpkg/ports/aom`, takes vcpkg's libaom 3.15.1 recipe to **3.15.2** from AOM's release tarball, `https://storage.googleapis.com/aom-releases/libaom-3.15.2.tar.gz` (6,629,974 bytes, uploaded 2026-10-09, SHA-256 `67bb54b245f33ed98600e08269e6139986e48114e14042474ddd8885801dfddc`, SHA-512 `4ba185a642c9dd32d1ab708916a5aed1a65d3cd6d2599d632f33c83849e6702ca91528ad09a007eb39ac2723f0b10de9a7905ed26abfef42a164f13e21aacb7a`), with `vcpkg_download_distfile` and the recipe's own `aom-rename-static.diff`. Why: vcpkg's port fetches with `vcpkg_from_git` and no SHA-512, so `build.ps1` couldn't check it, and a local `git archive`'s bytes depend on the git version. Everything else is vcpkg's (Perl, NASM, the `ENABLE_*=OFF` options, the copyright list), and libaom's decoder stays compiled into libaom: nothing references it, so the linker drops it. `v3.15.3-rc1` (2026-10-21) is too new for D9, and a release candidate anyway.
  - **dav1d 1.5.4** is vcpkg's port unchanged: GitHub's archive of tag `1.5.4` (2026-07-14, commit `54706fc6bc0cdecab7e9593974a4039cc038fca7`, SHA-512 `7ee5906640495919462b2242c44a8c3cc577fdda52fc25257792f6df919429d54e4a48c315a7a11759385f044f68d3d6573fd720853f447e2d8520a13693827f`), built static with meson (`vcpkg-tool-meson` 1.9.0), Python 3.14.7 and NASM 3.01. `build.ps1` checks the pax comment's commit, as for x265.
  - **The code:** the encoder is per format. HEIC keeps D33's parameters. AVIF is libaom at speed 6, 4:2:0, quality 50 by default, tune `auto` (IQ on libaom 3.15: quality 50 is cq 37), and min(cores, 8) threads (the plugin's default is every core, up to 64), each set explicitly so a libheif bump can't change it; ICC as for HEIC (a `prof` box and no nclx beside it). Decode is unchanged: `set_convert_hdr_to_8bit` makes 10- and 12-bit AVIF 8-bit. It only drops bits: as for HEIC (heic-spec J11), only an ICC profile is passed on and no color is converted, so an AVIF whose only color description is nclx and isn't sRGB is read as sRGB (see "Not handled yet"). The caps come from libheif's decoder and encoder descriptors. The banner's configuration line is `libheif=1.23.7 libde265=1.1.3 x265=4.3 dav1d=1.5.4 aom=3.15.2 plugins=disabled crt=static protocol=2`: dav1d's version from the static library's own `dav1d_version()` (libheif's plugin name gives dav1d's API version), libaom's from its encoder's name.
  - **The bundling gate** (`scripts/check-sidecars.mjs`) also reads the banner's configuration line: its `protocol=N` must be `PROTOCOL` in `crates/grape-heif-proto/src/lib.rs` (D25's 2026-10-28 note). A `grape-heif.exe` built for D33 (`1.23.7-grape.1`, `protocol=1`) would refuse every HEIC and AVIF job ("Grape's image helper doesn't match this version of Grape."), HEIC ones that worked before included, so no installer can carry one. `pnpm tauri dev` doesn't run the gate, so `PLATFORM_WINDOWS.md` → Dev setup, `START_HERE.md` and TESTING's first AVIF item say to replace it on the PC.
  - **Fixtures:** `grape-heif-fixtures` also writes `opaque.avif`, `alpha.avif`, `odd-3x5.avif`, `deep.avif` (10-bit), `grid.avif`, `icc.avif` and `damaged.avif` (cut in half), each sniffed before it's written. `grid.avif` may sniff as HEIC: libheif 1.23 gives an AV1 grid no codec brand.
  - **Licenses:** the exe stays GPL-3.0-or-later as a whole; BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, MIT and ISC are GPL-compatible. `licenses/grape-heif/` gains `dav1d.txt` (`COPYING`, the bytes of `licenses/ffmpeg/dav1d.txt`), `dav1d-PATENTS.txt` (`doc/PATENTS`, the Alliance for Open Media Patent License 1.0), `dav1d-x86inc.txt` (the ISC notice of `x86inc.asm`) and `aom.txt` (vcpkg's copyright file: libaom's `LICENSE` and `PATENTS` and the fastfeat, vector and x86inc licenses). The patent license's §1.2.1(b) asks for it in a binary distribution's legal notices, so each project's own copy ships. `about.hbs`'s grape-heif section names both, and the source bundle gains the two archives, the dav1d, aom, vcpkg-tool-meson and vcpkg-cmake-get-vars ports and `overlay/aom`.
  - **Build cost (estimated, not measured):** the first build goes from about 30 to about 45–50 minutes (libaom 10–15, dav1d 3–5), and about 5 GB of disk; the exe grows about 5–6 MB. With a binary-cache hit the build step is unchanged. vcpkg's `--only-downloads` fetches the build tools too, Strawberry Perl among them (304,301,401 bytes), so `grape-heif.yml` caches `downloads\tools` beside the binary cache (keyed on the lock and the overlays; only when the workflow's cache input is on, so `release.yml` fetches them fresh), and `build.ps1` treats a vcpkg folder that holds only `downloads` as empty. That's the binary cache's level of trust: vcpkg doesn't hash an unpacked tool it finds again.
- **macOS:**
  - **Decode:** ImageIO, hinted with the sniffed format's type (`public.heic` or `public.avif`). The type ImageIO then reads may be any of `public.heic`, `public.heif`, `public.heics`, `public.avif` and `public.avis`, whichever the sniff said. That departs from the spec's per-format lists, because libheif writes AV1 grids that sniff as HEIC (and ImageIO would type them AVIF), and ImageIO types some AVIFs `public.heif` (WebKit bug 241904). `CGImageSourceSetAllowableTypes` takes those five on 14.2+. Everything else is D33's decode: the size and decoder budget from the properties first, the upright full-size thumbnail, RGBA8 with the ICC profile, Display P3 for PQ and HLG. `caps().avif.decode` is `public.avif` in `CGImageSourceCopyTypeIdentifiers()`.
  - **Encode:** `grape_platform::avif::encode`, safe Rust and OS-neutral, behind a new feature `avif-encode` that the app's `Cargo.toml` turns on for macOS builds only (Windows and Linux builds never compile rav1e). ravif with quality 80 unless the request names one (1 to 100, checked before ravif can assert), alpha at the same quality, speed 6 (ravif's default of 4 took 3× as long for files 3% smaller), 8 bits and min(cores, 8) threads. An RGBA picture is two rav1e encodes, color and alpha, and they take turns: ravif is built without its `threading` feature, whose `rayon::join` ran both at once on two pools (twice the threads, and both encodes' memory at the same time), and rav1e with its own `threading`, so each encode still gets its pool (*2026-10-28 fix*; a test reads `Cargo.lock` to keep it so). The pixels go to ravif as they are, through rgb's `FromSlice`, and are copied only when an ICC profile has them converted. EXIF is never written. A build without the feature answers `noEncoder`, "This Mac can't write AVIF files."; ImageIO's AVIF encoder is never used.
  - **Color:** ravif writes BT.709 primaries with the sRGB transfer (nclx) and no ICC profile, so pixels with an RGB profile are first converted to sRGB with moxcms (already in `Cargo.lock` through `image`, now a direct optional dependency at the same version and features), alpha untouched. A profile moxcms can't read, or a gray one, is taken as sRGB. A Display P3 iPhone photo becomes sRGB: right in every viewer, its colors outside sRGB clipped. Windows keeps the profile.
  - **rav1e's assembly** is on only for `aarch64-apple-darwin` (ravif's `asm` feature in a target table), built by its build script with Xcode's clang. The Intel slice is cross-built on the arm64 runner without nasm, so it's pure Rust, about 2.6× slower (29.6 s on one thread, 12.1 s on four, for 12 MP on the test Xeon).
  - **The heap bound:** `main.rs`'s `heif_worker()` arms `grape_engines::pdf::heap` at `WORKER_HEAP_BYTES` (2 GiB) right after `ImageIo::restrict_process()`, before `worker_main`, for every job, HEIC included. It's the Mac worker's first memory cap (D33 listed one as a follow-up): past it the worker exits 86 (`OVER_HEAP_EXIT`), which `heif.rs` reports as "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped." on both OSes (`grape-heif.exe` never exits 86). It counts rav1e and Grape's buffers, not ImageIO's own `malloc`. `heif_worker()` runs `run_heif_worker`, the worker's steps in order on every OS (restrict, arm, serve), so a test runs the same path in a child of the app's test binary with a stand-in `restrict` and `serve`: the bound is armed after `restrict` and before `serve`, a block 64 MiB under it is given, and one over it ends the child with exit 86. Taking the arm out of the worker's path fails that test on every OS.
- **Engine** (`heif.rs`, now "HEIC and AVIF through a helper process"):
  - `HEIF = [Heic, Avif]`. `supports(from, to)` is a format of `HEIF` to or from `image::RASTER`, or HEIC to AVIF and back: 22 pairs. `convert_with` sends them to `from_heif`, `to_heif` and `heif_to_heif`; `encode_pixels` is split out of the old `to_heic`, `pub(crate)`, for D39's SVG too.
  - **The content decides**, whatever the name: `Sniff::Heic` and `Sniff::Avif` both go to the helper, so a `.avif` that's HEIC decodes as HEIC, and a `.heic` that's AVIF as AVIF (D33's refusal of AVIF in a `.heic` is gone). `NotHeif` goes to the raster engine (FORMAT_MATRIX rule 3) and `OtherHeif` is refused, as before.
  - **Encode:** `image::prepare(img, Format::Heic, …)` is the stand-in for AVIF, because `image.rs` gives RGBA8 for HEIC and AVIF needs the same; a comment says so. The caps are `Limits::encode_limits(format)`, the job's limits held to `proto::Limits::for_encode(format)`, and over them is "… is too large to save as AVIF." before anything starts. The output must sniff as the format asked for.
  - **HEIC ⇄ AVIF:** the decode runs in progress (0.05, 0.45) and the encode in (0.5, 0.9), each run with its own work folder and deadline. A source that isn't HEIF is decoded by the raster engine and then encoded, in one run.
  - **AVIF → PDF** (`pdf::create`): `reads` takes `heif::HEIF` with the helper, and an AVIF page is stored lossy (JPEG quality 90) with its ICC profile, like HEIC's: an AVIF is a photo codec's output. `combines` is unchanged, so Create PDF never takes an AVIF.
- **Limits:** D33's, with the encode side cap per format (16,888 for HEIC, 16,384 for AVIF) and the same 35,651,584 pixels; 120 s + 3 s per megapixel to encode (227 s at 35.6 MP, far above ravif's and libaom's measured times). The Job Object stays 2 GiB on Windows; at 35.6 MP libaom on 8 threads is estimated at 0.6–0.8 GB, unmeasured, and CI's `heif-it-windows` logs the real peak.
  - **rav1e's memory, measured** (*2026-10-28 fix*): the Rust heap the Mac worker's bound counts, at the cap (8192 × 4352), quality 80, speed 6, on Linux (x86_64, release, without assembly; two 8-thread pools, as on a Mac with 8 cores or more):

    | Picture | Color and alpha at once, with Grape's copy (as first built) | Taking turns, no copy (now) |
    |---|---|---|
    | RGBA: a gradient with light grain, a soft-edged disc on a transparent background | 1.35–1.37 GB | 0.98 GB |
    | The same with heavy grain (0–47 added to each color) | | 1.34 GB |
    | RGB with grain 0–15 / 0–47 / 0–127 | | 1.09 / 2.17 / 2.28 GB |
    | Every byte random, RGB / RGBA | 2.66 GB (RGBA) | 2.28 / 2.51 GB |

    So a photo-like picture at the cap needs about 1 to 1.4 GB, and the alpha encode no longer adds to the color one. A picture that is mostly noise (heavy grain in every channel, or random bytes) still goes past the 2 GiB bound with 4 threads or more, and its job ends "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped.": rav1e 0.8.1 always delays its loop-filter decisions to the end of a tile (`enable_delayed_loopfilter_rdo`), so it keeps every superblock's coded symbols until then, in blocks of 128 to 256 KB, and that grows with the picture's detail and with the threads (the same 0–47 grain RGB: 1.03 GB on 1 thread, 1.42 GB on 2, 2.26 GB on 4, 2.17 GB on 8). The first build's "about 0.8 GB" was an estimate for RGB alone. CI's DMG job encodes a 35.6 MP RGBA picture with light grain and soft transparent edges through the shipped worker (the runner's 3 cores, so 3 threads; 1.0 GB counted for the same request on Linux with 4) and prints its time and peak resident memory.
- **Messages:** where a message names a format it names the right one: "… is too large to save as AVIF.", "This Mac can't write AVIF files." (`NoEncoder` on a Mac; on Windows, where `grape-heif.exe` always has both encoders and one without isn't this Grape's, "Grape's image helper can't write AVIF files. Reinstall Grape."), "… is a kind of AVIF image Grape can't read yet." (`Unsupported` on AVIF content; HEIC content and `OtherHeif` keep "a kind of HEIF image"), "… isn't a readable AVIF image.". The helper is no longer only HEIC's, so every user message says **"image helper"**: "Grape's image helper is missing.", "… stopped unexpectedly.", "… couldn't start.", "… doesn't match this version of Grape."; details say "HEIF helper". The app's helper row is "Grape's image helper is missing" / "Reinstall Grape to convert HEIC and AVIF files." (31 characters, inside the toast's 248 px title; the detail may take its second line). *Amended by D28 (2026-10-28): its detail is "Reinstall Grape to work with HEIC and AVIF files.", which answers a HEIC Compress drop too.* An AVIF encode that fails says "Grape couldn't write a AVIF file.": that's lane B's shared `publish::Names::encode_failed`, which already says "a MP4" and "a AIFF", and is left to lane B.
- **The app:** the helper row's words above; `ships: cfg!(windows)` stays, because on macOS the helper is Grape itself. On Windows without `grape-heif.exe` every AVIF drop (to and from the rasters, to PDF, HEIC ⇄ AVIF) gets that row; on macOS they run; on Linux they're "coming". No tool is offered or run on an AVIF.
- **Counts** (from grape-core's matrix and `supports_with`, with every sidecar and the helper): 146 v1 pairs run without the image helper, and it adds 24 (the 22 above, HEIC → PDF and AVIF → PDF), so **170 of the 178 v1 pairs** run. Left in M3: SVG's 8 (D39). Tools are unchanged.
- **New dependencies** (each checked against D9 on crates.io on 2026-10-28: published on or before 2026-10-14, none yanked; all in Grape's `Cargo.lock`, none in Windows or Linux app builds):
  - **ravif 0.13.0** (pinned `=0.13.0`, 2026-01-19, BSD-3-Clause): the AVIF encoder's front end in `grape-platform` (optional, `avif-encode`; `default-features = false`, and `asm` on Apple silicon; not its `threading`, above). It writes the AVIF container (avif-serialize) around rav1e's AV1.
  - **rav1e 0.8.1** (2025-06-16, BSD-2-Clause, with the Alliance for Open Media Patent License 1.0): the AV1 encoder ravif drives, with its arm64 assembly on Apple silicon. Also a direct optional dependency of `grape-platform` (pinned `=0.8.1`, `default-features = false`, `threading`), named only to turn its threads on.
  - **rgb 0.8.53** (2026-02-25, MIT): ravif's pixel types, already locked through it; a direct optional dependency (`=0.8.53`, `default-features = false`) for `FromSlice`, which lends ravif the picture's bytes instead of a copy.
  - **moxcms 0.8.1** (2026-03-07, BSD-3-Clause OR Apache-2.0): already locked through `image`; now a direct optional dependency of `grape-platform` for the sRGB conversion.
  - `grape-heif-proto` is now a dependency of `grape-platform` on every OS (`avif.rs` takes its `Pixels` and `Failure`), not only on macOS.
  - The 41 new lock entries (versions, dates, licenses): in the Mac app: ravif 0.13.0 (2026-01-19, BSD-3-Clause), rav1e 0.8.1 (2025-06-16, BSD-2-Clause), v_frame 0.3.9 (2025-06-09, BSD-2-Clause), av1-grain 0.2.5 (2025-10-23, BSD-2-Clause), avif-serialize 0.8.9 (2026-05-07, BSD-3-Clause), av-scenechange 0.14.1 (2025-05-21, MIT), rayon 1.12.0 (2026-04-14), rayon-core 1.13.0 (2025-08-12), crossbeam-deque 0.8.8 (2026-09-05), crossbeam-epoch 0.9.21 (2026-09-05), bitstream-io 4.10.0 (2026-04-14), no_std_io2 0.9.4 (2026-05-05), imgref 1.12.3 (2026-08-26, CC0-1.0 OR Apache-2.0; Grape takes Apache-2.0), rgb 0.8.53 (2026-02-25, MIT), loop9 0.1.5 (2024-01-15, MIT), y4m 0.8.0 (2023-04-21, MIT), arrayvec 0.7.8 (2026-07-02), itertools 0.14.0 (2024-12-31; 0.15.0 was already locked), num-bigint 0.4.8 (2026-07-05), num-integer 0.1.47 (2026-08-12), num-rational 0.4.2 (2024-05-08), num-derive 0.4.2 (2024-02-06), aligned 0.4.3 (2025-12-15), aligned-vec 0.6.4 (2025-03-15, MIT), as-slice 0.2.1 (2021-03-25), equator 0.4.2 and equator-macro 0.4.2 (2024-12-21, MIT), maybe-rayon 0.1.1 (2023-02-03, MIT), simd_helpers 0.1.0 (2019-10-06, MIT), profiling and profiling-procmacros 1.0.18 (2026-05-04), noop_proc_macro 0.3.0 (2020-06-19, MIT), arg_enum_proc_macro 0.3.4 (2023-09-12, MIT), paste 1.0.15 (2024-05-07) and pastey 0.1.1 (2025-08-12); build tools only: built 0.8.1 (2026-05-21, MIT), and on Apple silicon nasm-rs 0.3.2 (2026-01-31) and jobserver 0.1.35 (2026-07-06, for `cc`'s parallel build); in the lock only, never built for a shipped target (rav1e's `cfg(fuzzing)` and dev dependencies): arbitrary 1.5.0 (2026-10-06), interpolate_name 0.2.4 (2023-09-12, MIT) and libfuzzer-sys 0.4.13 (2026-06-04, (MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND NCSA; cargo-deny's target list leaves it out, so NCSA needs no allowlist entry). Those without a license named are MIT OR Apache-2.0. 35 of them ship in the Mac app: `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` goes from 313 to 348 crates.
  - `paste` 1.0.15 is RUSTSEC-2024-0436 (unmaintained, no fix), through rav1e: CI's advisories step reports it and doesn't fail.
  - Native, in `grape-heif.exe` only: dav1d 1.5.4 (BSD-2-Clause, x86inc ISC, AOM Patent License 1.0) and libaom 3.15.2 (BSD-2-Clause, with BSD-3-Clause, MIT and ISC parts, AOM Patent License 1.0), above.
  - The licenses: D23's amendment of 2026-10-28 (BSD-2-Clause, and AOM's patent license in Grape's own Mac binary's notices).
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-28 (nothing ran on Windows or a Mac; the cross-target checks were clippy, and rav1e's arm64 assembly was assembled and its symbols checked with llvm-nm, not linked): `grape-heif-proto`'s 65 unit tests (protocol 2's exact JSON both ways, `encode` without `format` refused, the sniff table with every AV1 rule, `serve` handing the codec the sniffed format and refusing an output of the other format); `grape-engines`' 34 `heif` unit tests through the fake helper (all 22 pairs, the content deciding, the side caps per format, exit 86, every message, the dispatch with and without the helper) and `pdf::create`'s AVIF cases (a lossy page with its ICC profile, never in Create PDF), 608 library tests in all, every integration suite and the PDFium renders (`pdf-it`); `grape-platform`'s 10 `avif.rs` tests (72 with `avif-encode`); the app's 292 + 7 (the heap bound's child among them); clippy with `-D warnings` for Linux, Windows and both Macs, with and without `heif-it` and `avif-encode`; `tools/grape-heif`'s clippy for Windows (types only: libheif doesn't link here), fmt, `cargo deny` and `cargo about`; the workspace's `cargo deny` and notices; actionlint; the gate's tests; the UI's typecheck and tests. The `heif-it` tests compile and pass clippy but need the real helper, and the Windows build's PowerShell was parsed and partly run with pwsh 7 against a fake worker. *After the review (2026-10-28):* the gate's 32 tests (D33's banner with `protocol=1`, a newer protocol, a banner without one and an unreadable `PROTOCOL` are refused; the fixture and `banner.rs`'s doc line speak the real one); `avif.rs`'s 12 tests (74 in `grape-platform` with `avif-encode`; pixels without a profile go to ravif uncopied, and `Cargo.lock` has ravif's `threading` off and rav1e's on); `heif`'s 35 (609 library tests; `noEncoder` worded per OS); the app's 292 + 7, the heap child now through `run_heif_worker`; the memory table in Limits, and the DMG job's cap request through `serve` and `avif::encode` (done, an AVIF, 1.0 GB counted); and the rest of the checks above again, with the notices and both `cargo deny` runs unchanged.
- **CI runs:** `grape-heif` (the build with both overlays, the tool's tests with the real libraries, the banner and the exact probe answer, dav1d and libaom static, the four new license texts against the built ports and dav1d's archive, the 15 fixtures, every AVIF fixture decoding through the worker and the decoded pixels encoding as AVIF and HEIC), `heif-it-windows` (the AVIF cases against that exe, the 35.6 MP AVIF encode and decode under the 2 GiB Job Object with their peaks printed, `--nocapture`), `heif-it-macos` (the worker built with `heif-it,avif-encode`: ImageIO decoding the AVIF fixtures, rav1e's AVIF read back by ImageIO, HEIC → AVIF, and AVIF → HEIC saying "This Mac can't write HEIC files." on the VM), `rust-macos` (the `avif.rs` tests, through the app's feature), the DMG job (the worker must answer that it writes AVIF, then encode an 8192 × 4352 RGBA picture to AVIF under its heap bound, printing the time and peak), and `avif-probe-macos`, informational: ImageIO's AVIF types and `sips --formats` on `macos-latest` (26, arm64), `macos-26-intel`, `macos-15` and `macos-15-intel`. Record its output here once it has run.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "AVIF, D41". There's no macOS 13 runner, so 13.x is manual QA.
- **Not handled yet:** on Windows, AVIF color given only as nclx (no ICC profile) other than sRGB: `grape-heif.exe` passes only an ICC profile on, as for HEIC (heic-spec J11), and libheif doesn't convert primaries or transfer, so a Display P3 or BT.2020 AVIF comes out desaturated and a PQ or HLG (HDR) one flat and washed out (the Mac converts both: ImageIO reads nclx, and PQ and HLG are drawn into Display P3); on the Mac, a picture near the cap that is mostly noise, past the worker's heap bound (Limits); animated AVIF (`avis`) beyond its primary image; the `mif3`/`mini` low-overhead form; gain maps (`tmap`) and `sato`; grids on output; 10-bit or HDR output; Compress presets for AVIF; P3 (nclx) output on the Mac instead of sRGB (rav1e and avif-serialize can write it); rav1e's assembly on the Intel slice (NASM 3.01, built from its tarball and pinned, on the macOS jobs); SVG → AVIF (D39).
- **Open question for Maxwell:** confirm BSD-2-Clause and the Alliance for Open Media Patent License 1.0 in Grape's own macOS binary (shared with D39's BSD-2-Clause question). With no, the Mac's only AVIF writer would be a libheif and libaom sidecar, a second native build pipeline (M §5(b) of the research), and these 41 crates would leave the lock.
- **Revisit if:** nclx-only wide-gamut or HDR AVIFs come up on Windows (attach a Display P3 ICC profile when the primaries are 12 and the transfer sRGB or BT.709; tone-map or refuse PQ and HLG); a real photo near the cap ends "needs too much memory" on a Mac (give pictures over about 24 MP two threads, 1.42 GB at the cap for heavy grain at about half the speed, or drive rav1e directly); libaom or dav1d ship a security release (bump under D9's override, as for libheif); libheif bumps; libaom's memory at 35.6 MP on many cores comes near the Job Object (then lower the 8-thread cap); ravif on an Intel Mac takes more than 20 s for a 12 MP photo (assembly through NASM, or one encoder on both OSes); output differences between the two OSes draw complaints; a viewer refuses tall AVIFs (the 8,704 height cap); or ImageIO's AVIF encoder proves itself on every Mac Grape supports.
- *2026-10-28 (D39):* SVG → AVIF and SVG → HEIC run: the SVG worker's pixels go through `encode_pixels`, held to one picture of either format (16,384 a side, 35,651,584 px) before the worker draws. With them all 178 v1 pairs run when every helper is there.

## D43: The Float shelf on macOS (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** Control-drop on FLOAT puts the items on the shelf on macOS too, so all six file actions run on both OSes. The model, placement, snap, commands, page and toasts are D21's and shared; only the window's native calls and the drag out are the Mac's own. Items drag out as a copy or an alias, never a move.
- **Window:**
  - The same Tauri window and builder as on Windows, plus `accept_first_mouse(true)`, so the first press on an inactive shelf starts a drag. On macOS it's built hidden at launch, not on the first Float: wry asks macOS to activate Grape whenever it builds a webview, which on first use would take the keyboard from the app in front. The cost is one idle WKWebView. If the launch build fails, the first Float builds it (logged).
  - `harden_window(…, WindowRole::Shelf)`: level 3 (`NSFloatingWindowLevel`), the overlay's collection behavior (every Space, over full-screen apps, out of Cmd-`), `hidesOnDeactivate` off, and the `Closable` style bit (the first responder put back after the style change), so Cmd+W (the default menu's Close Window) is a close request, which empties and hides the shelf as Alt+F4 does on Windows. Order: overlay 101 > toast 100 > tool panel 99 > shelf 3 > normal windows, the order Windows ends up in. No title bar, no minimize, no zoom. Never tao's or Tauri's `set_closable`, which makes tao's own view first responder instead of the webview.
  - `show_shelf` sets the frame and calls `orderFrontRegardless`, never key and never activate, and records the app in front; `hide_shelf` orders it out and, if it was key and Grape is active, hands activation back to the app the user was last in other than Grape that still runs: `follow_app_activations` observes the workspace's `NSWorkspaceDidActivateApplicationNotification` from launch and keeps the last 8 pids. The shelf stays open while the user switches apps, so the app in front at the show is only the fallback. Its frame is read from `NSWindow.frame` in points (`window_frame`), never Tauri's `outer_position`/`outer_size` (backing pixels). A click on it activates Grape, as on Windows; Grape never does it itself.
  - Drops on it: wry accepts them as a copy and ignores them (D18), so nothing is registered and nothing is logged.
- **Drag out:** `-[NSView beginDraggingSessionWithItems:event:source:]` on the window's content view, from the async `float_drag_out` through `run_on_main_thread`.
  - One `NSDraggingItem` per item (a file URL, a directory URL for a folder, from the shelf's model with no disk access; a path that isn't absolute is left out), each with its type's icon from `iconForFileType` (deprecated, but it reads Launch Services, never the file). Files from several folders work.
  - A synthesized `LeftMouseDragged` event at the pointer, with only the timestamp taken from `NSApp.currentEvent`: WebKit consumed the real mouse-down. Electron, Chromium and drag-rs do the same.
  - Called through `msg_send!` with an optional return inside `objc2::exception::catch`: Apple documents a nil session, and the typed binding panics on it. Nil or an exception → `Failed`.
  - Grape's own `NSDraggingSource` offers Copy | Link in every context, and None once the drag is stale. It never offers Move, Generic (Finder's move on the same volume), Private or Delete (the Dock's Trash, where the source does the deleting). Modifier keys only filter: Option copies, Control or Option+Command makes an alias, Command alone refuses the drop. An operation outside Copy | Link at the end is logged as a warning.
  - The left button is read before anything is built, right before the call, and again when AppKit begins the drag one run-loop turn later; up then, the mask turns None so nothing can drop.
  - The call returns at once, so `float://drag-ended` comes from the source's `endedAt` callback: a drop on one of Grape's own windows is Cancelled (wry answers every drop there with Copy), else Copy → Copied, Link → Linked, anything else → Cancelled. The source is kept until the next drag starts, never released inside its own callback.
  - No Esc watchdog: AppKit's session doesn't hold the main thread, and Esc is AppKit's.
  - The Mac drag monitor counts Grape's own drags out: a press during which one started never becomes a wheel drag (on Windows the allowlist keeps it out, D14).
- **Why:** this is the established way to drag files out of a webview window on macOS (Electron's `DragFileItems`, Chromium's web contents view, CrabNebula's drag-rs), with Windows' copy-or-link rule kept by the source's mask. Level 3 keeps the shelf under every other Grape window and under the Dock, menu bar and other apps' menus. A plain Tauri window keeps one code path with Windows; an `NSPanel` would need swizzling or a new plugin.
- **New features, no new crate** (macOS only): objc2-app-kit 0.3.2 `NSDragging`, `NSDraggingItem`, `NSDraggingSession` (the source protocol, items, session and the call), `NSGraphicsContext` (the event constructor's parameter type) and `NSImage` (the drag image). The activation observer uses objc2-foundation's `NSNotification` and `NSDictionary`, which objc2-app-kit's `NSWorkspace` feature already turns on. objc2-app-kit is Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT and already in `Cargo.lock`, which doesn't change.
- **Tested:** `cargo test` on Linux (the mask has no Move, Delete, Generic or Private bit; the outcome mapping, the own-window rule, the own-drag rule, the icon types, the frames, the names of unexpected operations and the list of apps last activated, in `mac_rules`; the shelf model, `drag_items` and the outcome words), clippy for Linux, Windows and the Mac. CI's `macos-latest` runs the rest, which hasn't run there yet: the shelf's level order, null windows refused, `drag_out`'s early returns (nothing to drag, the button up, a null window), the `NSDragOperation` bits matching `mac_rules`', and the activation observer (refused off the main thread; through its real selector it notes another app and never Grape). No test builds a window or starts a drag. Nothing has run on a Mac.
- **Needs a Mac** (`TESTING.md` → "Float on the Mac, D43"): first, the Dock's Trash; then whether AppKit takes the synthesized event, the drops into Finder with and without keys, the own-window check, no wheel during a shelf drag, a fast flick, Esc, the first click on an inactive shelf and activation (Stage Manager), which app gets activation back after the header's ×, launch-time activation, the level over full-screen Spaces, Cmd+W and Cmd+H, the page after a drag, and the snap on two displays.
- **Revisit if:** a drop on the Trash ever removes a file (stop shipping Float on the Mac first); AppKit refuses the synthesized event; the shelf is hidden over a full-screen app or by Stage Manager (level 98); WebKit is left pressed after a drag (post a synthetic mouse-up after `endedAt`); Command alone refusing the drop confuses people (ignore modifier keys: always a copy, no alias); `iconForFileType` stops working (`iconForContentType`, a new crate); the `Closable` bit shows chrome or breaks the keys (drop it: Cmd+W then beeps); Cmd+H hides the wheel (a Grape menu without Hide); the idle webview's memory matters (build lazily and hand activation back).
  - *Amended 2026-10-28 (first Mac test review):* Cmd+H can't hide the wheel now. `harden_window` turns `canHide` off for the overlay, toast, tool panel and shelf, so the default menu's Hide (`NSApp hide:`) hides only Settings, and the four can still be ordered front while Grape is hidden. Showing Settings unhides Grape first (`macos::unhide_app`), since nothing else would. A Grape menu without Hide is still the fallback if the Mac check shows otherwise.

## D28: Lane B's image tools, slice 2: WebP, TIFF and HEIC Compress; TIFF and HEIC Remove Metadata (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** `run_job` runs D26's five "coming" image pairs:
  - Compress on WebP and TIFF, and Remove Metadata on TIFF and HEIC, with no sidecar;
  - Compress on HEIC with Grape's HEIC helper (D33).

  That makes 43 tool pairs with every sidecar instead of 38. D26's rules hold: only Remove Metadata removes metadata; ≤90 % lossy and ≤95 % exact, or `NoChange`; the content decides which code runs; outputs keep the source's literal extension. Amends D26 (its coming pairs) and D16 (libwebp, for Compress only). The numbers are in ENGINES.md → Tools. Lane B builds the slice in small commits; until a pair's commit lands, `run_job` refuses it and the app says "… is coming" (D35).
- **Remove Metadata, TIFF:**
  - A compact rewrite of every IFD: every page and SubIFD, image blocks byte for byte, the byte order and classic/BigTIFF kind kept.
  - A whitelist of layout, color, resolution and page tags, with ICC and Orientation, and every tag a libtiff 4.5 codec reads to decode (Predictor, the fax options, JPEGTables, LERC's parameters); GDAL's no-data value (42113) stays, and its GDAL_METADATA (42112) goes, with the band scale, offset and units among what it holds.
  - Photoshop's IRB is filtered down to paths, the clipping path, alpha channel names, resolution, guides and display info.
  - The DCF Adobe RGB Exif goes back when that's how the file signaled its color.
  - Exif, GPS, XMP, IPTC, GeoTIFF, Photoshop layers (37724) and every other tag are dropped and reported.
  - Tags, in both TIFF tools: every integer tag either tool decides on is read as libtiff 4.5 reads it, so that Grape sees the file libtiff sees: the layout tags, PhotometricInterpretation, FillOrder, Predictor and the Exif ColorSpace as one value of any integer type but IFD and IFD8 (signed ones not negative, a classic file's LONG8 and SLONG8 out of line); Compression, BitsPerSample and SampleFormat as that or a value for each sample (at least SamplesPerPixel, all SHORT-sized, the first that many the same); YCbCrSubsampling as two; the Exif and GPS pointers as a LONG, IFD, LONG8 or IFD8. A Compression libtiff can't read is refused by both ("… says how it's compressed in a way Grape can't read yet."). So is an IFD with entries for one of those tags (or the strips, tiles, counts and SubIFDs) that aren't all the same, as libtiff reads the first and Pillow the last: that message for Compression, "… says how its image data is laid out in a way Grape can't read yet." for the others; the same entry twice is read as one. Strip and tile offsets and counts are read as libtiff reads them too: any integer type but IFD and IFD8 (BYTE and signed ones with libtiff's warning), signed ones not negative; typed IFD or IFD8, or negative, libtiff can't read the file, and it's damaged.
  - Refused: DNG, old-style JPEG, metadata inside JPEG strips or in a JPEGTables (any IFD's, read as libtiff reads it: a byte for each value of any integer type but IFD and IFD8, or of ASCII or UNDEFINED), JPEG strips it can't check for metadata ("… has JPEG data Grape can't check for metadata yet.": segments or scans past the 64 KiB it reads, a hierarchical JPEG, or JPEGTables libtiff can't read as bytes), metadata inside WebP strips (an EXIF or XMP chunk; a strip that isn't a RIFF container covering it, but for a pad byte, or a bare VP8 or VP8L bitstream, or whose chunks run past the first 64, "… has WebP data Grape can't check for metadata yet."; a strip JPEG and WebP pages both list is read as each), the data of any codec whose stream may hold metadata Grape doesn't read: an allowlist copies only None, CCITT RLE, Group 3 and Group 4, LZW, Deflate, PackBits, NeXT, ThunderScan, PixarLog, SGILog, LERC, LZMA, ZSTD and JBIG data, whose streams hold nothing a reader reads but the picture (a ZSTD stream's skippable frames and a JBIG stream's comments, which their decoders skip, as bytes past any stream's end), reads every code that holds JPEG streams (7, DNG's 34892 outside a DNG, 33007, 99) as JPEG and both WebP codes (50001, 34927) as WebP, and refuses the rest, naming JPEG 2000, JPEG XL (52546 too), JPEG XR and PNG ("… has JPEG 2000 data …", "… has JPEG XL data …", "… has JPEG XR data …", "… has PNG data …") and calling any other "image data" ("… has image data Grape can't check for metadata yet."), and ImageJ/OME files, whose layout lives in their description.
  - Byte counts, in both TIFF tools: those libtiff 4.5 ignores ("Bogus" or "Wrong" StripByteCounts: a lone strip at a nonzero offset counted 0, or, uncompressed, past the file's end or short of libtiff's figure for it, its scanline times ImageLength (for subsampled YCbCr less than the strip holds: a count between the two is taken at its word, and refused as below); more than two uncompressed strips or tiles in a plane whose first two counts differ and aren't 0, checked on the entries libtiff reads: as many as its own count of strips or tiles, 0 for any missing, as the kind it reads them as, whichever tags list them) are worked out from the picture, each block sized exactly (from BitsPerSample's first SamplesPerPixel values, as libtiff reads them), where libtiff gives each strip ImageLength over its count of strips rows, rounded down, and each tile a full tile; where the file lists more or fewer entries than libtiff counts, they're refused as below, as the blocks Grape copies and the picture libtiff reads may differ, even where the listed strips happen to cover libtiff's one strip exactly (ten of 3,000 bytes back to back with no RowsPerStrip, which libtiff reads as the same 30,000 bytes: telling those apart isn't worth the code). libtiff's count, and Grape's sizes and Compress's layout with it, come from the layout tags read as libtiff reads them (any integer type but IFD and IFD8, signed ones not negative, a classic file's LONG8 out of line; tiles whenever there's a TileWidth or TileLength, a size left out set from a RowsPerStrip listed before them as libtiff sets it (the TileLength its rows, the TileWidth the ImageWidth), counted deep by ImageDepth over TileDepth, which strips ignore), and more than one entry where libtiff can't read those tags, and uncompressed data, a lone strip's too, that Grape can't size as libtiff does (a layout libtiff can't read or count, or a BitsPerSample, PhotometricInterpretation or YCbCrSubsampling it can't read), are refused however right their counts ("… says how its image data is laid out in a way Grape can't read yet."), as which blocks libtiff reads, or what readers that size the data themselves read from it, can't be checked. Those it takes at their word that Pillow and ImageMagick read past (0 bytes at a nonzero offset; an uncompressed block short of the picture), and those it ignores that Grape can't work out (compressed data, or blocks listed for a count other than libtiff's), are refused before anything is written ("… says the wrong size for some of its image data, which Grape can't rewrite yet."). 0 bytes at 0 stays empty (GDAL's sparse tiles).
  - The output is re-read before publishing.
  - Known limits, for both TIFF tools: a Compression, or an uncompressed IFD's sizing tags (the layout, BitsPerSample, PhotometricInterpretation, YCbCrSubsampling), that libtiff can't read is refused, even where libtiff then reads the file by its defaults (a PhotometricInterpretation it ignores, a YCbCrSubsampling it takes as 2 × 2), as other readers may not; a SubIFDs entry typed SHORT, which libtiff ignores, is followed. Blocks that overlap each other (but for one listed twice), an IFD or a tag's value out of line (any IFD's, Exif, GPS and Interop included) are refused as not a readable TIFF image, though libtiff, Pillow and ImageMagick read such a file; blocks worked out from the picture that would are refused as wrong-size, as libtiff reads those bytes (an XMP packet, say) as pixels; an Exif, GPS or Interop IFD Grape can't read (one claiming over 4,096 entries, say) isn't checked, so one inside a strip or tile is kept there, as the pixels every reader reads it as.
- **Remove Metadata, HEIC:**
  - `meta` is rebuilt in place without its Exif, XMP, IPTC and other metadata items (any item but a picture that describes another), or the `udes`/`crtt`/`mdft` properties (`ipma` renumbered).
  - A top-level `free` box takes up the bytes saved, so no offset changes; a `meta` that ends the file (Samsung's) ends it sooner instead. Removed bytes, and every byte of `mdat` and `idat` no kept item points to, are zeroed (other fields when any isn't). An `iloc` entry with no item (no `infe`) goes, the bytes it points to no item's; one a kept item refers to by anything but a `cdsc` (data in it, a picture made of it) is refused.
  - XMP and C2PA `uuid` boxes become zeroed `free` boxes; the `uuid`, `xml ` and `bxml` boxes directly in `meta` (a Canon HIF's encoder version) leave it. Trailing motion-photo data and Samsung's `sefd` trailer (capture time, mobile country code) are cut.
  - The primary's Exif, when its Orientation is 2 to 8, stays with that Orientation alone, as D26's minimal Exif for JPEG: its entries byte for byte, its one extent overwritten in place, zeros after, whether or not `irot`/`imir` turn the picture too (Samsung and iOS 11 portraits have only Exif's). One that can't take it in place, or that a kept item refers to by anything but a `cdsc` (a picture made of it, data in it), is refused.
  - Every image item (thumbnails, alpha, depth, gain maps, `tmap`) and every other property stays byte for byte; kept data that names another data reference counts as this file's, as libheif reads it.
  - No helper and no decode. Sequences and layouts it can't rewrite safely are refused.
  - Known limits: SEI inside HEVC, APP1, APP13 and COM segments inside a JPEG-coded item (a thumbnail; no real sample has them), and `uuid`, `xml ` and `bxml` boxes deeper in `meta` than its own (inside an unknown box of `meta`'s or `dinf`, beside `ipco` and `ipma` in `iprp`, or as an `ipco` property; Apple's spatial and stereo photos have `uuid` properties that must stay), stay, unreported; HEIF's low-overhead `mini` form is refused as "… isn't a readable HEIC image."; only the first Exif that describes the primary is read for the orientation, IFD0's SHORT Orientation of 1 to 8, the last of two, as for JPEG (LONG, IFD1 only, 0 or 9 dropped).
- **Compress, TIFF:**
  - Lossless at every preset.
  - Each None, LZW, Deflate or PackBits strip or tile is re-deflated with zlib at a level by how much the file has to recode (speed, below), with horizontal differencing added for 8- and 16-bit integer samples (photometric 0, 1, 2 or 5, sample format 1 or 2, rows of at most 64 MiB) unless an LZW or Deflate source has a predictor of its own, which stays with its bytes. The layout and every other tag stay; FreeOffsets and FreeByteCounts go.
  - Each block is read back through a second handle, inflated and checked against the source's CRC32, and the output's IFDs are walked again, before publishing.
  - Its tags are read as libtiff reads them (Remove Metadata's Tags, above); a compressed IFD with one it decides on that libtiff can't read, and other readers might read otherwise (a FillOrder, Predictor, PhotometricInterpretation, BitsPerSample or YCbCrSubsampling), is copied as it is, and so is an uncompressed one with such a FillOrder, Predictor or SampleFormat; an uncompressed one whose sizing tags libtiff can't read is refused with its file, as Remove Metadata's Byte counts say.
  - An IFD Grape can't decode (old-style LZW, broken or short data), whose recode writes more bytes than a copy of it would (each block it lists once, unless it's copied already), or whose recode takes the output past 95 % of the source, is taken back and copied as it is, the copy held to 95 % too. Other codecs, FillOrder 2, blocks listed as strips that libtiff reads as tiles (by a TileWidth or TileLength) or as tiles it reads as strips, tiles more than one slice deep (TileDepth), strips whose RowsPerStrip is 2^31 or more but isn't 2^32 − 1, which Pillow reads uncompressed and fails on as Deflate, an IFD whose PhotometricInterpretation libtiff doesn't know (not 0 to 6, 8 to 10, 32803, 32844 or 32845), and an IFD with empty (sparse) strips or tiles, which as Deflate the tiff crate and Pillow fail on, are copied as they are. An entry typed IFD or IFD8 that Grape doesn't follow is refused ("… has data Compress can't move yet.").
  - Ends `NoChange` before anything is created when there's nothing to recode, and as soon as the output passes 95 % (an IFD being recoded is copied as it is first, if that fits). The detail says first, and always, which IFD took the output past 95 % and how: copied as it was to be, and why; copied instead of recoded, and why; or both recoded and copied. Then why each IFD it recoded, or tried to, was taken back and copied as it is, every one; then why each other IFD was copied as it is, the first 16 and how many more. When the finished file passes 95 %, the detail starts with those taken back the same way.
  - Speed (the design's Q10, decided 2026-10-29): the level comes from the decoded bytes Compress recodes in the whole file (a block listed more than once counted once for each recipe it's recoded by, its codec, decoded size, row's bytes and added predictor, as it's recoded once for each), known from the model before anything is written: 9 for up to 16 MiB, 6 for up to 256 MiB, and 3 past that, at every preset. Level 9 took 247 s on a 1.08 GB uncompressed RGB scan (4.4 MB/s), against the design's bar of 90 s for 1 GiB; past 256 MiB the level must run at 18 MB/s or more, so that 1 GiB stays under about a minute. Levels 1 to 5 did, on photo-like scans, and 3 wrote the least of them, faster than 4 and 5, which wrote more (miniz_oxide's 4 and 5 parse lazily with fewer probes than 6). Measured in a Linux release build on 4 cores (decoded MB/s, and the output's share of the source; the photo-like scans are a real 8000 × 6000 photo and a 12 MP iPhone one, tiled, uncompressed in strips of 32 rows):

    | Level | Photo-like, 256 MiB | iPhone photo-like, 128 MiB | ImageMagick `plasma:`, 256 MiB |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | 53.4 MB/s, 45.7 % | 59.8 MB/s, 46.7 % | 59.1 MB/s, 59.9 % |
    | 2 | 49.5 MB/s, 42.4 % | 50.0 MB/s, 43.1 % | 32.5 MB/s, 63.8 % |
    | 3 | 32.2 MB/s, 39.9 % | 29.9 MB/s, 42.0 % | 22.4 MB/s, 64.0 % |
    | 4 | 28.0 MB/s, 42.1 % | 29.1 MB/s, 44.4 % | 19.6 MB/s, 63.5 % |
    | 5 | 21.0 MB/s, 41.0 % | 23.7 MB/s, 43.3 % | 19.5 MB/s, 63.5 % |
    | 6 | 10.5 MB/s, 39.0 % | 12.6 MB/s, 40.8 % | 20.0 MB/s, 63.5 % |
    | 9 | 3.4 MB/s, 36.7 % | | 19.5 MB/s, 63.5 % |

    A 1.08 GB photo-like scan (18,000 × 20,000) took 32.8 s at level 3 (32.9 MB/s), to 39.6 % of its size. Blocks deflated on several threads at once went under Revisit.
  - Known limits: lossless only (JPEG, fax and JPEG 2000 pages gain nothing); a compressed IFD with a tag it decides on that libtiff can't read (an uncompressed one only for its FillOrder, Predictor or SampleFormat), or tiles more than one slice deep, is copied as it is; strips keep their size; past 16 MiB to recode the file is deflated at level 6, and past 256 MiB at level 3, a few percent larger than the level above it would make it; each strip or tile costs about 14 µs however small (Linux release build), half of it the deflater's reset (miniz_oxide clears about 300 KB for each zlib stream, flate2 has no lighter reset, and a new deflater costs 74 µs) and a quarter the inflater's that reads it back, so a million one-row strips of 32 bytes take 15.0 s and 4,194,304 (the cap) 60.7 s; a MakerNote with offsets from the file's start, and unknown LONG tags that were really offsets, go stale; why an IFD was copied as it is reaches the log only when the job fails or ends `NoChange`, since an engine has no log for a job that succeeds; Pillow (12.3 and 13) misreads big-endian 32-bit float and int32 samples in any compressed TIFF (libtiff hands them back in the machine's order, which Pillow reads as the file's; `tiffcp -B -c zip` makes such a file too), so a big-endian uncompressed float or int32 page that Compress turns into Deflate looks wrong in Pillow only, where libtiff, ImageMagick and Grape's own decoder read it right.
- **Compress, WebP:**
  - Lossy sources: image-webp decodes, Grape scales, then libwebp 1.6.0 at method 6 (≤2048 q70 / ≤4096 q80 / full q85, alpha lossless).
  - Lossless sources: lossless at every preset (`-z 9`, exact), checked pixel for pixel.
  - ICCP, EXIF (dimensions patched) and XMP are carried in a VP8X container Grape writes.
  - Animated files are refused.
- **Compress, HEIC:**
  - The helper decodes upright 8-bit pixels; Grape scales (≤2048 / ≤4096 / full ≤35.65 MP); the helper encodes at libheif q40/50/60 or ImageIO 0.60/0.70/0.80 (provisional).
  - Grape grafts back the source's Exif (Orientation 1, dimensions patched, a sideways IFD1 unlinked), its XMP (Orientation 1) and other metadata items. Every run checks D33's three `out.heic` invariants first (one file-level `meta`; every `iloc` item at construction method 0 or 1; the helper's own metadata items found and replaced); when one doesn't hold, the job is `EncodeFailed` ("Grape couldn't write a HEIC file.") and nothing is published.
  - Gain maps, depth, mattes, thumbnails, motion-photo video and C2PA are dropped, as D26's JPEG drops them.
  - Refused: 10-bit, wide `nclx` the helper hands back without a profile, a second picture, non-square pixels, sequences, and High over one HEVC picture ("Try Balanced").
  - A Mac whose ImageIO doesn't list HEIC is told before anything is decoded; one that lists it but can't encode is told after the decode.
- **API:** `imagetool::runs_tool(tool, from, heif)`. No new `MetaField` or `Note`.
- **New dependencies:**
  - libwebp-sys =0.14.4: crate MIT, published 2026-04-29; vendors libwebp 1.6.0, BSD-3-Clause with Google's patent grant; `default-features = false, features = ["std"]`; +1 lock package.
  - Grape's own `grape-webp` crate (MIT), which holds the only `unsafe` for images and calls only the encoder.
  - weezl 0.1.12 is a direct dependency, for TIFF Compress's LZW decoding with TIFF's early code-size switch (MIT OR Apache-2.0; already locked through image's tiff, so no new package).
  - tiff 0.11.3 as a dev-dependency (MIT, already locked).
  - `deny.toml` and `about.toml` clarify libwebp-sys as MIT AND BSD-3-Clause; `about.hbs` prints `PATENTS`. A build must never set `TARGET_CPU`.
- **Lane A:** `heif::Encoder` (with `max_picture`, `check_encode` and `encode`), `Decoder::decode_within`, `other_heif` made `pub(crate)`, a fake helper that writes real HEIF and echoes the quality, four fixtures, the app tests that used TIFF as "coming", and CI's heif-it cases. The protocol stays at 2 (D41).
- **Not chosen:**
  - webp 0.3.1 (libwebp 1.3.1, before CVE-2023-4863's fix);
  - webpx (4 more crates);
  - zenwebp (AGPL);
  - protocol v2 metadata fields (ImageIO's Exif path is lossy, and the two OSes would differ);
  - re-striping TIFF;
  - piecewise TIFF relocation;
  - an allow-list of auxiliary-image XMP;
  - compacting HEIC;
  - scaling a 48 MP HEIC quietly at High.
- **Status 2026-10-29:** designed. Remove Metadata runs on TIFF and on HEIC, and Compress on TIFF (41 tool pairs with every sidecar); Compress on WebP and on HEIC is coming. Each pair's commit adds its line here.
- **Tested:** Remove Metadata on TIFF: unit tests on files the tests build (the reader in both byte orders and kinds, every cap, a cut at every byte; the writer, which the tiff crate decodes back to the same samples; the plan's report, IRB filter, DCF Exif, refusals and self-check), and `tests/image_tools.rs` through `run_job`. On Linux, 28 real files from Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 (LZW, Deflate with and without a predictor, PackBits, JPEG, none; II and MM; classic and BigTIFF; tiles; up to 10 pages; 8 and 16-bit and float; SubIFDs grafted on; Exif with GPS and Interop, XMP, IPTC, a Photoshop IRB, ICC, GeoTIFF, the DCF signal and the text tags): the ImageJ one refused, the 27 others with every page pixel-identical in both readers and every block byte-identical, only the kept tags, the IRB down to its kept resources, the DCF Exif where it belonged, and none of the dropped text. A 144 MB file took 1.6 s (debug build) in 11 MiB, and one at the cap of 4,194,304 strips peaked at 148 MB, its self-check holding the plan's block list and the output's (279 MB when it held four), and at 160 MiB of heap at most when its strips repeat further on (2.50 models; 2.75 with every strip at one byte, 3.00 with each byte two strips half the file apart, and 5.16 with each byte two strips in a row, when the plan held the repeats through the self-check, as hash sets at first, and the sorted references grew as they were collected); `tests/tiff_heap.rs` keeps a million and two strips (just past a power of two) under 2.25 times the model's heap in the file's order, last first and all at one byte (2.01 measured), and under 2.75 when each byte is two strips, in a row or half the file apart, or two bytes take turns (2.26, 2.50 and 2.50; 2.51 and 3.01 for the first two while the list of repeated blocks grew by doubling, before it was counted first), and 32 pages that share a 1 MiB block of Photoshop's resources under 4 MiB (3.10 times the block measured, where each page held its own filtered copy before), and four pages with their own 3 MiB blocks under 6.5 blocks (6.02 measured, 9.02 while the self-check held what it filtered; 16 pages of 4 MiB peaked at 72 MiB, 132 MiB before, and are refused now, at 24 MiB, with 16 MiB kept), and a page and its three SubIFDs, each with its own 3 MiB block, too (6.02; 9.02 while the self-check held the SubIFDs' filtered resources until it was done with their page, and a page with 16 SubIFDs of 1 MiB peaked at 18.10 MiB, 33.10 before), and 40 pages that name one 1 MiB block, each 2 bytes more of it, refused as too large once more is read than the file holds, under 18 MiB (2.09 measured; 16.09 while the cap was 16 MiB read in all, 41.09 when each page's filtered copy was held until the writer refused the file, and 176 MiB and 656 MiB with a 16 MiB block named 10 and 40 ways, 32 MiB now). The cap counts what's kept, so 40 pages with their own 522 KB blocks (a resolution, a path and a 500 KB thumbnail) are stripped, in 1.43 MiB (refused while it counted what was read); at it, one 16 MiB path peaks at 48 MiB, and a second page with its own is refused at 48 MiB too. 4,096 pages whose 8 text tags are one blank 64 KiB value are planned in 0.09 s, reading 1.3 MB (9.6 s and 2.1 GB when the report read a blank value again for each entry), and 256 pages of 4,088 Software tags, each a blank 5-byte value before the last, are stripped in 2.47 s, reading 17.9 MB (5.52 s and 62 GiB when each value read refilled the 64 KiB read buffer); 4,194,304 one-byte strips listed last first are stripped in 9.72 s, reading 71 MB (19.12 s and 254 GiB when each strip did; as JPEG strips, scanned too, 14.04 s and 76 MB, 32.98 s and 508 GiB before), and in the file's order in 8.74 s (7.03 s through the buffer); 65,536 pages whose IFDs are laid out last first are stripped in 2.65 s, reading 11.9 MB (2.74 s and 4.0 GiB through the buffer). With JPEG strips read through every scan unless the first is a sequential frame's only one, 7 JPEG TIFFs from Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 (one 540 KB strip, three planes, 32 strips, gray) still came out pixel-identical in Pillow, and of progressive strips (Pillow's, in a TIFF made for them) an 8 KB one was stripped pixel-identical, the same with an APP1 between its scans refused, and a 505 KB one refused. A baseline strip with 2 stray bytes between its APP0 and an APP1, which libjpeg warns of and reads past, is refused (it was stripped with its Exif left in). Remove Metadata on HEIC: unit tests on files `isobmff::tests::Builder` builds (an iPhone-like grid in `idat` with tiles, a thumbnail, Exif, a gain map with its own XMP, a depth map and a tone map grouped with the grid; `meta` after `mdat`; `iloc` v0, v1 and v2 with 32-bit ids; 64-bit fields and base offsets; several extents; Exif in `idat`; two Exif items; `iloc` first; a `largesize` and a size-0 `meta`; `udes`, `crtt` and `mdft` among the primary's properties; IPTC, a region annotation and Exif under another type, each describing the primary; XMP and C2PA `uuid` boxes, a trailing `mpvd`, trailing zeros and junk; `uuid` (Canon's and XMP's), `xml ` and `bxml` boxes in `meta`, each alone, and empty ones: no bytes, or none past a `uuid`'s user type or an `xml ` or `bxml` box's version and flags, which were reported): the model's every cap, a cut at every byte and junk at every byte of `meta`; the plan's kept bytes, zeroed ranges, report, `ipma` renumbering, groups, a primary at data reference 1 (kept, checked, and an Exif over it refused), bytes of `mdat` and `idat` no item points to, a portrait Exif (Orientation 6) in every layout kept with that Orientation alone in Apple's and Samsung's header forms (refused in several extents, in too few bytes, in another item or file, over kept data, or when a kept item refers to it by anything but a `cdsc`: a grid made of it, a JPEG thumbnail or another item whose data is in it, a `thmb`, `auxl`, `prem`, `base` or other reference; damaged, before a byte of it is read, when an extent passes its file, or they add up past any number; with 1 MiB, 64 KiB and a byte after its header, in `mdat` and in `idat`, its zeros past the first MiB pass the check, which failed them as other fields left), what follows an Exif's first MiB (read across 20 extents, and stopped by a cancel at its next 64 KiB) reported as other fields only when a byte of it isn't zero, so a second pass over a portrait one finds nothing (it found other fields while that went unread), an `iloc` entry with no item (an Exif's, its `infe` renamed) in `mdat` and in `idat` dropped, its bytes zeroed and reported as other fields unless they're zeros (it was kept, its "SecretMake" with it, unreported), and refused when a kept item leans on it (its data in it, a grid made of it, a `thmb`, `auxl`, `prem`, `base` or other reference; a `cdsc` only loses it), every refusal, a cut at every byte of a file (refused, or stripped and checked) and the self-check against planted changes; and `tests/image_tools.rs` through `run_job` (libheif's and an iPhone's box order, `.heic`, `.HEIC`, `.heif`, `.hif`, AVIF content named `.heic`, a HEIC named `.jpg`, a motion photo, a portrait photo, a file with none, the refusals, a cut file, a cancel before the read and during the copy). On Linux, real files, each output decoded by libheif 1.23.6 (pillow-heif 1.8.1) to the same pixels as its source in every image, thumbnail and auxiliary image (an iPhone 15 Pro photo's HDR gain map among them), with no XMP or metadata item left, no Exif but an Orientation-only one where the photo turns, and none of the planted strings: 8 files pillow-heif wrote (Exif with GPS, XMP, thumbnails, three images with their own Exif and XMP, a primary that isn't first, alpha, 10-bit); an iPhone 15 Pro (a 48-tile grid, a thumbnail, a gain map with its own XMP, Exif) and an iPhone 7 photo and a Samsung motion photo (`meta` after `mdat`; its 2.4 MB `mpvd` cut and reported as embedded media) from PhotoPrism's samples, and the iPhone 15 Pro one with XMP and C2PA `uuid` boxes, an `mpvd`, junk, zeros or a `free` box added at its end; the 63 files of the HEIF conformance suite (nokiatech/heif_conformance): 45 stripped (43 compared; libheif can't decode the other two's sources), C050's `udes` and C051's `crtt` and `mdft` reported, 17 sequences refused, and C034 refused as laid out in a way Remove Metadata can't handle, its Exif's extent overlapping the primary's last 4 bytes; libheif's example HEIC and its AVIF named `.heic`. With the HEIC fixes of 2026-10-29, 157 real files (those, and the HEIC test files of metadata-extractor, Exiv2, pillow-heif, ExifTool, c2pa-rs and SDWebImage) and libheif's fuzzing corpus (134) ran again: of the 157, 128 stripped, 19 sequences, C034 and 9 damaged files refused; the 122 libheif decodes (6 whose primary libheif can't decode aside) came out the same in every image, thumbnail, auxiliary and depth image, 200MP.heic's primary, thumbnail and gain map among them; 13 outputs from 10 distinct files (the iPhone 15 Pro, iPhone 7 and motion-photo samples are among the 157 twice) kept an Orientation-only Exif in Apple's or Samsung's form (an iPhone 7 on iOS 11, which has no `irot`; a Samsung Galaxy S24 FE, whose `irot` is blanked to `free`; the iPhone 15 Pro; the Samsung motion photo, now ending with its `meta`; 200MP.heic); pillow-heif's IPTC item and its Exif under the type `E\xEAif` went, and a Canon HIF's 1 KB that no item points to, and the 16-byte `mdat` Nokia's writer ends its files with (`NHW_0001`), were zeroed and reported; that Canon HIF's `uuid` box in `meta` (`CNCV`, its encoder's version) goes too, reported as other fields, and none of its camera's strings is left. The fuzzing corpus never panics, and what it strips passes its own check; a second pass over each of the 224 outputs, the corpus's too, finds nothing and copies it byte for byte. A 15 MB file took 36 ms and a 29 MB one 68 ms (debug build). After the fixes that followed (a portrait Exif's extents judged before it's read, one a kept item leans on refused, its zeros past 1 MiB, `meta`'s `uuid` boxes), all 293 files ran again with the same counts (and libheif's two `mini` files, named `.heif`, refused as unreadable): the 222 HEIF outputs decode as their sources do (the other 2 are one JPEG named `.heic`, twice), the 13 Exifs hold an Orientation of 6 alone, and a second pass over each of the 224 finds nothing and copies it byte for byte. After the next (an Exif's bytes past its first MiB read, an `iloc` entry with no item dropped, `meta`'s boxes that hold nothing unreported), all 293 ran again with the same counts and the same reports; 223 outputs came out byte for byte as before, and one changed: a metadata-extractor sample's `iloc` entry for id 0, with no `infe` and no extents, left it, and it decodes as before. A second pass over each of the 224 again finds nothing and copies it byte for byte. `tests/heic_heap.rs` bounds the peak itself, in MiB, since a model is several times the bytes it's read from, more so the denser they are: a `meta` of data just under 16 MiB (an `idat`) under 36 MiB (32.06 measured, twice it; 6.00 times it while the rebuilt `meta` was copied twice and its `free` header doubled the buffer it went in), one as large packed at every cap under 80 MiB (73.25 measured, 76 in a review's own build of such a file; 4.74 times a 15 MiB one, 8.60 before, and 5.23 while the check held what had been written), one of 9.75 MiB packed as densely as the model lets it (7-bit property indices, no groups) under 64 MiB (55.88 measured, 5.73 times it; 59 MiB for a review's 9.6 MiB one), and one of 3.8 MiB whose 262,132 one-byte extents each leave a byte after them under 25 MiB (22.94 MiB, those bytes zeroed as the copy goes; 32.69 when each was an edit of its own). Compress on TIFF: unit tests on files the tests build and the tiff crate's (`codec.rs`: each codec's strips decoded as the tiff crate decodes them, PackBits' literals, runs and no-ops, data short of a block or past it, old-style LZW, steps of 256 KiB or of whole rows, the predictor in both byte orders against the tiff crate's, strips, tiles, planes and subsampled YCbCr sized as libtiff sizes them, layout tags it can't read, a block recoded and read back to the same CRC32, a broken encoder caught; `compress.rs`: five pages of the tiff crate's and big-endian 16-bit, planar, tiled, float and SubIFD pages decoded by the tiff crate to the same samples, a stray predictor on None and PackBits, FillOrder 2, JPEG, fax and unknown codecs copied byte for byte, an IFD with junk, cut or old-style LZW in a strip taken back and copied, Exif, GPS, Interop and every other tag moved with its value, an unfollowed IFD-typed entry refused, blocks listed twice and sparse tiles, the 95 % stop, a broken encoder, a cancel within a block, and the output's check against a changed byte count and a lost IFD); and `tests/image_tools.rs` through `run_job` (classic and BigTIFF, the three presets making the same file, `Scan.TIF`, a TIFF named `.png`, `NoChange` for JPEG pages and for its own output, the DNG and IFD-typed-entry refusals, a cut file, a cancel before the walk and while strips are deflated). On Linux, 49 real files from Pillow 12.3, ImageMagick 6.9.12 and Python (none, LZW and Deflate with and without a predictor, PackBits, JPEG, G4, 1-bit, palette, CMYK, RGBA, 16-bit in both byte orders, float with and without predictor 3, tiles, planes, one-row strips, FillOrder 2, YCbCr 1 × 1 and subsampled 2 × 2, BigTIFF in both byte orders, multi-page files mixing LZW, JPEG, G4, Deflate, PackBits and none, an LZW strip with junk in it, and Exif, GPS and Interop IFDs, XMP, IPTC, a Photoshop IRB, ICC, GeoTIFF and a SubIFD added to five of them): 34 were compressed, to between 15 % and 93 % of their size, and 15 ended `NoChange`, 5 with nothing to recode (JPEG, G4, FillOrder 2) before anything was written and 10 at 95 % or past it (most of them Deflate already). Every page and SubIFD of the 34 decodes to the same pixels in Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12, and in the tiff crate where it reads the source; every tag of every IFD has the same value; the JPEG, G4 and damaged LZW pages are the source's byte for byte; no source changed. (Pillow 13 reads an uncompressed YCbCr 1 × 1 source as YCbCr samples but its Deflate output through libtiff's RGBA path, as RGB; ImageMagick and the tiff crate decode the two the same.) A 1.08 GB uncompressed RGB scan (18,000 × 20,000, strips of 32 rows) came to 42 % of its size in 247 s in a Linux release build, peaking at 11 MiB. Byte counts (2026-10-29 review): libtiff 4.5.1 (`tiffinfo -s`, `tiffcp`) on 20 files Python wrote set the rules, its `tif_dirread.c` read alongside; the review's cases (a lone gray 300 × 100 strip, every byte there, counted 0 or 15,000, and ten strips counted 3,000 and 1,500 in turn), for which both tools had published a file of no picture (122 bytes, from Remove Metadata), half the picture and the rest garbage, or a garbled one, now come out whole, read by libtiff without a warning and by Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 as they read the source (so does a 300 × 95 picture in ten such strips, which libtiff can't read at all), and the cases it trusts and the others read past (a strip of 0 bytes, or short, after two that agree, or of two) are refused; unit tests for each rule in the reader and both tools, and the refusal through `run_job`. A block shared by strips of different sizes (a 200 × 950 picture whose ten strips list one 20,000-byte block, the last of 50 rows), by pages of different heights, or by a page that's taken back and one that's recoded, either way round, failed as too large, counted against the source each time it was written, and the next page wrote it again; it's counted once now, and each way it's written is kept (a third page reuses the second's). A page that grew recoded (the review's, libdeflate's level 6 with a predictor, went from 67,196 to 68,967 bytes) was written recoded; it's taken back and copied as it is now (a unit test with a page of noise). Compress's heap: a million one-row strips peaked at 2.09 times the model's heap (16 bytes a strip; the writer's offsets and sizes are as much again), the same listed last first, and 3.34 with each block listed twice (40 bytes a repeated block), and 65,536 one-strip pages at 119.6 MiB; `tests/tiff_heap.rs` holds 131,074 strips under 2.5 models in the file's order and last first and 3.75 in pairs (2.19, 2.19 and 3.44 measured, the codecs' 1.1 MB taken off), and the 65,536 pages under 112 MiB (99.56 measured; 119.56, and under 128 MiB, before the change below). Its heap for each IFD entry (2026-10-30 review): 256 pages of 4,096 entries, 1,046,784 of them RATIONALs out of line, which Compress copies as they are, peaked at 234 MiB, about 230 bytes an entry, where Remove Metadata holds 56: the writer looked up every value out of line in a map, to write one that entries share once, and grew each IFD's entries by doubling. It looks up only the values more than one entry copies now, found by sorting their places, and lays out each IFD's entries at their number: 97.3 MiB, about 97 bytes an entry (the field to write, 40, the writer's entry, 40, and where its value goes, 16), which `tests/tiff_heap.rs` holds under 112 MiB. 4,194,304 one-byte strips, each byte listed twice, peaked at 209.4 MiB, the two in one file at 273.3 MiB, and every cap at once (65,536 pages of 16 entries, 8 of them RATIONALs, and 64 one-byte strips, each byte listed twice) at 315.4 MiB in 364 s (debug build), against 216.9 MiB for Remove Metadata. Two strips that share a byte, which libtiff 4.5.1, Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 read, are refused by both tools as not a readable TIFF image (a known limit, as before). The level's tiers: a unit test on their boundaries, and on a plan's level (bytes copied as they are don't count). Why IFDs were copied as they are reached the job's detail only when the finished file ended `NoChange`; it comes with every `NoChange` and failure after the plan now, the 95 % stop while writing among them (through `run_job`), and with every way the writer stops. A cancel that landed in the temp file's last flush ended Compress as a failed write; it's a cancel now, through the closing step Remove Metadata shares, whose own flush already ended as one (a unit test cancels with bytes still buffered). A page whose four 32 × 32 tiles were listed under StripOffsets beside a TileWidth and TileLength, which libtiff 4.5.1, Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 read as tiles, was recoded as strips of 16 rows, and each of them read 2,048 of its 4,096 pixels wrong; it's copied as it is now, and so are tiles listed with no tile size, which libtiff reads as strips (through `run_job`). A one-page Deflate file whose recode and copy both passed 95 % (`tiffcp -c zip:2` or ImageMagick's `-compress Zip` make them) ended `NoChange` saying its page was copied as it is, though nothing was made; the detail says it passed 95 % recoded and copied as it is too now (through `run_job`). An RGB file whose BitsPerSample lists four values, which libtiff reads as its first three, was refused as wrong-size by both tools, which sized its strips from all four; they're sized from the first three now, and both tools' outputs read back as the source does in libtiff 4.5.1, Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12. Tag types (2026-10-30 review): both tools read a tag they decide on only as a single unsigned value inline, where libtiff reads more, so they and libtiff saw different files: a gray JPEG strip holding an Exif APP1 with a GPS IFD, its Compression 7 a SSHORT, was published by Remove Metadata with the GPS in it (refused as a SHORT); a Compression of 1 typed SSHORT, SLONG, SBYTE, LONG8 (out of line) or SLONG8, or two SHORTs of 1, kept libtiff's "Bogus" and "Wrong" rules from running, and so did a BitsPerSample typed BYTE, SBYTE, SSHORT, SLONG or SLONG8, which also skipped the short-strip refusal, and both tools published damaged files. Every tag either tool decides on is read as libtiff 4.5.1 reads it now (`tif_dirread.c` read alongside, and `tiffinfo` on typed files: a SSHORT, LONG8 or three SHORTs of 1 read as None, two values for three samples "Incorrect count", PackBits' 32773 as a SSHORT "Incorrect value", a RATIONAL "Incompatible type"), and a Compression libtiff can't read is refused; unit tests for each reader, and each case through `run_job`. Blocks listed as the other kind (TileOffsets with no tile size, which libtiff reads as strips; StripOffsets beside a TileWidth, which it reads as tiles) weren't sized, so libtiff's rules and the short-block refusal didn't run on them: the round-2 file with its strips under TileOffsets, and a lone 90 × 90 strip there counted 10, were published, the second as 10 bytes ("got 88 bytes, expected 8100" in libtiff); they're sized as the kind libtiff reads them as now, the first refused and the second worked out to the picture, which libtiff reads without a warning (unit tests, and `tiffinfo` on each: libtiff's "Wrong" rule runs on four tiles listed as strips too). A lone uncompressed strip whose layout libtiff can't count (a RowsPerStrip of 2^32 − 2, a lone TileWidth) wasn't sized, so a 300 × 100 picture's strip counted 10 was published as 10 bytes, where Pillow read the whole picture from the source and failed on the output; uncompressed blocks Grape can't size are refused now, a lone strip's too (unit tests, and each case through `run_job`). libtiff 4.5.1 sets a RowsPerStrip listed before the tile sizes as the tile's too (`_TIFFVSetField`: the TileLength its rows, the TileWidth the ImageWidth set by then), which Grape counted as no tiles: a gray 64 × 64 page in four strips of 16 rows with a TileLength of 16 and no TileWidth, which libtiff, Pillow 12.3 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 read as four tiles of 64 × 16, the same bytes, was refused as wrong-size by both tools. Grape sets it as libtiff does now, in the IFD's order (`tiffinfo` on the file reads tiles of 64 × 16, and with the RowsPerStrip listed after the TileLength "Cannot handle zero number of tiles", as Grape counts it): Remove Metadata's output reads the same in all three, and Compress copies the page as it is (unit tests on the order, and the file through `run_job`). A volume's tiles (ImageDepth 2, TileDepth 2: four 32 × 32 tiles of a 64 × 64 page, 2,048 bytes each) were recoded at libtiff's tile size, one slice's, so Compress kept the first slice of each and dropped the second (8,358 bytes to 402); tiles more than one slice deep are copied as they are now, and ImageDepth 2 in tiles one slice deep is recoded as before (a unit test reads each tile back). The detail of a `NoChange` kept 16 lines, so the one about the IFD that stopped the job could be one of the "… and 5 more"; a page planned to be copied that passed 95 % kept the plan's words ("IFD 0 copied as it is: its compression is 7"), and one that couldn't be decoded read "IFD 0 took the output past 95 % of the source copied as it is, as it was to be: its LZW is broken: …". The line that ended the job comes first and is always kept now, in place of the plan's line for that IFD, and says plainly what happened (unit tests on the words, with 20 copied pages stopped at the first and the last, and through `run_job`). Round 6 (2026-10-30 review): an uncompressed IFD whose sizing tag libtiff can't read (a PhotometricInterpretation typed RATIONAL, which libtiff 4.5.1's `tiff2rgba` and Pillow 12.3 read with "tag ignored", or two SHORTs; a PlanarConfiguration of 3; a RowsPerStrip of 2^32 − 2) was refused as wrong-size however right its counts, and D28 said Compress copied it; it's refused as a layout Grape can't read now ("… says how its image data is laid out in a way Grape can't read yet."; unit tests, and through `run_job`), and only a compressed IFD with such a tag is copied as it is. Both tools took the first of two entries for a tag, as libtiff does, where Pillow takes the last: a gray JPEG strip holding an Exif APP1 with a GPS IFD under Compression 5 then 7 was read as LZW, never scanned, and published by Remove Metadata with the GPS in it; entries for a tag either tool decides on that disagree are refused now (unit tests on Compression, RowsPerStrip, PhotometricInterpretation, Predictor, SampleFormat and the counts, and the file through `run_job`). Strip and tile offsets and counts were read as SHORT, LONG, LONG8, IFD or IFD8 only, where libtiff 4.5.1 reads any integer type but IFD and IFD8 (`TIFFReadDirEntryLong8ArrayWithLimit`): a file with them typed SSHORT, SLONG, SBYTE or SLONG8, which libtiff reads with a warning, was refused as damaged, and one with them typed IFD or IFD8, which libtiff can't read ("Incompatible type for \"StripOffsets\"", `tiffinfo`), was published by both tools; they're read as libtiff reads them now (unit tests on each type and a negative count, and through `run_job`).
