JPEGTables were scanned as their raw bytes, and only a JPEG IFD's: the review's 16 × 16 gray JPEG strip whose tables held SOI, an Exif APP1 with a Make and a GPS IFD, DQT and DHT, and EOI, each byte a SHORT, which `tiffinfo` reads as "JPEG Tables: (401 bytes)" and libtiff's TIFFGetField hands on as the stream, was published with the GPS in it. They're read as libtiff reads them (`TIFFReadDirEntryByteArray`), in any IFD, and refused as unchecked where libtiff can't read them as bytes (unit tests on every type, out-of-range and negative values, and an LZW page's tables; the review's file in each type through `run_job`). Metadata inside other codecs' data went unread: a 64 × 48 RGB strip of WebP (50001), a Pillow 12.3 lossless WebP saved with `exif=` (a Make, GPS) and `xmp=`, which libtiff 4.5.1 decodes (`tiff2rgba`), was published with both chunks; WebP strips and tiles have their RIFF chunks walked now, by their headers, and one with an EXIF or XMP chunk is refused (WebP with neither is copied as it is), and JPEG 2000 and JPEG XL data, whose boxes may hold Exif and XMP, is refused as data Grape can't check yet, as no check of it is both cheap and exact (unit tests on the chunks, the bound and the codecs; a WebP strip with and without its chunks, and the codecs, through `run_job`; the review's file refused). At the final 95 % check, the detail kept the plan's 16 lines first, so the line about the page that was taken back ("its blocks came to N bytes recoded, where a copy writes M") was one of the "… and 5 more" (twenty 64 × 48 gray JPEG pages, then a page of uncompressed noise; and that noise first, then twenty 16 × 16 JPEG pages); the lines of IFDs taken back come right after the line that ended the job, if one did, at either stop, every one (unit tests on the log, and both files through `run_job`). An RGB 40 × 24 page in one uncompressed strip with a RowsPerStrip of 2^31 or 0xFF000018 (any from 2^31 to 2^32 − 3) was recoded, and Pillow 12.3, which reads the source, failed on the output ("decoder error -9"; libtiff 4.5.1 and ImageMagick 6.9.12 read both); such strips are copied as they are now. The review's rule, any RowsPerStrip past the picture's rows but 2^32 − 1, would copy every page the tiff crate writes under 1 MB (it writes 1,000,000 bytes' worth of rows a strip), which Pillow, libtiff and ImageMagick read compressed from 25 rows of 24 to 2^31 − 1 alike, so only the range Pillow fails on is. A PhotometricInterpretation of 255 or 65535 failed in the review's ImageMagick on the output; ImageMagick 6.9.12-98 here read Grape's output of 10 such pages (1, 3 and 4 samples, 8 and 16 bits, either byte order, one strip or several) as it read the source, but an IFD with a PhotometricInterpretation libtiff doesn't know is copied as it is anyway, as what readers make of it is guesswork (unit tests on both rules, and the RowsPerStrip page through `run_job`). Round 7 (2026-10-30 review): a strip that a WebP page and a JPEG page both listed was read once, as the first page's kind, and passed the other's check: two RGB 64 × 48 pages, the first WebP and the second JPEG, whose strip was a JPEG with an Exif APP1 (a Make and a GPS IFD), and the other way round with a lossless WebP holding EXIF and XMP chunks, were published with them; a block IFDs share is read once as each kind of data they list it as now (a unit test, and both files through `run_job`). LercParameters (50674) went, as other fields, so a LERC file with Deflate or ZSTD inside (`tiffcp -c lerc:s1 -r 16`, `lerc:s2`; GDAL's LERC_DEFLATE and LERC_ZSTD) was published and then couldn't be decoded ("LERCPreDecode: lerc_getBlobInfo() failed" in `tiff2rgba`, `tiffcp` and ImageMagick); it's kept now, and the three `tiffcp` files (none, Deflate and ZSTD inside) come out decoding as their sources do in libtiff 4.5.1 and ImageMagick 6.9.12, with nothing reported (a unit test, and both through `run_job`). A sweep of `tif_dirinfo.c` and each codec's fields in libtiff 4.5.1, and of what tifffile 2026.10.15 reads to decode, found no other tag a decoder needs that went: the fax codecs' BadFaxLines, CleanFaxData and ConsecutiveBadFaxLines (326 to 328) and its FaxRecvParams, FaxSubAddress, FaxRecvTime and FaxDcs (34908 to 34911) only describe a fax, SGILog's StoNits (37439) only scales what it decodes, and the other codecs' fields (JPEG's quality and color mode, ZIP, LZMA, ZSTD and WebP levels, PixarLog's and SGILog's data formats) are never in a file. Compression codes other than 7, 50001 and the JPEG 2000 and JPEG XL ones went through unread: a 16 × 16 gray JPEG strip with an Exif APP1 (a Make and a GPS IFD) under 34892 or 33007 was published with it, as was data under 99, 34927, 34933 (PNG, which may hold `eXIf` and `tEXt`), 52546 (JPEG XL as DNG 1.7 numbers it, which D28 said was refused), and 34934 and 22610 (JPEG XR). Remove Metadata copies only codecs whose streams hold nothing a reader reads but the picture now, reads the JPEG and WebP ones, and refuses every other code (unit tests on each code, and the review's file and the refused kinds through `run_job`); `tiffcp`'s None, LZW, Deflate, PackBits, Group 3 (1-D and 2-D), Group 4, JBIG, JPEG, LZMA, ZSTD, WebP and LERC files are stripped and decode as their sources do in ImageMagick 6.9.12. A WebP strip whose RIFF size covered only its VP8L, its EXIF and XMP chunks after it in the strip, and one of ALPH, EXIF and VP8L chunks with no RIFF header, which libtiff decodes as libwebp walks such chunks, were published with their Exif: a WebP strip must be a RIFF container that covers it (one pad byte after it allowed) or a bare VP8 or VP8L bitstream now, and anything else is refused as WebP data Grape can't check (unit tests, and both through `run_job`; `tiffcp -c webp`'s file is stripped as before). Each WebP chunk's header was read alone, a seek and a read of the file each, 1.32 million of each for 20,000 strips of 64 chunks; a block's first 64 KiB are read once now, and its headers walked in them, 4 KiB read from one past them: that file is stripped with 40,038 reads and 40,025 seeks in all, its copy and self-check among them, in 0.19 s (debug build), and a unit test holds 2,000 such strips under 4 reads and seeks a strip (2.0 measured, 194 before). Not yet on the PC or a Mac.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "Image tools, slice 2, M4 (D28)", which grows with the pairs.
- **Open for Maxwell** (each has the default above):
  - HEIC Compress drops gain maps, depth, mattes and thumbnails, so HDR photos come out SDR. Or refuse such files, or carry the gain map later?
  - A 48 MP HEIC at High is refused with "Try Balanced". Or scale it quietly to one HEVC picture until grid encoding lands?
  - HEIC Remove Metadata keeps `thmb` thumbnails as image data. Or drop and report them, since a stale one can show an uncropped original?
  - TIFF Remove Metadata drops Photoshop layers (37724) and GeoTIFF tags and refuses ImageJ/OME files. Keep any of them instead?
  - TIFF Compress is lossless at every preset (Deflate plus predictor), and lossless WebP stays lossless. Lossy at Small instead?
  - The WebP and HEIC qualities are provisional until QA.
  - Should JPG and HEIC → WebP conversions turn lossy now that libwebp is in? Default: a separate decision later.
- **Revisit if:**
  - libwebp ships a security release (bump under D9's override);
  - Windows Photos or WIC misreads Deflate plus predictor, or 16-bit predictor (fall back to LZW, or to 8-bit only);
  - one-row strips miss the 95 % rule in QA (re-strip);
  - lane A lands grid encoding (lift High's limit) or 10-bit output;
  - QA shows Apple's HDR rendering, or Portrait editing (the depth map's own XMP goes), is lost after Remove Metadata;
  - users ask for lossy TIFF, animated WebP or lossy WebP conversions;
  - large TIFF scans are too slow on the PC at level 6 or 3, or their size matters more than the time: deflate a file's blocks on several threads at once.

## D39: SVG input (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** SVG → JPG, PNG, WEBP, TIFF and BMP, HEIC and AVIF (with the image helper, D33 and D41) and PDF (all 8 of FORMAT_MATRIX's `svg` pairs), and SVG pages in Create PDF, are drawn by **resvg 0.48.1** in **Grape's SVG worker**, a process of its own: `Grape --grape-svg-worker`, Grape's own executable, dispatched in `main.rs` before anything else starts, on every OS, as the PDF worker is (D29). `.svgz` is the same format (grape-core's alias, lane B's), and gzip content is read whatever the name. The worker returns pixels; the parent publishes them through the raster engine (`image::publish_pixels`), the image helper's encode (`heif::encode_pixels`, D41) or D37's page writer. The code is `grape-engines/src/svg/` (`protocol.rs`, `resolve.rs`, `size.rs`, `worker.rs`, `run.rs`), lane A's module in lane B's crate by agreement (2026-10-28), as D27, D29, D33 and D37 were. Lane B also agreed to `lib.rs`'s new items (`SidecarPaths::svg_worker`, `Engine::Svg`, the `svg::supports` arm before the HEIF arm, the `run_job` arm, `Note::ImagesLeftOut` and `Note::DrawnSmaller`, and the doc comments) and to the two lines in `Cargo.toml`. `image.rs`, `headers.rs`, grape-core and `media/` don't change: `image::{decode, publish_pixels, pixels_fit, prepare, RASTER}` are called as they are. The spec is `svg-spec.md` (2026-10-28). It replaces ENGINES.md's "`resvg` + `usvg`: rasterize" row and FORMAT_MATRIX's "2× for very small viewBoxes".
- **Why a worker, always** (measured on Linux with resvg/usvg 0.48.1; nothing ran on Windows or a Mac):
  - **No cancel, progress or budget hook.** `resvg::render` returns `()` and usvg's parse is one call. resvg's maintainer calls safe rendering of untrusted SVG "impossible by design" and recommends a separate process (resvg #815, #641).
  - **Unbounded time.** A 1.4 KB `<use>` file was still drawing after 300 s; feMorphology, feTurbulence and feConvolveMatrix files each ran past 60 s; a 230-byte blur at 2000² took 55 s and 790 MiB.
  - **Unbounded memory.** A 558 KB data-URI `<use>` file passed 4 GiB in 19 s; a 160 KB entity file made roxmltree hold 3.8 GiB.
  - **Process death.** `Pixmap::new` aborts on a failed allocation, and a stack overflow can't be caught: a 30,000-link mask chain (2.7 MB) overflows even a 64 MiB stack. resvg has open panic issues (#1190, #1185, #1168, #1044, #937, #935, #933).
  - Any of these in Grape's own process would take the tray and the drag hooks with it (golden rule 4). So, as for D29 and D33, a crash or a hang ends only the worker, and a cancel is a kill. It's not a sandbox: the worker has the user's rights.
  - **Not the PDF worker:** its request needs PDFium's path (SVG would fail without PDFium), its limits and its timeout are page-shaped, it has no containment hook, and it writes only JPG and PNG.
- **How it runs:**
  - On `helper.rs`'s `Launch`, `start`, `Running` and `WorkDir`, like the image helper: `DETACHED_PROCESS` on Windows; stdin and stdout piped, stderr discarded; its work folder as its current folder; the app's containment hook (`set_contain`: on Windows a Job Object of its own, 2.5 GiB committed, killed on close, one process). The environment is inherited (fontdb reads `%USERPROFILE%`).
  - **Protocol** (`svg/protocol.rs`, the SVG worker's own, `PROTOCOL = 1`): one JSON request on stdin, `{protocol, source, workDir, mode, limits}`, where `mode` is `raster` or `page` and `limits` carries every bound below; JSON lines on stdout: `image {width, height, cssWidth, cssHeight, scaled}` once, after the size is known and before the canvas is allocated, `progress` (0.2 after the parse, 0.3 after fonts, 0.9 after the drawing), then exactly one `done {width, height, channels, leftOut, scaled}` (exit 0) or `error {kind, detail}` (exit 1; kinds `read`, `notSvg`, `tooLarge`, `write`, `protocol`, `internal`). Exit 2 is bad usage and 86 the heap bound. Both sides refuse unknown fields. No line holds a path, a name or file contents; XML errors give their position only.
  - **Pixels** go through `pixels.rgba` in a private `grape-svg-{pid}-{nanos}-{n}` folder (0700 on Unix) under the OS temp folder, written `create_new` with grape-heif-proto's `PixelWriter`: straight alpha, 3 channels when every pixel is opaque, else 4, no ICC (resvg draws sRGB; the output is untagged). The file format is unchanged, so grape-heif-proto's protocol isn't bumped. The parent reads it once through `helper::read_pixel_file` (heif.rs's reader, moved), held to the size the `image` line announced. `remove_stale_work_dirs` sweeps `grape-svg-` folders too.
  - The worker arms the **existing `WorkerHeap` bound** (`pdf::heap`) at the request's 2 GiB itself, so `main.rs` needs no arm for it. For SVG the bound sees nearly everything, because resvg, usvg, tiny-skia and roxmltree are pure Rust; past it the worker exits 86. It's the only memory cap on macOS, where there's no Job Object.
  - The job runs on a thread with a **64 MiB** stack inside `catch_unwind`; every panic is silent. The worker never installs a logger, so usvg's and resvg's warnings go nowhere. On macOS (and Linux) it exits when Grape goes away: it watches its parent, since there's no Job Object to end it.
  - Before anything starts, the parent sniffs the file (FORMAT_MATRIX rule 3): a `.svg` whose content is PNG, JPEG, WebP, TIFF or BMP goes to the raster engine, as HEIC's `NotHeif` does. Gzip and text go to the worker.
  - **Cost:** a spawn and a small drawing took 2.1 ms (median) on Linux, 6.0 ms with system fonts. A Windows `CreateProcess` of `Grape.exe` with Defender will cost more, and a 200-icon drop is 200 spawns, one after another on the `grape-jobs` thread: measure both on the PC.
- **What the worker does:**
  1. Reads the source read-only, at most 64 MiB (a larger file is refused unread, from its length).
  2. Gunzips when the content starts `1F 8B`, at most 64 MiB inflated (64 MiB + 1 byte is "too large"; a bad stream "isn't a readable SVG image").
  3. Takes UTF-8 only, a BOM allowed. UTF-16 is refused as not an SVG.
  4. Parses with roxmltree: DTDs allowed (Illustrator and Inkscape files carry `<!DOCTYPE svg PUBLIC …>`, and `allow_dtd: false` refuses them all), **no entity resolver** (`SYSTEM` entities and external DTDs are never fetched), at most 2,000,000 nodes, which embedded SVGs share. Entity amplification is left to the heap bound (about 380 MB/s of growth trips 2 GiB in about 5 s).
  5. Loads the system's fonts only when the document has a `text` element in the SVG namespace or in none, or a `data:image/svg+xml` reference (a nested SVG may hold text); otherwise an empty font database.
  6. Builds usvg's tree from the parsed document (`Tree::from_xmltree`) with `resources_dir: None`, 96 DPI, a 100 × 100 default size, `languages: ["en"]`, no style sheet and **Grape's data-only resolver**. usvg's own limits (1,000,000 elements after `<use>`, depth 1,024) are "too large".
  7. Sizes the canvas (the size rule below), sends the `image` line, draws on a transparent canvas, and writes the pixels.
- **External references: `data:` only.** usvg's default resolver takes every `<image>` and `<feImage>` href that isn't `data:` as a file path, absolute or relative to the process's folder, and reads it whole: measured, it baked a private PNG into the output and read `/dev/zero` until out of memory, and on Windows a UNC href (`\\host\share\x.png`, `//host/share`, `\\host@SSL\…`) would make `Path::exists` talk SMB or WebDAV, a network call in the conversion path (golden rule 3) that can leak an NTLM hash. Grape's resolver never reads a file: every href that isn't `data:` is refused and counted. Embedded data is admitted within a **job-wide budget** shared by every nested SVG: PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP whose header size is known (`imagesize`), each at most 16,384 px a side and 67,108,864 px, at most 128 MiB decoded in all; nested `image/svg+xml` up to 64 per job, never gzip. Each nested SVG is parsed as the file is (UTF-8, no entity resolver, within what the file's XML and the earlier nested SVGs left of the 2,000,000 nodes) and drawn with Grape's resolver, so its own links are refused and counted and its data shares the budget; usvg's `from_data_nested` isn't used, because it parses with no node limit and drops a nested SVG's links uncounted. A nested SVG over a limit is left out and counted; a damaged one draws nothing and isn't counted, as with an embedded raster damaged past its header. usvg doesn't cache, so every `<use>` copy calls the resolver again and is counted again, which bounds the 558 KB → 4 GiB case. Refusals are counted once each (an href by a hash of its text; data by its length and a hash of its first 4 KiB), and the count is the toast's "images left out". usvg 0.48.1 already ignores `file://` and `http(s)://` URLs, `<use href="other.svg#a">`, CSS `@import` and `@font-face`, XInclude, external entities, `<script>`, `<foreignObject>` and `<a>` (strace showed no open or socket). This matches what browsers do with an SVG used as an image ("secure static mode").
- **Fonts:** text is drawn with the **system's fonts**; no font is bundled, and no web font is ever read. fontdb scans fixed local folders: `%SYSTEMROOT%\Fonts` and the user's `AppData\Local` and `AppData\Roaming` `Microsoft\Windows\Fonts` on Windows; `/Library/Fonts`, `/System/Library/Fonts`, `/System/Library/AssetsV2/com_apple_MobileAsset_Font*`, `/Network/Library/Fonts` and `~/Library/Fonts` on macOS. They're mapped (`memmap-fonts`), not read whole: a font that changes or is cut short while mapped can crash the worker (SIGBUS on macOS), which ends one job. Generic families are fontdb's: Times New Roman, Arial, Comic Sans MS, Impact (Papyrus on macOS) and Courier New. Measured here: 110 faces in 4–13 ms; Windows isn't measured.
- **The size rule:** usvg gives the SVG's size in CSS pixels at 96 per inch, the viewBox applied (`viewBox="0 0 24 24"` alone is 24 × 24; `width="200"` with `viewBox="0 0 100 50"` is 200 × 100; 210 mm is 793.7; no size and no viewBox is the content's right and bottom edges, or 100 × 100 when empty).
  - **Images** (JPG, PNG, WebP, TIFF, BMP, HEIC, AVIF): twice that when the longer side is under 256 px (the size, not the viewBox: `width="1000" viewBox="0 0 24 24"` is 1000 px), rounded up, at least 1 px; then fitted at the largest uniform scale within the target's caps, floored, and "Drawn smaller to fit" (`Note::DrawnSmaller`) when it was. The caps: 16,384 a side and 67,108,864 px for JPG, PNG, TIFF and BMP; 16,383 a side for WebP (its format's limit); 16,384 a side and 35,651,584 px for HEIC and AVIF (one picture of either, D33, D41).
  - **A PDF page** (SVG → PDF and an SVG in Create PDF) is the SVG's own size, `css × 0.75` pt (96 CSS px = 72 pt = 1 in), so a 210 mm SVG makes an A4-wide page; D37's 14,400 pt cap scales it down evenly and each side is at least 1 pt. Its pixels are drawn at **300 DPI** (`css × 300 / 96`), fitted into 16,384 a side and 64 MP; a page drawn smaller counts in `Note::PagesScaled`, "drawn below 300 DPI". Physical size, unlike D37's 1 px = 1 pt for raster images, because an SVG states its size in real units and a raster image's DPI is often wrong; 300 DPI because 96 prints soft.
- **Outputs:**
  - JPG, PNG, WebP, TIFF, BMP: `image::publish_pixels` with D16's rules (JPEG on white at quality 90, PNG and TIFF with alpha, lossless WebP, BMP with alpha when there is some). Names say SVG.
  - HEIC, AVIF: `heif::encode_pixels` (D41), RGBA8 through `image::prepare(…, Format::Heic, …)`, no ICC, the helper's default quality.
  - PDF and Create PDF (`pdf/create.rs`): `Picture::Pixels` gains `page: Option<(f64, f64)>` in points; an SVG page is stored **lossless** (Flate with PNG predictors; 1-bit for pure black and white), because vector art's flat colors and hard edges smear in JPEG, with alpha as a soft mask. `reads` and `combines` take SVG with `svg_worker`; Create PDF's rule stands (an SVG that can't be read stops the whole PDF and the toast names it), and an SVG's progress falls in the first half of its share.
  - An SVG that draws nothing (empty, or every reference refused) still writes its image, transparent or white, as a browser shows it.
  - **Why raster PDF pages in v1:** svg2pdf 0.13.0 is unmaintained and sits on usvg 0.45, ttf-parser and fontdb 0.23 (58 more packages, 15 duplicates, and `gif` in Grape's own `image`); krilla-svg 0.8.1 needs usvg/resvg 0.47 and forces `svgz` and `memmap-fonts` on. Either means a second SVG stack or a downgrade, and both embed subsets of system fonts in files users share (font licensing, fsType bits).
- **Limits:**

| Limit | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Source file | ≤ 64 MiB, refused unread | roxmltree holds the whole text |
| Gzip content | ≤ 64 MiB inflated (64 MiB + 1 read to tell) | a 3 MB svgz asked usvg for 3 GiB |
| XML | UTF-8; DTDs on; no entity resolver; ≤ 2,000,000 nodes in all, the file's and its nested SVGs' (about 330 MiB) | above |
| usvg's own | 1,000,000 elements after `<use>`; depth 1,024 | not settable |
| External hrefs | none; each counted as left out | above |
| `data:` per job | ≤ 64 nested SVGs; ≤ 128 MiB decoded in all; each raster ≤ 16,384 a side and ≤ 67,108,864 px | above |
| Canvas | the size rule's caps, per target | above |
| Worker heap | 2 GiB (`WorkerHeap`, exit 86) | a legitimate 64 MP canvas with a full blur peaked at 1.3 GiB (49 s) |
| Windows Job Object | 2.5 GiB committed, killed on close | covers stacks and anything outside the heap |
| Job thread stack | 64 MiB | depth-1,024 trees need over 1 MiB |
| Timeout | 60 s, extended by 1 s per started canvas megapixel (1,048,576 px, as in the 64 MP cap) once the `image` line arrives (124 s at most) | the legitimate 64 MP blur took 49 s here |
| Cancel | the worker is killed within the 100 ms poll | heif.rs's |

  - The parent also checks the `image` line: its size within the caps it sent, and `image::pixels_fit(W, H, Rgba8, fit, 0)` for the output (`to`, or HEIC for HEIC and AVIF, or `pdf::create`'s PNG for a page). A refusal kills the worker and says "… is too large to convert.". A worker that ends without a result after committing 90% of the Job Object's cap "needs too much memory".
- **Messages** (details never hold a path): "logo.svg isn't a readable SVG image." (`notSvg`); "… is too large to convert." (`tooLarge`, or the parent's check); "Grape couldn't read logo.svg." (`read`); "Grape couldn't write its temp files." (`write`); "… took too long, so Grape stopped." (the timeout); "… needs too much memory, so Grape stopped." (exit 86, or a crash at 90% of the Job Object's cap); "Grape's SVG helper stopped unexpectedly." (a crash, `internal`, `protocol`, lines that disagree, a spoiled pixel file); "Grape's SVG helper couldn't start." (it can't start or be contained: `NotYetSupported`, as D29's); "Cancelled. Nothing was saved.".
- **Job notes** (`lib.rs`; `Note` is `#[non_exhaustive]`): `Note::ImagesLeftOut(n)`, the references an SVG left out (in a PDF, summed over its SVGs), and `Note::DrawnSmaller`, an SVG drawn smaller to fit an image target. `Note::PagesScaled(n)` now also counts SVG pages drawn below 300 DPI. The app words them in the done toast's title (D35), in this order after an archive's left-out: "1 image left out" / "3 images left out", then "2 pages drawn below 300 DPI", then "Drawn smaller to fit", then Remove Metadata's and Compress's. The widest, "4294967295 images left out", is 178.5 px of the title's 246 in Inter SemiBold 13 px, and "Drawn smaller to fit" 122.1 px. The log line counts them: `; 3 image(s) left out; drawn smaller`.
- **The app:** `main.rs` runs `grape_engines::svg::worker_main()` for `Grape --grape-svg-worker` (`svg::WORKER_FLAG`, exactly, as the first argument) before Tauri, a window, the tray, the single-instance check or a logger starts, on every OS. `sidecars.rs` gives every job `current_exe()` as `SidecarPaths::svg_worker` on every OS, like the PDF worker, and doesn't log it. `jobs.rs` has no HELPERS row for it (it's Grape itself); SVG → HEIC or AVIF on Windows without `grape-heif.exe` gets the image helper's row ("Grape's image helper is missing" / "Reinstall Grape to convert HEIC and AVIF files."), on macOS it runs, and elsewhere it's "coming". `contain.rs` doesn't change: the hook is process-wide.
- **Counts** (from grape-core's matrix and `supports_with`): with every sidecar but the image helper, SVG adds 6 pairs to the 146, so 152 v1 pairs run; the image helper adds 26 more (D41's 24, and SVG → HEIC and AVIF), so **all 178 v1 pairs** run when every helper is there. M3's conversions are complete. Create PDF takes SVG (M4).
- **New dependencies** (grape-engines only; each lock entry checked against D9 on crates.io on 2026-10-28):
  - **resvg =0.48.1** (2026-08-02, Apache-2.0 OR MIT), `default-features = false`, features `text`, `system-fonts`, `memmap-fonts` and `raster-images`, never `svgz` (usvg's gunzip has no bound) or the defaults: Grape's SVG renderer, with usvg 0.48.1 (the parser, text through skrifa and harfrust), tiny-skia 0.12.0 (the rasterizer, BSD-3-Clause) and fontdb 0.24.0 (system fonts). Its re-exports (`resvg::usvg`, `resvg::tiny_skia`, `usvg::roxmltree`, `usvg::fontdb`) are used, so none of them is a direct dependency. resvg brings no second `png` major: tiny-skia asks for png 0.18, the 0.18.1 lane B already uses. usvg 0.48 no longer uses ttf-parser (RUSTSEC-2026-0192) or rustybuzz (RUSTSEC-2026-0206). OSV lists no advisory for resvg, usvg, roxmltree, tiny-skia, fontdb, svgtypes, harfrust, skrifa, read-fonts, imagesize, simplecss, data-url or kurbo.
  - **imagesize =0.15.0** (2026-07-09, MIT): embedded images' header size, inside the data resolver (already locked through usvg).
  - **Pins for D9** (cutoff 2026-10-14): `font-types` **0.12.6** (2026-10-03; 0.12.7 is from 2026-10-15T14:22Z and passes after 2026-10-29T14:22Z) and `memmap2` **0.9.11** (2026-06-22; 0.9.12 is from 2026-10-21).
  - **roxmltree 0.21.2** (2026-10-24) is taken **under D9's security override**: it fixes a stack overflow on deeply nested elements (0.21.1 aborts on 10,000 nested `<g>` with a 1 MiB stack and on 1,000,000 with 64 MiB), the diff is about 43 lines, and the parser is fed untrusted input. A fresh resolve picks it anyway. 0.21.0 is yanked.
  - The 31 new lock entries (all MIT OR Apache-2.0 unless named): arrayref 0.3.9 (2024-09-14, **BSD-2-Clause**), bytemuck_derive 1.12.1 (2026-09-11, Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT), color_quant 1.1.0 (2020-10-09, MIT), data-url 0.3.2 (2025-08-21), euclid 0.22.14 (2026-03-18; in the lock only, never built), float-cmp 0.9.0 (2021-07-21, MIT), font-types 0.12.6, fontconfig-parser 0.5.8 (2025-05-12, MIT; Linux only), fontdb 0.24.0 (2026-07-29, MIT), gif 0.14.2 (2026-04-09), harfrust 0.12.0 (2026-07-03, MIT), imagesize 0.15.0, kurbo 0.13.1 (2026-05-13), memmap2 0.9.11, pico-args 0.5.0 (2022-06-04, MIT), polycool 0.4.0 (2025-10-23), read-fonts 0.41.0 (2026-07-03), resvg 0.48.1, roxmltree 0.20.0 (2024-05-23; Linux only, through fontconfig-parser) and 0.21.2 (the override), simplecss 0.2.2 (2025-01-06), skrifa 0.44.0 (2026-07-04), slotmap 1.1.1 (2025-12-06, Zlib), strict-num 0.1.1 (2023-05-27, MIT), svgtypes 0.16.1 (2026-01-09), tiny-skia and tiny-skia-path 0.12.0 (2026-02-02, BSD-3-Clause), unicode-script 0.5.8 (2025-12-03), unicode-vo 0.1.0 (2019-06-11), usvg 0.48.1 (2026-08-02) and xmlwriter 0.1.0 (2019-08-12, MIT). arrayvec 0.7.8 and rgb 0.8.53 came in with D41. Nothing already locked changed version; adding the crates made the resolver move `cssparser-macros`' syn from 3.0.6 to 2.0.119, which was set back by hand (`--locked` accepts it). `cargo deny`'s duplicate list is the same before and after.
  - **Licenses:** arrayref 0.3.9 (BSD-2-Clause, through tiny-skia, on every OS) is one more BSD-2-Clause crate in Grape's own binary, which D41's D23 amendment already allows. No GPL, LGPL or MPL anywhere in the graph. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` goes from 348 to 376 crate versions, with no new clarification (arrayref ships its `LICENSE`).
  - **Size:** about +4.1 MB stripped on Linux x86_64; Windows and macOS sizes come from CI's artifacts.
  - The `grape-svg-worker` test binary (`src/bin/`, no required feature) installs a third copy of `WorkerHeap` (the app's and the PDF worker bin's are the other two; change all three together).
- **Tested** on Linux, 2026-10-28 (nothing ran on Windows or a Mac; the cross-target checks were clippy only):
  - `svg`'s 53 unit tests. In `size`, `protocol`, `resolve` and `worker` (36): every row of the size rule, the 255.9/256 boundary, rounding up and the 1 px minimum (NaN and `f64::MAX` too), each target's caps, floor-after-fit never passing a cap, page mode (A4 is 2481 × 3508); the exact JSON both ways and unknown fields refused at every level; the resolver (the four raster types admitted, the side, pixel, byte and nested-SVG budgets, gzip refused, absolute, relative, `\\host\share`, `\\host@SSL`, `//host/share`, `file:///` and `http(s)://` hrefs refused, an `<feImage>` href through the same resolver, 1,000 uses of one href counted once, a nested SVG's links refused and counted two levels deep, the node limit shared with the file's XML, a damaged nested SVG not counted); the worker through `serve` (gunzip and its bound, UTF-8 with a BOM, UTF-16 refused, the SVG 1.1 DOCTYPE, an external entity never read, 2,000,001 nodes, text detection, usvg's own limits, a percent-encoded nested SVG's link counted in `done`, a nested SVG past the node limit left out).
  - In `svg/tests.rs` (17, the parent, with the library's test binary as the worker): every raster target, HEIC and AVIF through heif.rs's fake helper, SVG → PDF (a 72 × 36 pt page, Flate, 300 × 150 px; a 105 mm SVG gives an A6-wide page), Create PDF on [svg, jpg] (2 pages, the JPEG passed through), `PagesScaled`, the rule-3 sniff with nothing started, every failure kind and misbehavior in Grape's words, cancel within 2 s, the deadline and its 124 s at 64 MP, the container's 2.5 GiB and a refused container, a crash at a 2,400 MiB peak as "needs too much memory", and the dispatch.
  - `tests/svg.rs` (15 tests, 16 on Windows, with the real `grape-svg-worker` binary): the five raster targets (48 × 48 from a 24 × 24 viewBox, white JPEG, alpha kept), the leak test (a red PNG next to the SVG, by absolute and relative path, through `<image href>`, `xlink:href` and `<feImage>`: no red pixel, `ImagesLeftOut(1)`), a `data:` PNG drawn, text drawn (fails, never skips, without a face), `width="1e6"` fitted with `DrawnSmaller`, gzip `.svg` and `.svgz`, a gzip bomb, damaged XML, a PNG named `.svg`, SVG → PDF and Create PDF through `run_job`, cancel at 0.5 s, a 1 s deadline, a 64 MiB heap bound, the mask chain's crash, and on Windows a worker in a Job Object. Every test hashes its source and checks that nothing is left behind.
  - `pdf-it` (`tests/create_pdf_render.rs`): an SVG page drawn by PDFium at 300 DPI matches the SVG drawn to PNG at that size within a mean error of 1/255, and its MediaBox is `css × 0.75`.
  - Lane B's `tests/dispatch.rs` moved by SVG's pairs (its `all_sidecars` now sets `svg_worker`; the counts are taken from grape-core's matrix), and a new test checks that with every helper exactly the 178 v1 pairs run.
  - The app's 298 + 8 tests: the flag only as the first argument; `svg_worker` is the executable on every system; SVG to the rasters, PDF and Create PDF runnable; SVG → HEIC and AVIF without the helper on each OS's rules; each note's title, singular and plural, and their order. The app's own debug build, run as the worker, drew five sample SVGs (a 24 px icon, a 30,000 × 2,000 one fitted, one with text, one with a `data:` PNG and a linked file left out, an A4-wide `.svgz`) to PNG and PDF through `run_job`.
- **CI runs:** `tests/svg.rs` in the Windows and macOS Rust jobs (on Windows, a worker in the app's Job Object); the SVG page render in their `pdf-it` step; SVG → HEIC and SVG → AVIF through the real helper in `heif-it-windows` and `heif-it-macos` (on GitHub's macOS VMs SVG → HEIC must say "This Mac can't write HEIC files."). None of it has run on GitHub yet.
- **Needs the PC and a Mac:** `TESTING.md` → "SVG, M3/M4".
- **Not handled yet:** linked images (a same-folder rule could be added later; starting closed is the safe direction); UTF-16 SVGs; `systemLanguage` from the OS's language (usvg takes `en`); vector PDF output; Create Collage (v2); animation and scripting (never).
- **Revisit if:** resvg/usvg **0.49.0** (2026-10-24, the filter-region panic fix #1144) passes D9 on 2026-11-07 (re-pin as needed); krilla-svg tracks usvg 0.48 or later (vector PDF pages); Windows font scans are slow (cache the database, or scan once per drop); spawns dominate many-file drops (several files per worker); users want linked images drawn.
- **Open question for Maxwell:** BSD-2-Clause in Grape's own binary is D41's question too (arrayref joins rav1e, v_frame and av1-grain, and it's on every OS); with no, SVG input has no engine.

- *2026-10-28 (lane A):* "it has no containment hook" under "Not the PDF worker" no longer holds: the PDF worker is contained now (D29's amendment). The other reasons stand.

## D45: Releases carry the Mac DMG (2026-10-28, proposed, confirm)
- **Decision:** a version tag builds the universal Mac DMG with the Windows installer, from the same tag, and the draft release carries both: `Grape-macOS-universal.dmg` next to `Grape-Setup-x64.exe`, with its line in `SHA256SUMS.txt`. The draft also gets Grape's own FFmpeg builds as two zips. This is ROADMAP M9's "Release workflow builds Windows + macOS together".
- **Why:** D18 runs the Mac port alongside Windows, and CI already builds the DMG on every run. One tag that builds both keeps the two in step: one commit, one set of sidecar builds, one FFmpeg source bundle.
- **One build for CI and the release:** `.github/workflows/macos-app.yml`, a reusable workflow like `ffmpeg.yml`, holds the Mac app build that was `ci.yml`'s `build-macos` job. Both `ci.yml`'s and `release.yml`'s `build-macos` call it, so both build the same DMG with the same checks: the sidecars, PDFium and license folders in Grape.app, no hardened runtime, the image helper worker's probe and the macOS 13 `dlsym` check, and an AVIF at the encode cap.
  - Its inputs: `ref` (the release passes its tag), `cache` (`false` in the release, which fetches PDFium fresh), `ffmpeg-sums` (the `ffmpeg` workflow's `macos-sums`, since a called workflow can't read `needs`) and `release`.
  - CI's call uploads the DMG as `Grape-macOS-universal`, as before; `README.md` and `PLATFORM_MACOS.md` send people there.
  - With `release: true` it copies Tauri's `Grape_<version>_universal.dmg` (there must be exactly one) to `Grape-macOS-universal.dmg`, the same name in every release, and uploads it with its line in a `SHA256SUMS` file as `Grape-macOS-universal-release` instead. The release job checks that the file is exactly the DMG's line, then appends it to `SHA256SUMS.txt` after the installer's, which stays first because the notes' PowerShell check reads the first hash.
- **Signing:** the DMG is ad-hoc signed and not notarized, as CI's (D4, D18). The notes give the first-open steps: System Settings → Privacy & Security → **Open Anyway**, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine`. Developer ID signing and notarization wait for an Apple Developer Program membership (D4).
- **Publishing:** drafts are still published by hand, and only after that OS's manual checks passed on the draft (`TESTING.md` → "Release, M7" for Windows, "Release, M9" for the Mac). While the Mac's haven't passed, Maxwell deletes `Grape-macOS-universal.dmg` from the draft, and the notes' Mac parts (`PLATFORM_WINDOWS.md` → "Releasing", step 7), before publishing. `SHA256SUMS.txt` keeps the DMG's line; the notes check each file on its own, and a check of every file then needs `sha256sum -c --ignore-missing`. Running the tag again puts the DMG back on the draft.
- **A failed Mac build means no draft**, as a failed Windows build does: the release job needs both. Re-run the failed jobs (`PLATFORM_WINDOWS.md` → "Releasing").
- **Grape's FFmpeg on the draft:** `grape-ffmpeg-windows-x64.zip` and `grape-ffmpeg-macos-universal.zip` hold the run's `ffmpeg-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `ffmpeg-apple-darwin` artifacts as they are: the programs under Tauri's names (the Mac's per-arch files too), `BUILDINFO`, `SHA256SUMS` and `licenses/ffmpeg/`. Each is checked against its `SHA256SUMS` and the hash the `ffmpeg` workflow reported, as the build jobs check them, and the Mac programs get their executable bit back before zipping. Both are in `SHA256SUMS.txt`. They're for building Grape from source; the installer and the DMG already carry them. This is M7's "Grape's own FFmpeg binaries as release assets". Lane B may later point `scripts/sidecars.lock` at a published release's copies instead of the dev builds (D24, D25); that's lane B's change, after the first published release.
- **No new dependency.** `zip` is on GitHub's Ubuntu runners.
- **Tested:** the YAML parses; the moved steps' run text matches `ci.yml`'s old `build-macos` job; `bash -n` passes on every bash block written or moved; the release job's bash steps, run locally against fake artifacts, build a `SHA256SUMS.txt` that `sha256sum -c` passes for all six files, and refuse a second DMG, a changed DMG, a wrong `macos-sums`, a changed program and a missing `licenses/ffmpeg/`; the notes render with no placeholder left. Nothing has run on GitHub: CI's next run tests the move, and the first real tag the rest (`TESTING.md` → "Release, M9").
- **Revisit if:** a Developer ID exists (sign with it, turn the hardened runtime back on, notarize and staple in `macos-app.yml` when `release` is on; D4, D29); a Mac failure holds back a Windows release often enough to matter (let the release job go on without the DMG); lane B points the lock at the zips (their names must then stay as they are).

---

## Open questions

- **O1: Confirm the open-source license.** MIT is assumed. GPL-3.0 is the alternative if you want forks to stay open. Note: the repo's `LICENSE` file is already MIT ("Copyright (c) 2026 sussyswimmer"), and `Cargo.toml` and the bundle say MIT.
- **O2: Apple Pages support.** There's no open library and `NSAttributedString` can't read `.pages`. How Tangerine does it is unknown; the website doesn't reveal it. Options: (a) skip; (b) macOS-only via scripting the Pages app if installed; (c) parse the IWA format (large effort). Default: skip until users ask.
- ~~**O3: Windows test hardware.**~~ Resolved: Maxwell has his own Windows PC (see D8).
- **O4: Optional add-on downloads** (LibreOffice for slides/office in v2). This needs a network call outside the conversion path. Decide before v2.
- ~~**O5: Show the wheel for drags from any app on Windows,** or only Explorer and the Desktop (the default)?~~ Answered by default in D14: Explorer and the Desktop only, with "Any app" as a possible setting in M6. Confirm or change.
- **O6: MPL-2.0 crates linked by Tauri.** Tauri 2.12 statically links `option-ext` 0.2.0 (MPL-2.0) through `dirs` → `dirs-sys`. ENGINES rule 1 allows only MIT/Apache/BSD/ISC/Zlib, or LGPL linked dynamically. MPL-2.0 is file-level copyleft: linking it into an MIT app is fine as long as the MPL files' source stays available, which it does (unmodified on crates.io). Proposal: amend rule 1 to allow unmodified MPL-2.0 crates, list them in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` in M7, and add a `cargo-deny` license check to CI.
  - *2026-10-25:* the notices and the check are done (D23). `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists the MPL-2.0 crates with links to their source on crates.io, and says `option-ext` is the only one compiled in. The other four (`cssparser`, `cssparser-macros`, `dtoa-short`, `selectors`) run only inside Tauri's macros at build time. `deny.toml` allows MPL-2.0 for those five by name and nowhere else.
  - Still Maxwell's call: amend ENGINES rule 1 to allow unmodified MPL-2.0 crates. Until then, a new MPL crate fails CI and needs a name added on purpose. Rule 1 also doesn't name Unicode-3.0, which `deny.toml` allows: a permissive license, used by 19 crates (the ICU crates under `url`, and `unicode-ident`).
- **O7: Wheel titles.** `TOOLS_AND_ACTIONS.md` lists the wheel titles (METADATA, ADD BG, BLEEP, …) as **[observed]** from the demo, while golden rule 1 bans Tangerine's wording. `grape-core` encodes them exactly as the doc lists them, in one `match` checked by the doc-sync test, so rewording them later is a one-line change on each side. Keep them, or write our own?
